# echOS Decision Log

Use this file as durable subsystem memory for architecture decisions that should survive across tasks.

## 2026-04-23 | usb | xhci-dma-address-and-slot-publication-truthfulness

- Decision: `src/drivers/usb/mod.rs` xHCI command ring, EP0 transfer ring, input context, control-transfer buffers, and descriptor fetch buffers no longer publish virtual addresses as if they were DMA-usable physical addresses. These paths now resolve through `crate::memory::try_virt_to_phys_u64` and fail closed with `UsbError::AddressTranslationFailed`.
- Decision: `DeviceSlot.input_context` moved to `Box<InputContext>` so the DMA-visible input context keeps a stable allocation after physical-address publication instead of taking a physical address to a stack/local value that is then moved by value into slot state.
- Decision: xHCI slot allocation is no longer append-order derived from the current `DEVICE_SLOTS` length. Slot publication now reuses the same slot for the same controller BDF + port pair and otherwise allocates the first free slot inside that controller's `max_slots` range, failing closed with `UsbError::SlotUnavailable` when the controller-local namespace is exhausted.
- Decision: TRB and ERST layout truthfulness tightened: `Trb` is now 16-byte aligned and `ErstEntry` is now 64-byte aligned so xHCI-shared ring metadata is emitted at hardware-legal alignment rather than relying on allocator luck.
- Decision: EP0/control traffic now resolves device identity back to the owning slot. Descriptor/configuration/class-control requests enqueue setup/data/status TRBs into the stored per-slot control ring and ring the slot doorbell, rather than dropping TRBs after construction or ringing the doorbell with a USB address where xHCI expects a slot index.
- Decision: `src/drivers/usb/hid.rs` no longer stops at setup-packet construction for `SET_PROTOCOL`, `SET_IDLE`, and LED `SET_REPORT`; these HID class requests now flow through `UsbDevice::control_transfer(...)`, and HID device type is inferred from interface subclass/protocol instead of staying `Unknown`.
- Rationale: USB/input exactness could not be closed honestly while DMA-facing xHCI structures used virtual aliases and slot IDs depended on ambient global list order. Those two flaws made field behavior sensitive to allocator layout and unrelated device-enumeration history.
- Tradeoffs: This closes the most dangerous truthfulness gap in the xHCI bring-up lane, but it does not yet prove event-ring completion handling, HID interrupt-traffic correctness, or real metal resume/re-enumeration behavior. USB input exactness therefore remains partial even after this slice.
- Validation: `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib controller_slot_id -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib trb_layout_is_dma_ready -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib submit_ep0_transfer_resolves_usb_address_to_slot_id -- --nocapture`; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -q`; `.\\.qoder\\skills\\echos-architect\\scripts\\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\\drivers\\usb\\mod.rs,src\\drivers\\usb\\hid.rs,docs\\agent\\driver-capability-matrix.md,docs\\agent\\decision-log.md`.

## 2026-04-23 | usb | xhci-command-endpoint-and-hid-interrupt-lane-published

- Decision: `src/drivers/usb/mod.rs` no longer drops `Enable Slot`, `Address Device`, or `Configure Endpoint` command TRBs after construction. Those commands now publish into the controller-owned command ring and ring doorbell 0, so the software-side ownership story matches xHCI command submission rather than relying on log-only side effects.
- Decision: `configure_device(...)` now parses interface/endpoint descriptors into a durable interface graph, allocates per-endpoint transfer rings, computes xHCI device-context indices from endpoint addresses, writes endpoint contexts into the slot input context, and issues a real `Configure Endpoint` command TRB instead of stopping at descriptor parsing.
- Decision: `DeviceSlot` now owns endpoint-ring state for interrupt and bulk endpoints, including pending interrupt-IN buffers and completed report queues; transfer-event dequeue now correlates event TRB pointers back to the pending interrupt buffer and publishes the completed payload into the owning endpoint queue.
- Decision: `UsbDevice::interrupt_transfer_in(...)` and `src/drivers/usb/hid.rs::HidDriver::poll()` now share that same lane: HID boot devices consume completed interrupt-IN reports before queueing a replacement transfer, so class-control and input-report traffic no longer live in disconnected code paths.
- Decision: The host-side test corpus no longer tries to prove DMA translation on the Windows test runner, because that path faults under host-only paging assumptions. Instead, the USB tests now prove the stable invariants that remain truthful on host: descriptor parsing, xHCI DCI mapping, transfer-event-to-buffer correlation, and completed-report consumption.
- Rationale: USB/input exactness was still open after the EP0 slice because the driver could construct truthful control traffic yet still fail to publish command TRBs, endpoint contexts, or interrupt completions into a single coherent ownership model. Closing that gap was the minimum repo-visible condition for saying the keyboard/mouse lane is no longer architecturally broken.
- Tradeoffs: This closes the repo-visible blocker for USB input exactness, but not the field-proof boundary. Real metal still needs warm-reset/re-enumeration traces, completion timing under a live xHCI controller, and keyboard/mouse smoke on the chosen admission machine.
- Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -q`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib controller_slot_id -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib configuration_parser_preserves_interface_endpoints -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib endpoint_id_formula_matches_xhci_dci_layout -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib complete_interrupt_transfer_moves_buffer_to_completed_queue -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib interrupt_transfer_in_consumes_completed_report_before_queueing -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib trb_layout_is_dma_ready -- --nocapture`.

## 2026-04-23 | release-gate | single-pc-admission-profile-narrowed

- Decision: Introduced a repository-visible single-machine admission contract in `docs/agent/single-pc-admission-profile-2026-04-23.md` and `docs/agent/single-pc-admission-profile.json` with the canonical target profile `single-pc-uefi-nvme-gop-ps2-wired`.
- Decision: Added `scripts/capture_physical_profile.ps1` to capture a Windows-host inventory for firmware, NVMe, keyboard path, USB controllers, display adapters, network adapters, audio, and Bluetooth into one JSON profile.
- Decision: Added `scripts/validate_physical_profile.ps1` to fail-closed on the minimum single-PC gate: UEFI firmware, at least one NVMe controller, at least one wired NIC, and a PS/2 keyboard fallback. USB input, native DRM, audio, WiFi, and Bluetooth are now explicit advisory or out-of-scope surfaces for this admission path.
- Decision: Wired that host workflow into `echsdk field-profile capture|validate` and updated the driver capability matrix so the first five hardware blockers are read against the narrowed single-PC admission contract instead of an unconstrained hardware universe.
- Rationale: The first hardware-side blockers could not be closed honestly while "supported hardware" still meant an unconstrained PC universe. Narrowing the release/install target to one machine class is the prerequisite that turns USB/input, display, and radio from vague universal blockers into concrete per-profile gates.
- Tradeoffs: This narrows the single-PC install claim instead of pretending that USB, native DRM, audio, WiFi, or Bluetooth parity is already complete. NVMe exactness work remains open inside the narrower target, but AHCI and broad radio/device-family parity no longer block the single-PC admission path.
- Validation: PowerShell parser validation, `cargo check --bin echosdk`, and touched-path gate are required for `scripts/capture_physical_profile.ps1`, `scripts/validate_physical_profile.ps1`, `src/bin/echsdk.rs`, `docs/agent/single-pc-admission-profile-2026-04-23.md`, `docs/agent/single-pc-admission-profile.json`, `docs/agent/driver-capability-matrix.md`, and `docs/agent/decision-log.md`.

## 2026-04-23 | userland | ech-tools-session-archive-control-bridge-slice

- Decision: Expanded `ech-tools` shell-bridge coverage from 107 commands to 127 commands by routing `chvt`, `ctrlaltdel`, `dmesg`, `ed`, `flock`, `killall5`, `last`, `lastlog`, `login`, `make`, `mesg`, `mknod`, `nologin`, `pwdx`, `respawn`, `su`, `sysctl`, `tar`, `vtallow`, and `watch` through shell-owned implementations.
- Decision: Kept the remaining 23 commands adapter-pending because they need real root, mount, swap, module, block-device, network, or session-namespace ownership before the catalog can honestly expose them as runnable commands.
- Rationale: This slice closes the Linux-shaped archive/editor/build/session-control band without mutating upstream `sbase` or `ubase` sources. The bridge reuses existing echOS shell, file, kmsg, file-lock, login/session, and policy-state lanes rather than adding a parallel userland ABI.
- Tradeoffs: `tar` is uncompressed ustar-style archive handling; `ed` is batch-oriented; `watch` and `respawn` use bounded repeat counts because the current shell command API returns owned command output rather than a backpressure-aware stream; `mknod` creates FIFO nodes while character/block device nodes remain adapter-owned work. New terminal policy atomics are cache-line aligned to avoid false-sharing exposure if shell policy reads become cross-core.
- Validation: `rustfmt --edition 2021 src\\shell\\mod.rs src\\userland\\ech_tools.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib shell::tests::session_archive_control_commands_bridge_through_ech_tools -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib ech_tools -- --nocapture`; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -q`; `.\\.qoder\\skills\\echos-architect\\scripts\\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\\shell\\mod.rs,src\\userland\\ech_tools.rs,docs\\apps\\ech-tools-command-reference-2026-04-20.md,docs\\apps\\ech-tools-composition-2026-04-20.md,docs\\agent\\decision-log.md`.

## 2026-04-23 | userland | ech-tools-calculator-byte-fifo-bridge-slice

- Decision: Expanded `ech-tools` shell-bridge coverage from 93 commands to 107 commands by routing `bc`, `chgrp`, `cols`, `dc`, `dd`, `fallocate`, `hwclock`, `mkfifo`, `readahead`, `sed`, `uudecode`, `uuencode`, `xinstall`, and `yes` through shell-owned implementations.
- Decision: Kept `renice` adapter-pending because the repository's CFS implementation is not currently exposed through the crate root used by the shell build; marking it bridged would create a catalog claim without a compiling runtime path.
- Rationale: This slice closes another practical Linux-shaped command band while staying inside existing echOS-owned VFS/host-file, POSIX pipe, RTC, and shell expression surfaces. `dd`, `fallocate`, `readahead`, `xinstall`, and `uu*` reuse byte-oriented file adapters; `mkfifo` uses the POSIX FIFO manager; `hwclock` reports the RTC cache.
- Tradeoffs: `bc` is routed through the existing shell arithmetic evaluator rather than a decimal arbitrary-precision engine; `sed` implements `s/old/new/[g]`; `yes` is finite in the shell bridge (`-n` controls line count) because the current command API returns an owned string rather than a backpressure-aware stream.
- Validation: `rustfmt --edition 2021 src\\shell\\mod.rs src\\userland\\ech_tools.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib shell::tests::remaining_text_and_file_commands_bridge_through_ech_tools -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib ech_tools -- --nocapture`; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -q`.

## 2026-04-22 | userland | ech-tools-checksum-stream-topology-bridge-slice

- Decision: Expanded `ech-tools` shell-bridge coverage from 73 commands to 93 commands by routing `cksum`, `du`, `logger`, `md5sum`, `mktemp`, `od`, `pagesize`, `pathchk`, `sha1sum`, `sha224sum`, `sha256sum`, `sha384sum`, `sha512sum`, `sha512-224sum`, `sha512-256sum`, `split`, `sponge`, `sync`, `tsort`, and `xargs` through shell-owned implementations.
- Decision: Added binary/text source helpers so bridged checksum and stream commands can consume either file paths or stdin without inventing a second adapter surface, and extended host-smoke-compatible file flows for `mktemp`, `split`, and `sponge`.
- Decision: `xargs` now preserves nested shell exit ownership instead of letting stdout heuristics guess success, keeping `ech-tools xargs ...` aligned with the existing `&&` and `||` control-flow contract.
- Rationale: This slice closes the checksum and stream-processing gap that remained after Tier 0 and the first Tier 1/Tier 2 waves, while staying inside Linux-shaped command names and reusing the repository's existing shell/runtime ownership instead of adding a parallel libc-style adapter ABI.
- Tradeoffs: `logger` currently targets the serial/kernel log lane only; `split` is line-count-based rather than byte-count-based; `sync` is a no-op under host-smoke/test mode because unit tests do not own a truthful block-device flush target.
- Validation: `rustfmt --edition 2021 src\\shell\\mod.rs src\\userland\\ech_tools.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib ech_tools -- --nocapture`; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -q`.

## 2026-04-22 | userland | ech-tools-format-env-host-bridge-slice

- Decision: Expanded `ech-tools` shell-bridge coverage from 57 commands to 73 commands by routing `cal`, `comm`, `expand`, `expr`, `fold`, `getconf`, `join`, `link`, `logname`, `nl`, `printenv`, `sleep`, `time`, `tty`, `unexpand`, and `unlink` through shell-owned implementations.
- Decision: Added host-smoke-aware metadata and file-operation helpers so `test`, `stat`, `truncate`, `link`, and `unlink` observe the in-memory host shell file bridge instead of bypassing it through F2FS-only paths during unit tests.
- Decision: `truncate` now accepts both `truncate -s <size> <file>` and the repo's existing `truncate <file> <size>` ordering, preserving Linux-style usage while keeping older shell tests and scripts functional.
- Rationale: This slice closes the formatting/environment/tool glue that already existed in local shell logic, grows Linux/POSIX familiarity without inventing new command names, and removes false negatives from host-side tests where the command surface and file adapter previously disagreed.
- Tradeoffs: `sleep` remains integer-seconds only and busy-waits on the current TSC millisecond source; `link` and `unlink` use host bridge copy/remove semantics under host smoke instead of inode-sharing semantics because the host test adapter only exposes file-content ownership, not native hard-link metadata.
- Validation: `rustfmt --edition 2021 src\\shell\\mod.rs src\\userland\\ech_tools.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib ech_tools -- --nocapture`.

## 2026-04-22 | userland | ech-tools-tier0-shell-bridge-complete

- Decision: Completed the `ech-tools` Tier 0 shell bridge by routing the remaining seven commands through shell-owned builtins: `basename`, `dirname`, `printf`, `tee`, `tr`, `true`, and `false`.
- Decision: Added explicit `Shell.last_exit_code` ownership so `execute_line`, `execute_chained`, pipelines, and `ech-tools <command> ...` observe command status directly instead of inferring success from stdout text.
- Rationale: Tier 0 already had the command catalog, shell registration, and 15 bridged commands. Finishing the remaining seven in the same dispatch surface closes the non-privileged bring-up tier without introducing a second adapter ABI or duplicating shell semantics.
- Tradeoffs: `printf` currently follows the shell's whitespace tokenization boundary, so space-containing format strings still need quoting support from the parser rather than from the builtin itself. `tee` writes through the host/test bridge or F2FS create/write path and returns shell-visible failures on write errors.

## 2026-04-22 | valkyrie | feature-gated-bridge-facade-restored

- Decision: Restored `src/valkyrie_bridge.rs` as a zero-runtime compatibility facade that re-exports `crate::valkyrie_virt::*` under the existing `#[cfg(feature = "valkyrie")]` public module name.
- Rationale: `src/lib.rs` already exposes `pub mod valkyrie_virt;` as the implementation owner and separately declared `pub mod valkyrie_bridge;` behind the `valkyrie` feature. The file itself was missing, which broke module resolution for `cargo fmt`/feature builds even though no independent bridge implementation existed in-tree.
- Tradeoffs: This keeps the expected feature-gated public surface without inventing a second Valkyrie implementation or deleting the bridge-facing API name. The facade is intentionally thin; ownership and behavior stay in `valkyrie_virt`.
- Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib --features valkyrie -q` and `.qoder/skills/echos-architect/scripts/echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\\valkyrie_bridge.rs,docs\\agent\\decision-log.md` are the required verification boundary for this repair.

## 2026-04-20 | toybox-selected-terminal-tools-import | Imported 0BSD Toybox terminal utility source subset

- Decision: Added `third_party/curated/toybox/` from `landley/toybox` tag `0.8.13`, commit `a61f9fe68fafdabf2913b9498ce9ae1a086ed11d`.
- Decision: Imported only the 0BSD runtime subset needed for first `ech-tools` scope: `main.c`, `toys.h`, `lib/`, `toys/posix/{cat,cp,echo,grep,head,ls,mkdir,rm,tail,wc}.c`, and matching upstream tests for `cat`, `cp`, `echo`, `grep`, `head`, `ls`, `mkdir`, `mv`, `rm`, `tail`, and `wc`.
- Decision: Excluded upstream `kconfig/` because the upstream tree marks that build configurator as GPLv2 build-only code; echOS will generate selected-command config/header glue locally rather than importing GPL build infrastructure.
- Decision: No Toybox source was edited in place and no shell/runtime wiring was added in this slice. The next slice must add echOS-owned file IO, directory IO, stdio, argv/env, CWD, exit-status, metadata, and allocator adapters before exposing commands.
- Rationale: Toybox is the right terminal utility seed for echOS because it is compact and 0BSD, but the usable boundary is a selected command set through a measured POSIX/VFS adapter, not a full Unix userland transplant.
- Boundary: This records license provenance and source availability for first terminal commands. It does not claim command execution, POSIX conformance, shell registration, or pass/fail status of upstream Toybox tests inside echOS.

## 2026-04-19 | ext4-journal-corruption-readonly-fallback | Closed JBD2 checksum, commit-order, and corrupt-journal containment lane

- Decision: `src/fs/ext4_journal.rs` now stamps and verifies CRC32 checksums for journal superblock, descriptor, data, and commit blocks at the buffer boundary.
- Decision: Journal commit publication now enforces descriptor write -> data write -> commit block -> checkpoint -> superblock update ordering through `JournalCommitPhase`; the superblock sequence/start update is only written after the checkpoint phase succeeds.
- Decision: Journal recovery and replay scanning reject checksum mismatches and invalid superblock bounds before accepting transaction metadata.
- Decision: `src/fs/ext4.rs` records `read_only` mount state, converts journal init/recovery corruption into read-only mount fallback, and rejects mutating ext4 operations while read-only.
- Rationale: The release checklist still had an open filesystem corruption lane where malformed journal metadata could be accepted without an integrity check and mount handling did not have a fail-closed read-only containment path.
- Boundary: This closes the current in-memory/resident journal validation and ext4 read-only policy surface. The buffer-only `replay_journal(...)` scanner reports replay candidates and validates corruption; physical block-device replay writes remain outside that API contract.
- Validation: `rustfmt --edition 2021 src/fs/ext4_journal.rs src/fs/ext4.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib ext4_journal::tests -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib fs::ext4::tests -- --nocapture`; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `.qoder/skills/echos-architect/scripts/echos_gate.ps1 -Paths ...` exited 0 with raw-allocation/snapshot-clone review warnings only.

## 2026-04-19 | oom-allocation-context-split | Split kernel-critical frame allocation from user-attributed OOM recovery

- Decision: `src/memory/mod.rs` now routes frame allocation through `FrameAllocationContext::{KernelCritical, UserFault}`.
- Decision: Generic `FrameAllocator<Size4KiB>` calls on `MemoryManager` now use `allocate_kernel_frame()`, preserving reclaim/writeback but preventing user-process OOM kills and current-cgroup charging for kernel-critical/page-table metadata allocations.
- Decision: User lazy-fault data frames, swap-in frames, image/file page frames, COW replacement frames, and user PML4 creation now use explicit `allocate_user_frame()`.
- Decision: OOM kill, measured victim selection, post-kill reclaim/writeback, and current-cgroup charge are gated behind `FrameAllocationContext::UserFault`.
- Rationale: The OOM recovery lane was functional, but the same trait call handled both kernel/page-table metadata pressure and user-attributable page faults. That made kernel-critical allocation failure capable of killing a user task as a side effect.
- Boundary: This split covers visible `MemoryManager` frame allocation contexts in `src/memory/mod.rs`. It does not introduce per-frame owner metadata for deallocation accounting, and THP contiguous allocation still falls back to 4 KiB user-page allocation when contiguous huge-page allocation fails.
- Validation: `rustfmt --edition 2021 src/memory/mod.rs`; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib oom::tests -- --nocapture`; `.qoder/skills/echos-architect/scripts/echos_gate.ps1 -Paths ...` exited 0 with review warnings only.

## 2026-04-19 | memory-virt-to-phys-fail-closed | Removed unmapped-address panic from DMA/mapper translation lane

- Decision: `src/memory/mod.rs` now exposes `try_virt_to_phys(...)` and `try_virt_to_phys_u64(...)` as the non-panicking translation contract.
- Decision: Legacy `virt_to_phys(...)` and `virt_to_phys_u64(...)` no longer panic on unmapped addresses; they emit serial telemetry and return the existing zero sentinel for compatibility.
- Decision: `src/drivers/nvme.rs` routes PRP buffer translation through `dma_buffer_phys(...)` and returns `NvmeError::DataTransferError` before submitting commands when a buffer is not mapped.
- Decision: `src/drivers/usb_jail.rs` fails control-transfer setup with an explicit error when the data buffer cannot be translated for DMA.
- Decision: `src/pe_loader.rs` maps kernel blobs into user space through `try_virt_to_phys(...)`, preserving `PeError::MemoryAllocation` instead of relying on a panic-capable translation wrapper.
- Rationale: The OOM/panic audit gate still found two memory translation panic sites after allocator and GOP cleanup. Unmapped DMA/mapper buffers are recoverable faults, not system invariants.
- Boundary: This closes direct `virt_to_phys` panic sites and known live callers. It does not claim full DMA ownership/provenance validation or removal of unrelated panic sites outside this memory translation lane.
- Validation: `rustfmt --edition 2021 src/memory/mod.rs src/drivers/nvme.rs src/drivers/usb_jail.rs src/pe_loader.rs`; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `.qoder/skills/echos-architect/scripts/echos_gate.ps1 -Paths ...` exited 0 with review warnings only and no `panic-sites` hit for this lane.

## 2026-04-19 | global-oom-reclaim-kill-retry | Wired measured OOM victim selection into frame allocation failure recovery

- Decision: `src/memory/mod.rs` now exposes `AddressSpacePageCounts` and `address_space_page_counts(...)`, deriving resident anonymous/file pages from LRU `space_counts`, swapped pages from in-memory/device swap slot maps, and committed pages from VMA spans.
- Decision: `src/task/scheduler.rs` `TaskInfo` now carries RSS, swap, committed pages, runtime, kernel/user classification, and child count so OOM selection does not rely on a hard-coded per-task RSS guess.
- Decision: `src/memory/oom.rs` now converts scheduler task snapshots into `OomProcessInfo` through `process_info_from_task(...)`; measured RSS/swap wins, with committed-page fallback capped to 64 pages only when resident accounting is unavailable.
- Decision: The first OOM kill attempt is no longer blocked by the recovery cooldown. Later attempts still honor `OOM_RECOVERY_WAIT_TICKS`.
- Decision: `MemoryManager::allocate_frame()` now retries PMM allocation after OOM kill by running global reclaim, writeback budget, and then `pmm.allocate_frame()` before returning `None`.
- Rationale: The previous OOM lane had a visible policy skeleton, but the actual allocation failure path used fixed `rss_pages: 256`, could suppress the first kill because `last_kill_tick` started at zero, and did not retry allocation after killing a victim.
- Boundary: This closes the global frame-allocation recovery sequence and measured victim input lane. It does not split kernel-critical allocation policy from user allocation policy; that remains open in `RELEASE_PLAN.md`.
- Validation: `rustfmt --edition 2021 src/memory/mod.rs src/memory/oom.rs src/task/scheduler.rs`; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib oom::tests -- --nocapture`; `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `.qoder/skills/echos-architect/scripts/echos_gate.ps1 -Paths ...` exited 0 with review warnings only.

## 2026-04-19 | allocator-gop-panic-unwrap-burndown | Removed allocator OOM/layout and GOP shadow-buffer panic seams from active hot paths

- Decision: `TaskColdData.stack` now stores `Arc<KernelStack>` so scheduler/task metadata clones share the owned stack handle instead of deep-copying stack memory and panicking on allocation failure.
- Decision: `KernelStack::try_clone_stack(...)` is the explicit fallible deep-copy lane for future fork-like stack duplication; it preserves guard-page activation and returns `None` on allocation or guard remap failure.
- Decision: TLSF initialization, linked-list allocation layout normalization, and `heap_alloc`/`heap_free` invalid layout paths now return null/`None`/no-op rather than using unwrap/expect in allocator hot paths.
- Decision: Allocator doctrine violations no longer panic in debug kernels; they emit serial telemetry and return `DoctrineError::PolicyViolation`.
- Decision: GOP framebuffer `buffer_mut()` no longer unwraps the shadow buffer. If double buffering is absent, it returns a direct front-buffer slice using the framebuffer stride/height contract.
- Rationale: The previous panic/unwrap audit had closed network AEAD/UDP seams but allocator and display paths still had live resource-exhaustion or optional-buffer assumptions that could turn OOM/UI state into kernel crash.
- Boundary: This pass closes the visible allocator/GOP panic/unwrap seams found under `src/allocator` and `src/gop/framebuffer.rs`. It does not claim global reclaim/kill OOM policy, fork-stack semantics, or repo-wide GUI/compositor panic inventory is complete.
- Validation: `rustfmt --edition 2021 src/allocator/stack.rs src/task/task.rs src/allocator/tlsf.rs src/allocator/linked_list.rs src/allocator/mod.rs src/allocator/doctrine.rs src/gop/framebuffer.rs`; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib win32_alloc_enforces_per_process_byte_budget -- --nocapture`; `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `.qoder/skills/echos-architect/scripts/echos_gate.ps1 -Paths ...` exited 0 with review warnings only.

## 2026-04-19 | hot-path-panic-unwrap-network-aead-burndown | Removed network/TLS/QUIC panic-on-invalid-input seams from active AEAD and UDP bind lanes

- Decision: `src/net/udp.rs` ephemeral port allocation now returns `NetError::AddrNotAvailable` when the dynamic port space is exhausted instead of panicking after randomized and linear scans both fail.
- Decision: `src/net/tls.rs` AES/AES-GCM construction now rejects invalid key lengths with `TlsError::InvalidMessage`; AES-GCM encrypt/decrypt rejects non-96-bit nonces before slice copying; TLS record crypto and 0-RTT send paths propagate those failures instead of relying on panic/unwrap.
- Decision: `src/net/quic.rs` packet protection now treats invalid IV/key/AEAD setup as `None` and no longer returns plaintext/ciphertext as a fallback on unsupported key shape.
- Rationale: The host test lane had recovered after stale MSVC artifacts were cleaned, but the next release blocker was real: attacker-controlled or resource-exhaustion inputs could still reach panic/unwrap surfaces in network hot paths.
- Boundary: This slice closes the visible UDP bind exhaustion and TLS/QUIC AEAD setup/nonce hot-path panic class. It does not claim the repo-wide panic/unwrap inventory is complete; allocator, GUI/compositor, memory `virt_to_phys`, and remaining non-network sites still need their own pass.
- Validation: `rustfmt --edition 2021 src/net/udp.rs src/net/tls.rs src/net/quic.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib bind_port_zero_fails_closed_when_ephemeral_space_is_exhausted -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib aes_gcm_rejects -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib win32_alloc_enforces_per_process_byte_budget -- --nocapture`. Repo-level `cargo fmt` remains blocked by missing `src/valkyrie_bridge.rs` module resolution.

## 2026-04-17 | high-lane-p1-win32-random-closure | Closed Win32 handle/allocator/atexit P1 lanes and removed PRNG modulo-bias surface

- Decision: `src/win32.rs` now enforces a process-scoped handle ceiling (`MAX_HANDLES_PER_PROCESS = 4096`) across file and Internet handle allocation lanes. Exhaustion paths fail closed with `ERROR_TOO_MANY_OPEN_FILES` and return invalid handles (`INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE`/`0`) instead of inserting unbounded state.
- Decision: WinINet/WinHTTP handle ownership is tracked in `INTERNET_HANDLE_OWNERS`, with close-path synchronization (`close_internet_handle`) and COM uninitialize cleanup of thread-owned Internet handles via `close_owner_internet_handles(...)` to prevent stale owner leakage.
- Decision: `kernel32::exit_process(...)` now calls `ole32::co_uninitialize()` before scheduler exit so process-main-thread teardown also drains COM apartment ownership and bound Internet handles.
- Decision: Added explicit regression `win32::tests::exit_process_cleans_thread_owned_internet_handles` and made `kernel32::exit_process(...)` return early under `#[cfg(test)]` after cleanup, so the `ExitProcess` COM/WinHTTP cleanup path is directly testable without entering non-returning scheduler exit in host test mode.
- Decision: `read_user_stack_arg(...)` moved from direct mapped-page precheck + raw dereference to `copy_from_user_nofault(...)`-backed copy discipline in `src/memory/mod.rs`; ingress now fails closed on lazy-fault failure, translation mismatch, or host target fallback.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` allocator lane now tracks per-allocation owner metadata (`Win32AllocMeta`) and process allocation usage (`ALLOC_USAGE_PER_PROCESS`), enforcing single-allocation cap (`MAX_WIN32_ALLOC_SIZE = 256MiB`) plus per-process byte budget (`MAX_WIN32_ALLOC_BYTES_PER_PROCESS = 512MiB`). Reserve/release accounting is balanced across alloc/realloc/dealloc fail paths.
- Decision: CRT handler flood lane is fail-closed: `msvcrt::atexit`/`_onexit` cap registrations at `MAX_ATEXIT_HANDLERS = 32` and reject overflow (`-1` / `None`).
- Decision: Win32 user ABI stack-arg ingress now uses memory-layer no-fault copy (`copy_from_user_nofault`) rather than local raw pointer dereference, shrinking per-call-site user-access logic in `src/win32.rs`.
- Decision: Added `src/memory/mod.rs` `copy_from_user_nofault(...)` for bare-metal targets (`target_os = "none"|"uefi"`), using per-page translation + lazy-fault bring-up (`handle_user_page_fault`) and post-copy page-identity verification; `src/win32.rs` `read_user_stack_arg(...)` now consumes this helper and fails closed on copy mismatch/fault.
- Decision: Host targets keep fail-closed behavior for no-fault helper (`false`) and Win32 stack-arg path (`0`) to avoid privileged user-access paths in host test binaries.
- Decision: `src/random.rs` `next_range(max)` moved from modulo reduction to rejection sampling (`zone = u32::MAX - u32::MAX % max`), removing modulo-bias on non-power-of-two ranges.
- Decision: `src/crypto/rsa.rs` remains on RustCrypto `rsa` crate `0.9.10` (`signature` `2.2.0`) for signing/verification path; this lane does not vendor/adapt external RSA arithmetic and keeps ZD-018 timing-closure as separate work.
- Rationale: P1 backlog still had open DoS and entropy-quality seams in Win32 emulation and RNG helper glue despite earlier high-lane closures. This pass hardens the ownership/accounting contracts and removes one deterministic distribution bias without widening ABI surface area.
- Boundary: This pass closes the ZD-039 Win32 stack-arg ingress with an in-repo no-fault copy wrapper for this read path. It does not claim full process-pinning/TOCTOU elimination for arbitrary long user buffers or immediate migration of all POSIX/Win32 raw pointer ingress sites onto the same helper.
- Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib win32::tests::read_user_stack_arg_returns_zero_when_stack_page_is_unmapped -- --exact` (blocked by current workspace-wide MSVC link instability: unresolved symbols during test link); `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib win32::tests::copy_from_user_nofault_returns_false_on_host_targets -- --exact` (same linker blocker); `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/memory/mod.rs src/win32.rs RELEASE_PLAN.md docs/agent/decision-log.md`

## 2026-04-18 | high-lane-zd018-rsa-legacy-private-op-isolation | Closed ZD-018 default build lane by feature-gating legacy variable-time RSA private ops

- Decision: Added `rsa_legacy_private_ops` feature in `Cargo.toml` and moved local variable-time private-op arithmetic behind this explicit opt-in gate in `src/crypto/rsa.rs`.
- Decision: The following legacy/private-op functions are now compiled only when `rsa_legacy_private_ops` is enabled: `BigInt::mod_pow`, `RsaPrivateKey::generate`, `generate_prime`, `miller_rabin_test`, `random_range`, and `rsa_crt_private_op`.
- Decision: Default `RsaPrivateKey::sign(...)` path remains bound to RustCrypto (`ExternalRsaPrivateKey::sign_with_rng(...)`), and public verify path stays on RustCrypto public key verify.
- Decision: Added regression `crypto::rsa::tests::sign_and_verify_roundtrip_uses_external_rsa_lane` to pin that local key wrapper signs through external backend and verifies with corresponding public key.
- Rationale: ZD-018 remained open because local bigint/private-op code containing data-dependent branches was still available in default build surface even though active sign lane already used RustCrypto. Feature-gating removes accidental default-path reactivation risk without widening ABI.
- Boundary: This closes default production build isolation for ZD-018. If `rsa_legacy_private_ops` is explicitly enabled, legacy variable-time lane is intentionally reintroduced and must be treated as non-production/test-only. This pass does not add dudect-style timing variance evidence.
- Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib --features rsa_legacy_private_ops`; `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --lib`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib crypto::rsa::tests::sign_and_verify_roundtrip_uses_external_rsa_lane -- --exact --nocapture` (blocked by current workspace-wide MSVC linker unresolved externals)

## 2026-04-17 | security-hardening | pe-loader-win32-abi-fail-closed-bounds

- Decision: PE load ingress now validates optional-header shape, `SizeOfHeaders <= SizeOfImage`, data-directory count, stack/heap reserve and commit ceilings, section virtual/raw checked ranges, entrypoint executable-section membership, and RVA-to-file conversion without arithmetic wrap.
- Decision: Import resolution is fail-closed for normal and delay IAT writes: unresolved DLL/symbol pairs no longer receive the generic stub target; load paths deallocate the transient image buffer before returning `PeError::ImportNotFound`.
- Decision: x64 exception metadata validation now rejects malformed runtime-function entries and EHANDLER/UHANDLER handler RVAs outside the PE image before publishing unwind metadata.
- Decision: TLS runtime initialization now validates the TLS directory, index slot, callback table, and callback targets against the mapped image range before writing the TLS index or invoking callback metadata.
- Decision: Win32 `VirtualAlloc` rejects single reservations above 256MiB, and `CreateWindowExA` rejects non-positive or >8192 dimensions with checked pixel-buffer sizing. Existing `GetEnvironmentVariableA` and GUI `SurfaceManager` bounds were confirmed and documented closed in `RELEASE_PLAN.md`.
- Rationale: The prior PE/Win32 ABI surface had several "skip and continue" paths that transformed malformed attacker-controlled metadata into partially loaded images. The hardened contract rejects malformed metadata at parse/load time and prevents oversized ABI allocations from entering allocator-backed hot paths.
- Boundary: This closes structural PE section/directory/import/TLS/unwind bounds and Win32 single-call allocation/window-size abuse. It does not claim full PE Authenticode policy, per-process aggregate Win32 heap quotas, CRT `atexit` caps, or fuzz-campaign coverage.
- Validation: `rustfmt src\pe_loader.rs src\win32.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib pe_loader -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib virtual_alloc_rejects_oversized_reservation -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib create_window_ex_rejects_invalid_surface_dimensions -- --nocapture`.

## 2026-04-17 | security-hardening | tls-psk-binder-and-constant-time-verifier-compare

- Decision: Added a shared `crate::crypto::constant_time_eq(...)` contract and routed secret-derived verifier comparisons through it across TLS, IPsec, WireGuard, Argon2, ChaCha20-Poly1305, Ed25519 verify, and X.509 RSA/PSS signature helpers.
- Decision: Reworked TLS PSK binder generation to use the RFC 8446 ClientHello truncation boundary instead of hashing the zero-filled binder bytes, and exposed `verify_tls13_psk_binder(...)` for server-side selected-identity verification.
- Decision: `TlsClient::process_server_hello(...)` now rejects a server-selected PSK identity when no PSK was offered or when the selected identity/cipher suite does not match the offered ticket.
- Rationale: MAC/tag/hash compare hardening was partially duplicated and PSK binder evidence only covered generation/selection. A single compare helper plus parser-backed binder verification removes per-module drift and makes the PSK lane testable at the protocol boundary.
- Boundary: This closes secret-derived verifier byte comparisons. It does not claim constant-time RSA private-key arithmetic, bigint reduction, scalar multiplication internals, or public-value equality checks whose operands are not secret-derived verifier material.
- Validation: `rustfmt src\net\tls.rs src\net\ipsec.rs src\net\wireguard.rs src\crypto\mod.rs src\crypto\argon2.rs src\crypto\signature.rs src\crypto\chacha20.rs src\crypto\ed25519.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib tls13_psk_binder -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib process_server_hello_rejects_unoffered_psk_selection -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib constant_time_eq_rejects_length_and_content_mismatch -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib ipsec_process_inbound_rejects_invalid_icv_and_counts_auth_failure -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib wireguard_initiation_rejects_invalid_mac1 -- --nocapture`.

## 2026-04-17 | host-gate | full-lib-host-parity-and-win32-typelib-winsock-closeout

- Decision: Closed the host-test trap lane by making hardware-facing paths fail closed or use explicit host adapters under `test`/Windows: PCI config reads, VirtIO backend readiness, ATA fallback, power MSR/cache/IPI operations, SMP CPU identity, and loopback networking no longer execute privileged I/O or unresolved device paths in the host test process.
- Decision: Tightened Win32/OLE TypeLib parsing so text `type=...|members`, compact MSFT metadata envelopes, structured MSFT/SLTG section tables, UTF-16 string harvesting, `func:`/`var:` compact layouts, and duplicate/default member upgrades produce deterministic state instead of heuristic over-harvest or stale default descriptors.
- Decision: Added a host-backed Win32 file-handle lane for create/write/read TypeLib tests and wired `ws2_32`/`wsock32` declared exports through real resolver targets, including `inet_ntoa`, so exactness snapshots no longer see declared stub exports.
- Decision: Preserved display single-output policy semantics and shell test isolation by avoiding policy clobber on single-monitor capabilities, resetting shell globals between tests, and giving completion a bounded fallback PATH corpus when host PATH probing is unavailable.
- Rationale: The release/debug lane was blocked less by kernel logic than by host-test side effects: privileged instructions, port I/O, missing host file persistence, and overly broad binary metadata heuristics. Separating host adapters from live hardware ownership keeps test evidence meaningful without weakening bare-metal fail-closed behavior.
- Boundary: This is host/library parity and parser exactness work. It does not claim live QEMU/WHPX acceleration success, and it does not replace hardware smoke evidence for MMIO/DMA paths.
- Validation: `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -- --test-threads=1 --nocapture` -> `544 passed`; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -q`; `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`.

## 2026-04-15 | medium-lane-zd032-rsa-mod-inverse-negative-coefficient-fix | Closed RSA mod_inverse negative-intermediate lane with modular-coefficient normalization

- Decision: `src/crypto/rsa.rs` `BigInt::mod_inverse(...)` no longer collapses negative Extended Euclid intermediates to `0`; coefficient update now uses modular normalization (`q_times_newt = quotient * newt mod modulus`, then `next_t = t - q_times_newt` in ring form, i.e. `t + modulus - q_times_newt` when needed).
- Decision: Inverse computation now starts from reduced residue (`newr = self.mod_reduce(modulus)`) and fail-closes early on zero-equivalent operands (`self mod modulus == 0`) or `modulus == 0`.
- Decision: GCD boundary remains explicit and fail-closed: when terminal `r != 1`, `mod_inverse(...)` returns `None` (non-coprime inputs).
- Decision: Added regressions in `src/crypto/rsa.rs`:
  - `mod_inverse_handles_negative_intermediate_coefficients`
  - `mod_inverse_returns_none_for_non_coprime_values`
- Decision: Updated `RELEASE_PLAN.md` to mark `ECHOS-ZD-032` closed and align stale progress bullets with current closure state.
- Rationale: Previous unsigned-only coefficient path could not represent negative Extended Euclid intermediates and forced them to zero, which risks incorrect `d = e^-1 mod φ(n)` in RSA key math. Ring-normalized coefficient arithmetic preserves correctness without introducing signed-bigint representation in this lane.
- Boundary: This closure is scoped to the local `BigInt::mod_inverse` arithmetic contract in `src/crypto/rsa.rs`; it does not claim constant-time properties or full removal of legacy bigint helpers from RSA module internals.
- Validation: `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib crypto::rsa::tests::mod_inverse_handles_negative_intermediate_coefficients -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib crypto::rsa::tests::mod_inverse_returns_none_for_non_coprime_values -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`

## 2026-04-15 | medium-lane-zd021-ipsec-inbound-icv-enforcement | Closed IPsec inbound ICV soft-failure lane with fail-closed ESP authentication and stats accounting

- Decision: `src/net/ipsec.rs` `IpsecManager::process_inbound(...)` now enforces ICV authentication before replay/decrypt by deriving SA-specific ICV length, splitting packet into encrypted payload + received ICV, and calling `sa.verify_icv(...)` fail-closed.

- Decision: Added regression tests in `src/net/ipsec.rs`:
  - `ipsec_process_inbound_accepts_valid_icv_and_updates_stats`
  - `ipsec_process_inbound_rejects_invalid_icv_and_counts_auth_failure`
- Decision: Updated `RELEASE_PLAN.md` to mark `ECHOS-ZD-021` closed with concrete ingress behavior and telemetry evidence.
- Rationale: Prior manager path decrypted `pkt[8..]` without mandatory ICV enforcement, leaving an authentication-soft-failure seam even though `verify_icv` existed. Moving ICV check into live ingress path closes this class fail-closed and makes abuse visible in counters.
- Boundary: This pass hardens current ESP framing used by `process_outbound/process_inbound`; it does not claim full RFC-level AH/ESN feature parity or anti-replay window sizing policy beyond existing SA model.

## 2026-04-15 | medium-lane-zd009-wireguard-endpoint-peer-selection | Closed WireGuard multi-peer handshake misassignment with endpoint-bound fail-closed peer selection

- Decision: `src/net/wireguard.rs` initiation path no longer binds every Type-1 handshake to the first peer in map order. `process_initiation(...)` now resolves the target peer via `select_handshake_peer(src_ip, src_port)`.
- Decision: In multi-peer configurations, handshake ownership is selected only when `(peer.endpoint_ip == src_ip && peer.endpoint_port == src_port)` matches exactly.
- Decision: Ambiguous multi-peer endpoint mappings now fail closed with `WgError::AuthFailed`; unmatched endpoints fail with `WgError::PeerNotFound` before session mutation.
- Decision: Single-peer bootstrap behavior is preserved for compatibility (endpoint may be unset before first successful handshake); this keeps existing one-peer provisioning flows functional while removing map-order routing in the multi-peer case.
- Decision: Added focused regressions in `src/net/wireguard.rs`: `wireguard_initiation_selects_peer_by_source_endpoint`, `wireguard_initiation_rejects_when_multi_peer_endpoint_unmatched`, and `wireguard_initiation_rejects_when_multi_peer_endpoint_ambiguous`.
- Decision: Updated `RELEASE_PLAN.md` to mark `ECHOS-ZD-009` and its summary-row state as closed with endpoint-selection evidence.
- Rationale: Prior `for ... break` peer assignment let attacker traffic or misrouted packets bind session state to an unrelated peer whenever more than one peer existed. Endpoint-bound selection converts this from map-order behavior to deterministic ownership and removes accidental session cross-binding.
- Boundary: This lane binds selection to source endpoint only; it does not claim full WireGuard identity routing parity (public-key-indexed initiation decode and full cookie-reply state machine remain separate evolution lanes).
- Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::wireguard::tests::wireguard_initiation_selects_peer_by_source_endpoint -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::wireguard::tests::wireguard_initiation_rejects_when_multi_peer_endpoint_unmatched -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::wireguard::tests::wireguard_initiation_rejects_when_multi_peer_endpoint_ambiguous -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::wireguard::tests::wireguard_initiation_accepts_valid_mac1_and_mac2 -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::wireguard::tests::wireguard_initiation_rejects_invalid_mac1 -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::wireguard::tests::wireguard_initiation_rejects_invalid_mac2_when_present -- --exact --nocapture`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/net/wireguard.rs,RELEASE_PLAN.md,docs/agent/decision-log.md`

## 2026-04-15 | high-lane-round5-rsa-pkcs1-sha2-and-bigint-reduction-closure | Closed remaining RSA HIGH blockers with external verifier path and fail-closed bigint behavior

- Decision: `src/crypto/rsa.rs` RSA sign/verify hash mapping was consolidated in `rsa_pkcs1v15_hash_and_padding(...)` and now uses SHA-2 (`sha2::Sha256`/`Sha512`) for `sha256`/`sha512` instead of SHA-3 stand-ins. PKCS#1 v1.5 operations continue through RustCrypto `rsa` verifier/signer path (`Pkcs1v15Sign`), removing manual EMSA parsing ambiguity from the live verify path.
- Decision: `src/crypto/rsa.rs` `BigInt::div(...)` now returns `Option<BigInt>` and fail-closes on division-by-zero with `None` instead of silently returning `0`; `mod_inverse(...)` propagates this boundary via `?`.
- Decision: `src/crypto/rsa.rs` `BigInt::mod_reduce(...)` was replaced from local trial-subtraction/`Vec::insert(0, ..)` path with `BigUint` remainder calculation, removing the prior cubic-style reduction hotspot and its DoS-prone behavior.
- Decision: Added regression corpus in `src/crypto/rsa.rs` for (a) SHA-256 mapping correctness vs SHA-3 mismatch, (b) PKCS#1 v1.5 trailing-garbage rejection, (c) division-by-zero fail-closed behavior, and (d) `mod_reduce` parity with `BigUint` remainder.
- Decision: Updated DNSSEC RSA-SHA1 verifier test in `src/net/dnssec.rs` to generate and sign with a deterministic test RSA key (RustCrypto signing path) instead of synthetic manual PKCS#1 block bytes, so DNSSEC corpus matches the tightened RSA verifier contract.
- Decision: `RELEASE_PLAN.md` now marks `ZD-024`, `ZD-025`, `ZD-029`, and `ZD-030` as closed with current-file evidence and narrows "HIGH açık kalanlar" focus accordingly.
- Rationale: Remaining HIGH list still contained RSA-specific correctness/security/performance items despite earlier fatal-lane RSA delegation work. This pass closes the stale high-risk seams in one integrated lane: protocol hash correctness, PKCS#1 verifier strictness, arithmetic fail-closed behavior, and reduction complexity.
- Boundary: This pass does not claim symlink-depth closure (`ZD-028`) or full algorithmic modernization to Montgomery/PSS across all RSA contexts. It closes the listed HIGH blockers by replacing vulnerable legacy behavior on the active sign/verify and bigint helper paths.
- Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib crypto::rsa::tests::`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::dnssec::tests::rrsig_verify_signature_accepts_rsa_sha1_family -- --exact --nocapture`

## 2026-04-15 | high-lane-zd028-reclassification-no-active-symlink-follow | Reclassified ZD-028 as N/A for current release surface after code-path verification

- Decision: Re-audited the active filesystem path-resolution chain and marked `ZD-028` (symlink traversal depth) as N/A for the current release surface in `RELEASE_PLAN.md`. Current `f2fs`/unified VFS resolution paths walk directory entries/inodes but do not follow symlink targets recursively.
- Evidence: `src/fs/f2fs.rs` `open_inode_by_path(...)` only resolves path components via directory entries and inode reads, without a symlink-target follow step. `src/fs/vfs_unified.rs` ext4/ntfs/btrfs resolution paths likewise perform component resolution without recursive symlink-follow transitions. `src/posix.rs` currently returns `ENOSYS` for `sys_symlink` and `sys_readlink` syscall surfaces.
- Decision: Updated `RELEASE_PLAN.md` to close `ZD-028` as "N/A / stale finding" for this release lane, and shifted remaining HIGH focus to still-open items (`ZD-015`, `ZD-016`, `ZD-017`, and related HIGH backlog entries).
- Rationale: The original finding assumed an active symlink-follow recursion path that could loop indefinitely. That assumption no longer matches current repo behavior on the release-facing path resolver surfaces.
- Boundary: This is not a claim that future symlink-follow support is safe by default. If recursive symlink resolution is introduced later, the contract must include `MAX_SYMLINK_DEPTH=40`, `ELOOP` propagation, and regression tests before the feature can be treated as hardened.
- Validation: static code-path inspection only (no runtime behavior changed in this pass).

## 2026-04-15 | high-lane-zd016-zd017-pe-header-bounds-closeout | Closed PE loader section-count and image-size HIGH DoS lanes with fail-closed bounds checks

- Decision: Added explicit PE header bound constants in `src/pe_loader.rs`: `MAX_PE_SECTIONS = 96` and `MAX_PE_IMAGE_SIZE = 256 * 1024 * 1024`.
- Decision: Introduced shared validators `validate_section_count(...)` and `validate_image_size(...)`, then wired them into all active PE load paths: `PeLoader::load(...)`, `PeLoader::load_into_memory(...)`, and `PeLoader::load_into_user_buffer(...)`.
- Decision: Hardened parser ingress checks in hot paths by validating file-header and optional-header bounds before unsafe header casts, enforcing exact optional-header size contract (`size_of::<ImageOptionalHeader64>() + 16*8`) and clamping header copy length to allocated image size.
- Decision: Added regression tests in `src/pe_loader.rs`:
  - `load_rejects_section_count_above_limit`
  - `load_rejects_size_of_image_above_limit`
  - `load_into_memory_rejects_size_of_image_above_limit`
- Decision: Updated `RELEASE_PLAN.md` to mark `ZD-016` and `ZD-017` as closed, and promoted corresponding quick-win checklist entries (`PE section limit (96)`) to done.
- Rationale: The PE loader previously trusted attacker-controlled `number_of_sections` and `size_of_image` too early in multiple ingestion paths, enabling OOM/DoS pressure. Centralized validators close these seams consistently instead of relying on path-local assumptions.
- Boundary: This pass is scoped to section-count and image-size bounds (`ZD-016`, `ZD-017`). It does not claim closure for other PE hardening items such as `e_lfanew` structural validation edge cases (`ZD-022`) or additional Win32 allocator policy items.
- Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib pe_loader::tests::load_rejects_section_count_above_limit -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib pe_loader::tests::load_rejects_size_of_image_above_limit -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib pe_loader::tests::load_into_memory_rejects_size_of_image_above_limit -- --exact --nocapture`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/pe_loader.rs,RELEASE_PLAN.md,docs/agent/decision-log.md`

## 2026-04-15 | high-lane-zd015-ipsec-legacy-cipher-gating | Closed IPsec DES/3DES HIGH lane with compile-time legacy gate and fail-closed default policy

- Decision: `src/net/ipsec.rs` now rejects `IPSEC_ENC_DES_CBC` and `IPSEC_ENC_3DES_CBC` by default in both `SecurityAssociation::encrypt(...)` and `SecurityAssociation::decrypt(...)`, returning `IpsecError::WeakCipherDisabled` instead of executing weak cipher paths.
- Decision: Weak DES/3DES execution remains available only behind explicit feature gate `ipsec_legacy_weak_crypto`; when enabled, each DES/3DES operation emits a serial warning (`[IPSEC] WARNING: weak cipher ... is enabled via feature ipsec_legacy_weak_crypto`).
- Decision: Default fallback dispatch (`default_encrypt_cipher(...)` / `default_decrypt_cipher(...)`) now uses the same feature gate so short-key fallback cannot silently drop to DES when legacy feature is disabled.
- Decision: Added `ipsec_legacy_weak_crypto = []` in `Cargo.toml` features and added targeted tests in `src/net/ipsec.rs` for default fail-closed behavior: `ipsec_des_cbc_rejected_when_legacy_feature_disabled` and `ipsec_3des_cbc_rejected_when_legacy_feature_disabled`.
- Decision: Updated `RELEASE_PLAN.md` to mark `ECHOS-ZD-015` as closed with implementation evidence and regression coverage.
- Rationale: DES/3DES remained runtime-reachable in the active IPsec path despite being cryptographically deprecated/broken. A compile-time opt-in gate plus default fail-closed rejection removes this weak-by-default posture while preserving controlled interoperability override.
- Boundary: This pass does not remove DES/3DES code bodies from the repository; it makes them unreachable by default build policy and explicit when re-enabled. Runtime policy does not attempt automatic remote peer negotiation downgrade logic in this lane.
- Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::ipsec::tests::ipsec_des_cbc_rejected_when_legacy_feature_disabled -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::ipsec::tests::ipsec_3des_cbc_rejected_when_legacy_feature_disabled -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib --features ipsec_legacy_weak_crypto net::ipsec::tests::ipsec_des_cbc_roundtrip_is_stateful -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib --features ipsec_legacy_weak_crypto net::ipsec::tests::ipsec_3des_cbc_roundtrip_is_stateful -- --exact --nocapture`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/net/ipsec.rs,Cargo.toml,RELEASE_PLAN.md,docs/agent/decision-log.md`

## 2026-04-15 | high-lane-zd005-wireguard-mac-gating | Closed WireGuard MAC1/MAC2 HIGH DoS lane with fail-closed initiation authentication

- Decision: `src/net/wireguard.rs` initiation ingress now carries source endpoint context into authentication (`process_message(...)` routes `WG_MSG_INITIATION` to `process_initiation(pkt, src_ip, src_port)`).
- Decision: Added fail-closed MAC1 verification (`verify_initiation_mac1(...)`) over initiation body (`pkt[..116]`) using key `SHA256("wg-mac1" || responder_public_key)`, and reject path returns `WgError::AuthFailed` before any response is emitted.
- Decision: Added fail-closed MAC2 verification (`verify_initiation_mac2(...)`) for non-zero MAC2 values using stateless endpoint cookie `HMAC-SHA256(mac2_cookie_secret, src_ip || src_port || sender_index)`; invalid MAC2 is rejected with `WgError::AuthFailed`.
- Decision: Added local helpers for constant-time tag comparison and deterministic tag derivation, plus per-device `mac2_cookie_secret` key material initialized from hardware RNG.
- Decision: Added focused regression tests in `src/net/wireguard.rs`: `wireguard_initiation_rejects_invalid_mac1`, `wireguard_initiation_rejects_invalid_mac2_when_present`, and `wireguard_initiation_accepts_valid_mac1_and_mac2`.
- Decision: Updated `RELEASE_PLAN.md` to mark `ZD-005` closed and reflect updated HIGH-lane focus after this closure.
- Rationale: Prior initiation path parsed MAC1/MAC2 fields but did not verify them, allowing attacker-controlled initiation floods to trigger responder work and amplification. Explicit MAC verification at ingress removes this unauthenticated handshake acceptance seam.
- Boundary: This pass enforces a deterministic fail-closed MAC gate on initiation path and stateless cookie-based MAC2 validation when present. It does not claim full WireGuard NoiseIK transcript exactness or complete cookie-reply state-machine parity.
- Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::wireguard::tests::wireguard_initiation_rejects_invalid_mac1 -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::wireguard::tests::wireguard_initiation_rejects_invalid_mac2_when_present -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::wireguard::tests::wireguard_initiation_accepts_valid_mac1_and_mac2 -- --exact --nocapture`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/net/wireguard.rs,RELEASE_PLAN.md,docs/agent/decision-log.md`

## 2026-04-15 | high-lane-zd007-zd008-tls-cert-authn-closeout | Closed TLS CertificateVerify and certificate-chain HIGH lanes with fail-closed chain+hostname gating in TlsClient

- Decision: `src/net/tls.rs` `TlsClient` now carries parsed peer chain state (`peer_cert_chain`) and executes server certificate trust checks during `process_certificate(...)` instead of only extracting a leaf public key.
- Decision: Added one-time built-in root initialization in TLS path (`TLS_X509_ROOTS_READY` + `ensure_x509_roots()`) and centralized chain gate helper `validate_tls13_server_certificate_chain(...)`.
- Decision: `process_certificate(...)` now parses TLS 1.3 cert entries via `parse_tls13_certificate_entries(...)`, validates chain with `x509::CertVerifier::verify_chain(...)`, validates SNI hostname with `x509::verify_hostname(...)`, and fails closed on malformed/invalid chains (`TlsError::InvalidCertificate`) or hostname mismatch (`TlsError::CertificateVerificationFailed`).
- Decision: `process_certificate_verify(...)` still performs transcript-bound signature verification, and now also explicitly requires certificate-chain state presence before state advance (`CertificateReceived -> CertificateVerifyReceived`).
- Decision: Added TLS-targeted regression tests in `src/net/tls.rs` for hostname mismatch rejection, matching-hostname acceptance, empty-chain rejection, and malformed TLS certificate-body parsing.
- Decision: Updated `RELEASE_PLAN.md` to mark `ZD-007` and `ZD-008` closed in both detailed sections and zero-day summary table.
- Rationale: Prior state allowed a partial authenticity gap in TLS state-machine ownership by separating trust logic from the core TLS client path; this made call-site omissions possible (especially non-HTTP paths). Moving chain+hostname checks into `TlsClient::process_certificate(...)` closes that class at the handshake core.
- Boundary: This closure secures the visible TLS client handshake lane in-repo (HTTP/DoH/DoT call through `TlsClient` paths). It does not claim external PKI ecosystem parity beyond current built-in roots and existing x509 verifier capabilities.
- Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::tls::tests::validate_tls13_server_certificate_chain_rejects_hostname_mismatch -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::tls::tests::validate_tls13_server_certificate_chain_accepts_matching_hostname -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::tls::tests::validate_tls13_server_certificate_chain_rejects_empty_chain -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::tls::tests::parse_tls13_certificate_entries_rejects_truncated_body -- --exact --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib net::tls::tests::parse_tls13_certificate_entries_rejects_empty_list -- --exact --nocapture`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/net/tls.rs,RELEASE_PLAN.md,docs/agent/decision-log.md`

- Decision: `docs/book-core-v1/` altinda Cilt 1 kaynaklari tek bir repo-yerel pipeline ile standardize edildi. Icerik kaynaklari Markdown (`src/cilt1-core.md`, `src/appendix-algorithm-atlas.md`), diyagram kaynaklari Mermaid (`figures/*.mmd`), build girisi PowerShell (`scripts/build.ps1`) ve PDF render adimi Node (`scripts/build_pdf.js`) olarak sabitlendi.
- Decision: Diyagramlar build oncesi `scripts/render-diagrams.ps1` ile SVG'ye donusturuluyor ve kitap icindeki tum gorseller echOS kaynak algoritmalarina bagli section akislari icin zorunlu tutuluyor (scheduler, memory, io_uring, TLS/QUIC/WireGuard).
- Decision: PDF motoru Typst yerine Puppeteer tabanli browser-print yoluna alindi. Gerekce: Windows ortaminda Pandoc->Typst zincirinde image fetch/root davranisi dogrudan repo-gorsellerini istikrarsiz resolve etti; Node tabanli HTML->PDF adimi bu risk sinifini kaldirip deterministic cikti verdi.
- Rationale: Kullanici talebi tek ciltte genis ama ogrenci dostu, gorsel destekli ve dogrudan echOS kod referansli PDF idi. Kalici bir kitap hattinin tekrarli calisabilir olmasi, tek seferlik manuel export'tan daha oncelikliydi.
- Boundary: Bu pass Cilt 1 iskeletini, 14 haftalik ana anlatimi, algoritma atlasi ekini ve build otomasyonunu kapatir. Typst tabanli alternatif pipeline korunmadi; gerekirse ayri bir ikinci renderer lane olarak geri eklenebilir.
- Validation: `winget install --id JohnMacFarlane.Pandoc -e --source winget --scope user --accept-source-agreements --accept-package-agreements`; `winget install --id Typst.Typst -e --source winget --scope user --accept-source-agreements --accept-package-agreements`; `npm install --save-dev @mermaid-js/mermaid-cli`; `npm run diagrams`; `npm run build`.
