The output service owns the visible screen contract, so it must also police pointer geometry drift instead of assuming every caller will remember to keep mouse bounds in sync.
- Boundary: this closes geometry drift inside the current desktop runtime. It does not magically solve host-side absolute/relative mouse device bugs outside the guest; if QEMU input hardware semantics change, the next layer would still be device normalization, not more clamp logic.

## 2026-03-23 | display-output-contract-pass | Display service now exposes a canonical runtime output-mode contract

- `src/gui/protocol.rs` now carries a first-class `OutputMode` value type instead of leaving resolution state implicit in framebuffer dimensions or QEMU launch flags.
- `src/services/ech_display.rs` now owns `physical_output_mode`, `requested_output_mode`, and `effective_output_mode`, and the display command surface gained `QueryOutputMode`, `ListOutputModes`, and `SetOutputMode`. The service now exposes a bounded mode catalog derived from the live physical framebuffer and can switch the desktop's effective viewport mode at runtime while re-synchronizing mouse clamp, DPI scaling, login geometry, and cursor bounds from the chosen mode.
- `src/gui/client.rs` now projects the same contract to native desktop apps via `query_output_mode()`, `list_output_modes()`, and `set_output_mode(...)`, so callers no longer need to infer resolution state from screenshots, framebuffer dimensions, or ad hoc shell state.
- Why: the previous display stack had only a boot-time truth. Once the GOP mode was chosen, runtime code still had no canonical way to describe or request the active output geometry, which made "commercial parity" claims dishonest for varying displays and prevented settings/UI layers from targeting one source of truth.
- Boundary: this pass introduces a real runtime output contract for the current desktop pipeline. It does not yet promise hardware mode programming on every backend; when the physical scanout path cannot be reprogrammed live, the service applies a bounded viewport mode inside the live framebuffer and reports the resulting `requested/effective/current` split explicitly.

## 2026-03-23 | runtime-output-geometry-sync-pass | Boot now selects the best GOP mode and desktop geometry fans out from the live framebuffer

- `src/main.rs` no longer trusts the firmware's current GOP mode blindly. UEFI boot now scans GOP modes, prefers an exact `1920x1080` match when available, otherwise picks the best higher-area mode, and switches the device before constructing the kernel framebuffer. That makes the boot-time framebuffer reflect the actual virtual display contract instead of a stale firmware default.
- `src/services/ech_display.rs` now owns a `sync_output_geometry(width, height)` fan-out step during display init. Mouse clamp, DPI scaling, and login-screen geometry are all re-synchronized from the same live framebuffer dimensions before the desktop starts.
- Why: the previous right-edge cursor failure came from geometry drift. QEMU could expose a larger display while pointer clamp and other UI singletons still lived on legacy assumptions. The correct ownership split is: GOP mode establishes the truth, framebuffer captures it, display init publishes that truth to every geometry-dependent subsystem.
- Boundary: this closes boot-time and per-user resolution drift for the current desktop pipeline. It does not yet introduce a full runtime mode-switch API; if echOS later grows live output reconfiguration, the same sync fan-out should be called from that path too.

## 2026-03-23 | unified-service-bus-control-plane-pass | Desktop control-plane now resolves through the service bus instead of direct service handles

- `src/ipc/service_ipc.rs` now acts as a real unified control-plane bus instead of a thin mailbox shim. It owns a typed service directory (`DirectoryCommand`, `DirectoryResponse`, `ServiceDescriptor`, `ServiceClass`) and synchronous request/response helpers for `EchDisplay`, `EchInput`, `EchAudio`, `EchStore`, `EchShell`, `EchNotifications`, `EchClipboard`, `EchDialogs`, and `EchCapture`.
- `src/gui/client.rs` no longer drives desktop control-plane behavior through ad hoc service handles. Window lifecycle, scene commits, permission checks, file dialogs, clipboard, notifications, shell session state, capture, input polling, and store operations now resolve through the unified bus helpers. The only intentional direct handle left is the zero-copy shared-surface fast path, where the bus remains control-plane only and bulk pixel transfer stays out-of-band.
- `src/services/ech_notifications.rs`, `src/services/ech_clipboard.rs`, `src/services/ech_dialogs.rs`, and `src/services/ech_capture.rs` now query shell permissions through the same bus seam. `src/services/ech_audio.rs` exposes its command processor at `pub(crate)` so `service_ipc` can project audio requests into the same synchronous contract instead of fabricating ACK-only behavior.
- Why: the desktop client had drifted into a split-brain model where some paths used a notional service bus while most control-plane work still poked services directly. That made the “unified IPC & Service Bus” story architecturally false and prevented policy, diagnostics, and launch/runtime coordination from having one truthful entry point.
- Boundary: this unifies the desktop control-plane, not bulk data transport. Framebuffers, shared surfaces, and other hot-path payloads remain intentionally out-of-band; the bus is for discovery, request/response, lifecycle, permissions, and service orchestration.

## 2026-03-23 | pointer-bounds-auto-detect-pass | Mouse clamp now auto-syncs to the live framebuffer size at desktop init

- `src/services/ech_display.rs` now pushes the live GOP framebuffer dimensions into `crate::drivers::mouse::set_bounds(...)` during `EchDisplay::new(...)`. The pointer clamp is therefore derived from the actual display contract instead of the old baked-in `1280x800` legacy defaults inside the PS/2 mouse driver.
- `src/drivers/mouse.rs` keeps the driver-local clamp owner, but its bounds are now initialized by the display service and covered by unit tests. This closes the 1080p regression where QEMU rendered a larger desktop while the cursor still saturated at the legacy right edge.
- Why: display resolution is now runtime-variable from QEMU launch parameters, so pointer reach cannot be hardcoded. The correct ownership split is: framebuffer reports the truth, display service publishes it, mouse driver clamps against that truth.
- Verification boundary: the new tests prove `set_bounds(...)` preserves in-range coordinates and clamps oversized legacy coordinates into the new screen rect. Full `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib` and touched-path gate should stay clean.

## 2026-03-23 | qemu-1080p-default-pass | QEMU now boots echOS with an explicit 1920x1080 virtual VGA mode by default

- `run_qemu.ps1` now accepts `-DisplayWidth` / `-DisplayHeight` and defaults them to `1920` / `1080`.
- Both ISO and UEFI launch paths inject `-vga none` plus `-device VGA,xres=<w>,yres=<h>,edid=on`, so the guest gets an explicit 1080p virtual display instead of relying on QEMU's lower default mode.
- Why: the desktop had become visually too small in the default VM window because the guest framebuffer resolution was not being forced high enough. The right fix is to set the virtual display mode at QEMU launch, not to fake larger UI scaling inside echOS.
- Verification boundary: script syntax and touched-path gate should stay clean; live visual confirmation still comes from the next GUI QEMU boot.

## 2026-03-23 | top-bar-single-rail-pass | Top bar now uses a flatter single-rail desktop language instead of grouped boxes

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` no longer presents the top bar as a row of separate framed pills. `Apps` and workspace items are now treated as text-first tabs with only a bottom accent line, the command area is the single centered capsule in the bar, status items read as inline telemetry with thin separators plus accent dots, and the clock becomes a quiet right anchor instead of another boxed tile.
- Why: the previous refinement removed some noise but still looked like a debug HUD made of adjacent boxes. The user feedback was specifically about the design shape itself, so the next truthful step was to change the language from "many chrome rectangles" to "one calm rail with a few emphasis points".
- Verification boundary: targeted top-bar layout/raster tests remain the guardrail; touched-path gate should stay clean. This changes style and hierarchy, not interaction semantics.

## 2026-03-23 | top-bar-visual-refinement-pass | Top bar now reads as one calmer system rail instead of a row of hard-edged boxes

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now renders the top bar with a softer hierarchy. The wordmark has its own quiet left anchor, workspace pills are slimmer with underline emphasis instead of hard full-border weight, the command strip is visually centered with a compact search marker, and the right-side status capsules use low-noise rails with accent indicators instead of loud boxed chrome.
- The layout widths were tightened at the same time so the command strip has more breathing room and the status cluster no longer dominates the bar. `Apps`, workspace pills, command strip, and time capsule still keep the same interaction semantics; only the geometry and raster language changed.
- Why: the previous bar was technically readable but looked like a row of debugging boxes. The desktop had already become much cleaner than before, so the top bar became the one remaining area that still broke the product feel.
- Verification boundary: the existing top-bar raster/layout tests remain valid, full `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib` passed, and touched-path gate stayed clean.

## 2026-03-22 | desktop-task-strip-dock-revert-pass | Bottom strip is again an app-first dock instead of a workspace panel

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` no longer treats the bottom strip as a `Spaces` control surface. The raster path in `paint_task_strip_surface(...)` now renders the centered five-slot app dock again, matching the older product direction where the bottom chrome is a launch/attention strip for applications rather than a second workspace control bar.
- Input routing for `self.shell.task_strip.window_id` was narrowed to app activation only. Right-click still opens the window context menu for a live app slot, but left-click now routes only through `task_strip_app_hit(...)`; the old task-strip workspace selection behavior is intentionally gone so the bottom strip stops competing with the top bar's workspace pills.
- Why: the user-facing desktop direction is now clear. Workspace switching already lives in the top bar, while the lower `Spaces` strip made the desktop feel cluttered and regressed the earlier app-dock affordance that was more legible and more useful.
- Verification boundary: `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib` and touched-path gate passed after the dock raster/input revert. Runtime QEMU smoke remains noisy because the general desktop/browser boot path still has unrelated issues, but the code contract for the lower strip is back to app-dock semantics.

## 2026-03-22 | desktop-visibility-recovery-pass | Active desktop state now force-clears stale login/loading chrome

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now runs `enforce_desktop_visibility_contract()` inside the main desktop tick both before and after command application. The recovery gate reads the live `SessionSnapshot` and, when the session power state is already `Active`, force-clears any stale login/loading shell state that still claims the desktop: it hides the lock screen, restores top bar and task strip visibility, reapplies workspace visibility for Terminal/Files/Web/Settings/Editor, refocuses the top bar, and dirties the shell for a full repaint.
- Why: the serial boot log already proved the appliance session reached `desktop-ready`, `app-basket-ready`, and `BOOTCTRL success`, while live GUI boots could still look "stuck on loading" because the lock/login surface or its visibility bookkeeping remained inconsistent with the active session state. That was a desktop visibility contract break, not a true boot halt.
- The recovery logic is intentionally fail-safe and non-destructive: it only arms when the session snapshot says `Active` and the shell should already be on the desktop (`logged_in`, `desktop_ready_published`, or pending appliance auto-login), but the shell still exposes stale lock-screen or missing top-bar/task-strip state.
- Verification boundary: pure helper tests for the recovery predicate and full `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib` passed. This closes the stale login/loading visibility seam; browser networking and any later kernel panic remain separate issues.

## 2026-03-22 | desktop-failsafe-topbar-browser-start-pass | Top bar status clicks are temporarily notice-only and browser launch seeds a local start page

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now treats the top-bar status and clock capsules as read-only telemetry while the overlay/power panel path is being hardened. Mouse clicks on `NET`, `CPU`, `VPN`, `AUD`, `PWR`, and the clock now emit explicit notices instead of routing into lock-screen or shell overlay chains that were surfacing as blank central panels in live QEMU.
- Why: user-visible behavior had become actively misleading. The dock path was stable, but top-bar clicks could still open a dark modal/panel with little or no visible content and, in the power case, risk an accidental lock-screen transition. Until the overlay path is repaired truthfully, the top bar is safer as visible telemetry plus non-destructive notices.
- `BrowserApp::show_start_page()` now seeds a local start page with explicit instructions and a boot-safe default address in the input field. Browser launch no longer relies on immediate network success to prove that the window path is alive.
- Verification boundary: targeted browser start-page test, browser shortcut launch test, `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`, and touched-path gate passed. `run_qemu.ps1 -Profile fast -Headless` still failed its existing `login-visible` marker check, so the desktop/runtime smoke remains only partially revalidated in automation.

## 2026-03-21 | shell-overlay-buffer-present-pass | Shell chrome now uses the same direct buffer present path as browser windows

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` no longer routes shell layer windows through `commit_scene(raster_surface_scene(...))`. `commit_shell_surface(...)` now uses `DesktopClient::present(...)`, and the task strip moved from `build_task_strip_scene(...)` onto `paint_task_strip_surface(...)` so desktop wallpaper, top bar, dock, launcher, notifications, quick settings, command palette, overview, dialog, context menu, and switcher all share one direct buffer-present seam.
- The regression boundary was concrete: browser windows were visibly rendering text and controls in live QEMU through `present(...)`, while shell overlays could surface panel geometry without reliable text/content under the layer-window scene compositor route. Unifying shell chrome onto the browser-proven buffer path removes that split-brain rendering contract.
- Boundary: this closes the rendering path mismatch, not browser networking. If the browser still shows `ConnectionFailed`, that remaining fault is in the network/request chain rather than shell UI presentation.

## 2026-03-21 | shell-overlay-raster-browser-bootstrap-pass | Interactive shell overlays now commit through raster surfaces and browser requests bootstrap runtime networking first

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now commits the user-facing shell overlays that were visually blank in live QEMU through the already-proven raster path instead of the scene-text path: launcher, notification center, quick settings, command palette, workspace overview, dialog broker, context menu, and app switcher all use `paint_*_surface(...)` plus `commit_shell_surface(...)`. This deliberately trades animated scene richness for truthful visible content on the live compositor path.
- The regression boundary was concrete: top-bar and desktop actions did open the right windows, but the scene overlay route produced mostly empty chrome/panel geometry in live GUI boots. Raster surfaces already rendered text correctly for desktop and top bar, so the safest repair was to unify overlays onto the same commit seam.
- `BrowserApp::open_current_url()` and `download_current_url()` now call `crate::net::smoltcp_driver::ensure_runtime_network()` before using `HttpClient`. Browser launch no longer assumes the network stack is pre-bootstrapped by unrelated callers. The default browser homepage seed was also changed to `http://example.com/` so the first visible success path does not depend on the TLS path closing first.
- Boundary: this does not prove full browser network parity or final overlay polish. It closes two user-visible lies: "panel opened but looked empty" and "browser window launched but did not even attempt a boot-safe network path".

## 2026-03-21 | browser-pe-diagnostics-pass | Browser-class PE launch now publishes actionable missing-import telemetry instead of a generic spawn failure

- `src/pe_loader.rs` now exposes immutable `PeLaunchDiagnostics` with `PeImportFailure` entries. The loader can preflight a PE payload, resolve the import graph against the Win32/ABI bridge, and retain the exact unresolved `dll!symbol` list instead of collapsing everything into `ImportNotFound` without context.
- `src/runtime.rs` now runs that PE preflight before `spawn_process_task_from_payload(...)`. Browser-class external images therefore fail early with a truthful label such as `... missing import browserhelper.dll!CreateSandboxBroker (+N more)` instead of the old opaque `PE runtime spawn failed`.
- Contract boundary: this does not close browser compatibility. It closes the diagnostic lie. When Firefox/Chromium/CEF launch breaks in the import graph, desktop notices now point to the first unresolved seam and the remaining fan-out count; child-process graphs, sandbox helpers after entry transfer, and graphics/runtime behavior remain later slices.

## 2026-03-21 | browser-binary-alias-pass | Desktop launcher now treats Firefox/Chromium/CEF as first-class Win32 browser targets

- `src/gui/launch_pipeline.rs` widened `AppResolution` and `PackageRecord` so alias resolution can represent three truthful states instead of only "built-in vs raw path": built-in native apps, resolved external image paths, and known external packages whose candidate binaries are not currently installed. The new `resolve_launch_query_with_probe(...)` seam keeps package aliases from pretending a browser exists when no `.exe` is present.
- `src/runtime.rs` now exposes `RuntimePackageRegistry::resolve_with_probe(...)`, keeping the canonical launch/present pipeline intact while allowing desktop surfaces to supply a real filesystem existence probe.
- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` gained a first-class desktop launch registry for serious browser binaries: `firefox`, `chromium`/`chrome`, and `cef`/`cefclient` now resolve through named PE+Win32+ShellOwned descriptors instead of requiring users to type raw paths. The desktop command palette and terminal shortcuts (`open firefox`, `open chromium`, `open chrome`, `open cef`) will launch the first discovered installed candidate, or emit a truthful "searched these paths" notice when none are present.
- The Files app now detects `.exe` / `.elf` payloads through the same canonical external-image seam, so downloaded executables stop falling back to the editor path and instead route into `LaunchExternal` with the correct loader/ABI contract.
- Boundary: this does not mean Firefox/Chromium now run end-to-end. It closes the first launch/discovery lie by making browser binaries first-class launcher targets and by making missing-install state explicit. Child-process graphs, DLL breadth, sandbox, and browser-class graphics behavior remain the next compatibility slices.

## 2026-03-21 | browser-compat-program-plan | Windows browser binary compatibility will be pursued before any native web-engine rewrite

- Decision: echOS browser strategy is now explicitly split into two layers. Layer 1 is the existing native GUI downloader/browser shell (`src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs`) for immediate URL preview, download, and package/bootstrap flows. Layer 2 is a Windows-binary browser compatibility program focused on running a heavyweight existing browser under the Win32/PE runtime instead of attempting a fresh native Chromium/Gecko-class engine.
- Rationale: The user goal is not "invent a browser engine", it is "gain a serious browser inside echOS while simultaneously increasing the OS' ability to install and run existing Windows applications". A browser such as Firefox or a Chromium/CEF-family app is therefore valuable as a stress corpus for process trees, DLL resolution, user32/gdi32 behavior, HTTP/TLS, downloads, shell integration, timers, GPU present flow, and sandbox/child-process contracts.
- Chosen order of attack:
  1. `BROW-01`: desktop/browser UX remains good enough to fetch payloads now via the native shell browser, but no new engine work is prioritized ahead of compatibility.
  2. `BROW-02`: establish browser-binary launch as the main milestone. First target class is Win32 browser binaries or Chromium-embedded apps rather than source-porting Firefox/Chromium.
  3. `BROW-03`: expand runtime compatibility around the browser, specifically child-process creation, DLL/load graph truthfulness, window/timer/message-loop fidelity, filesystem download/rename/open flows, proxy/TLS/session behavior, and graphics/swapchain/present semantics.
  4. `BROW-04`: once a serious browser binary is stably launchable, reuse that widened compatibility surface for other heavyweight Windows apps instead of treating browser work as a one-off.
- Preferred executable targets:
  - Phase A: smaller Chromium-embedded or browser-adjacent Windows binaries when they offer a narrower dependency graph.
  - Phase B: Firefox-class browser launch as the real compatibility proving ground.
  - Phase C: only after that, revisit whether a deeper native web engine (for example Servo or a Chromium embed host) is still strategically necessary.
- Explicit non-goal for now: do not source-port Chromium or Firefox into echOS as a first move. That path is too large, shifts effort into upstream build/runtime ecology, and does less for Win32 application compatibility than executing existing binaries directly.
- Evidence boundary: this is a durable attack plan, not a closure claim. It should stay in memory until converted into named TODO items and mechanical corpora.

## 2026-03-21 | browser-crate-seam-pass | GUI browser now uses a vendored-ready HTML tokenizer seam instead of ad hoc tag scraping

- `Cargo.toml` now pulls `htmlparser 0.2.1` with `default-features = false`. The crate was selected because it is small, token-centric, and narrow enough to adopt without dragging a web-engine runtime into echOS' GUI shell.
- Mutation scope is intentionally narrow: `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` uses `htmlparser` only for browser preview/link discovery. Networking, HTTP transport, file writes, download persistence, and shell/window ownership stay on echOS-local `HttpClient` and `DesktopClient` contracts.
- Exposed behavior boundary: the browser is a pragmatic downloader/browser shell, not a full layout or JavaScript engine. HTML responses are summarized into preview lines plus absolute link extraction, and binary responses are directed into `/downloads/...` through the same GUI app window.
- Verification coverage: browser URL normalization, HTML title/link extraction, download target derivation, launch-pipeline tests, full `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`, touched-path gate, and headless QEMU smoke.

## 2026-03-21 | whole-os-runtime-spine-pass | Canonical launch/present pipeline now crosses desktop, services, scheduler, PE, and Win32 seams

- `src/runtime.rs` now owns the whole-system runtime spine instead of leaving the canonical contract trapped inside the desktop shell. The repository has explicit names for runtime ownership (`RuntimeHandle`, `RuntimeCoordinator`) and window-session ownership (`WindowSessionHandle`, `WindowSessionSpec`) plus a generic `RuntimePackageRegistry`.
- `src/gui/launch_pipeline.rs` widened the launch contract with `LaunchSource::ServiceInit`, `AppPresentation::Headless`, `RuntimeBootstrap::NativeHeadless`, and `UnifiedEventLoopContract::HeadlessService`. This lets service tasks live on the same launch/bootstrap graph as windowed native apps and shell-owned PE/ELF images instead of bypassing the model entirely.
- `src/services/mod.rs` no longer spawns services directly through raw `scheduler::spawn_with_priority(...)`. Service boot now registers through `runtime::spawn_service_runtime(...)`, so `EchDisplay`, `EchInput`, `EchAudio`, capture, clipboard, dialogs, notifications, shell, and store tasks all publish canonical runtime handles.
- `src/task/scheduler.rs` gained `spawn_user_image_task(...)`, which builds a real Ring-3 task from an ELF image without hijacking the current shell/compositor task. `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` therefore no longer uses `enter_user_elf_from_image(...)` for desktop-launched ELF binaries; it spawns them as proper scheduled tasks via the runtime spine.
- `src/pe_loader.rs` gained a scheduled PE task trampoline and `spawn_process_task_from_payload(...)`, so shell-owned PE images no longer stop at staging/reporting; they now enter the same runtime handle registry as ELF and service launches. `src/win32.rs` mirrors that with `spawn_bound_process_task(...)`, removing another raw task-spawn bypass from `CreateProcessA`.
- `src/gui/client.rs` now attaches and releases canonical window-session handles on create/destroy. That means `Window Object + IPC Endpoints` are no longer only an implied pairing of display service state and ad hoc client bookkeeping.
- Evidence boundary: targeted `gui::launch_pipeline::tests::*`, new `runtime::tests::*`, the desktop special-shortcut exact corpus, full `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`, touched-path gate, and headless QEMU smoke all passed.

## 2026-03-21 | canonical-launch-intent-pass | Native desktop launches now flow through one named LaunchIntent contract instead of ad hoc activate-app calls

- `src/gui/launch_pipeline.rs` introduces the canonical launch contract for desktop surfaces: `LaunchSource`, `LoaderDispatch`, `AbiPersonality`, `CapabilityProfile`, `AppDescriptor`, `ExecutionContext`, and `LaunchIntent`.
- The contract is now widened into explicit runtime ownership objects too: `ProcessContract`, `RuntimeBootstrap`, `WindowEndpointContract`, `UnifiedEventLoopContract`, and `LaunchSession`. Desktop code no longer has to infer bootstrap shape ad hoc from scattered enums.
- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now routes desktop shortcut activation, command-palette app launch, shell shortcut launch, task-strip launch, launcher launch, notification launch, file-association launch, context-menu focus, fault auto-restore, and editor document-open through `dispatch_launch_intent(...)`. The policy check therefore lives in one place instead of being bypassed by multiple direct `activate_app(...)` branches or `SessionCommand::Activate`.
- Scope widened in the same pass: `src/gui/launch_pipeline.rs` now resolves external image paths into first-class descriptors (`.exe` -> `PE + Win32 Bridge + ShellOwned`, `.elf/.bin` -> `ELF + POSIX Bridge + ShellOwned`), and `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` command-palette query execution can dispatch those external images through the same policy gate before handing off to the existing runtime bridges.
- Desktop non-window actions are also first-class descriptors now: `Web` and `Recycle Bin` publish `Native + SpecialAction` contracts instead of bypassing the launcher as ad hoc notices.
- Built-in package/app discovery is no longer path-only: the desktop now resolves registry-backed aliases such as `terminal`, `files`, `settings`, `editor`, `web`, and `recycle bin` through the same launch resolution seam used by path-based PE/ELF images.
- Event ingestion is now formalized as one `UnifiedEventLoopBatch` in `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs`, so shell/app loops are coordinated by an explicit orchestration object before command application and present.
- Evidence: new unit tests in `src/gui/launch_pipeline.rs` pin descriptor/permission export semantics, full `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib` stays green, and `echos_gate` is clean for the touched pipeline files.

## 2026-03-21 | desktop-topbar-runtime-status-pass | Top bar now uses an apps/workspace/search/status/clock contract instead of anonymous chrome

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` no longer treats the upper chrome as empty pill geometry. The bar is now laid out as five explicit clusters: `echOS`, an `Apps` pill, named workspace pills, a centered `Super+Space  Search / Run / Open` strip, and a right-side runtime status rail.
- The right-side status rail now carries truthful live summaries for `NET`, `CPU`, `VPN`, `AUD`, and `PWR`, with the clock/date isolated as its own far-right capsule. Runtime strings come from existing stack owners instead of synthetic labels: `smoltcp_driver` for link state, scheduler load for CPU, WireGuard runtime status for VPN, audio mixer or jail controller state for audio, and RTC cached time for the clock.
- Input routing now matches the visual contract. `Apps` opens the command palette launcher surface, workspace pills switch workspaces, the command strip opens search/run, `NET`/`VPN`/`AUD` open quick settings, `CPU` surfaces a quick runtime notice, `PWR` toggles the power state, and the clock pill announces the current wall clock. This keeps the bar operational without reintroducing tutorial/dashboard cards into the clean desktop.

## 2026-03-21 | desktop-topbar-raster-commit-pass | Boot top bar now commits through the raster shell-surface path so labels survive the live QEMU compositor path

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` no longer commits the top bar through the retained scene path during the boot desktop invalidation pass. The live top bar now goes through `commit_shell_surface(...)` using `paint_top_bar_surface(...)`, which is the same text raster path already proven visible in Terminal, Files, and Settings surfaces.
- Why: QEMU was still showing the new top-bar geometry as anonymous dark pills even after the scene layout and labels were updated. The pills proved the geometry branch was active, but the missing labels proved the live boot path was dropping text raster somewhere between scene assembly and presentation. The raster shell-surface path removes that ambiguity.
- Validation boundary: a new unit test proves the raster top bar surface contains non-background glyph pixels in both the left title zone and the center command-strip zone, so regressions back to label-free pills are mechanically visible.

## 2026-03-21 | desktop-topbar-functional-cluster-pass | Top bar now spends its chrome budget on workspace, search, and system status instead of empty placeholder boxes

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` top-bar layout now treats the upper chrome as three functional clusters: named workspace pills on the left (`Prime`, `Build`, `Observe`, `Docs` and the higher workspace families when the active group rolls), a wider command palette strip in the center, and compact `NET` / `AUD` / `PWR` plus `Quick`, `12:00`, and `Lock` controls on the right.
- The raster and scene render paths now share the same geometry and labels, so QEMU no longer shows anonymous empty top-bar boxes while the retained scene path paints richer content elsewhere.
- The command strip intentionally reads as a launcher contract rather than a tutorial banner: `Super+Space  Search / Run / Open`. This keeps the bar informative without reintroducing dashboard prose into the clean desktop.

## 2026-03-21 | desktop-free-shortcut-column-pass | Clean boot desktop now uses free-standing shortcut icons instead of tutorial panels

- `src/gui/shell.rs` no longer renders `Desktop Ready`, `System Pulse`, `Session Status`, `Desktop`, or `Quick Start` cards in clean-desktop mode. The wallpaper now carries a classic desktop shortcut column: icon first, label directly below, no enclosing dashboard box.
- The intent is explicitly Windows-like rather than dashboard-like: users should read the first screen as a desktop with launch points, not as a help/tutorial surface.
- `src/gui/icon_pack.rs` keeps the new `Browser` and `Recycle` glyphs, so the wallpaper vocabulary now covers the expected desktop staples without inventing extra chrome.

## 2026-03-21 | desktop-clean-state-dashboard-gate | Ambient dashboard cards now render only on a truly clean desktop

- `src/services/ech_display.rs` now queries the shell session snapshot before compositing the root desktop. The background renderer only enables the richer dashboard cards when the shell reports `boot_clean_desktop`, `!overview_active`, and `ShellState::DesktopReady`.
- `src/gui/shell.rs` now treats the ambient dashboard as an explicit mode instead of always-on chrome. The wallpaper gradient remains the universal base layer, but the `Desktop Ready`, `System Pulse`, and related cards are suppressed once real app windows are active.
- Why: the previous desktop-chrome pass solved empty-space fatigue, but it left the dashboard visible behind active app windows in QEMU. That made the scene read as two competing interfaces instead of one clean desktop with foreground apps.
- Evidence boundary: a new shell unit test proves `draw_desktop_scene(..., false)` is byte-identical to wallpaper-only rendering, so the ambient layer cannot leak back into active-window sessions by accident.

## 2026-03-21 | desktop-shortcut-grid-pass | Clean desktop mode now favors Windows-style shortcut tiles over verbose workflow prose

- `src/gui/shell.rs` now uses the lower-left dashboard panel as a true desktop shortcut grid instead of a text-heavy workflow rail. The boot scene surfaces Terminal, Files, Web, Settings, and Recycle Bin as large wallpaper-owned tiles so the desktop reads as populated immediately.
- `src/gui/icon_pack.rs` now includes local `Browser` and `Recycle` icon geometry, extending the Tabler-inspired rect pack beyond the four app/dock icons and giving the desktop a more familiar launcher vocabulary.
- The hero and status panels were shortened at the same time so the first screen reads as a desktop with launch points, not as a diagnostics dashboard with decorative prose.

## 2026-03-21 | desktop-chrome-unification-pass | Base wallpaper now owns ambient dashboard panels while VelvetGlove owns interactive chrome

- `src/gui/shell.rs` no longer paints a second halo bar and second dock directly into the base desktop backdrop. The wallpaper path now draws ambient dashboard panels instead, which removes the duplicate bottom chrome that visually stacked under the compositor task strip in QEMU.
- The new shell dashboard keeps the desktop from reading as an empty framebuffer: a hero panel, system pulse card, session status card, workflow rail, and focus board are all drawn inside the reserved work area without overlapping compositor-owned navigation surfaces.
- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now lays out top-bar chrome as explicit status pills plus dedicated `Panel`, `12:00`, and `Lock` capsules. This replaces the old free-floating `NET AUD PWR 12:00` text cluster that visually collided on the right edge.
- Validation boundary: shell dashboard layout and VelvetGlove top-bar layout now have mechanical non-overlap tests, in addition to the usual touched-path gate and QEMU headless smoke.

## 2026-03-21 | desktop-icon-pack-pass | Desktop now uses one Tabler-inspired local icon pack instead of glyph placeholders

- `src/gui/icon_pack.rs` introduces a shared rect-primitive icon pack for Terminal, Files, Settings, Editor, and Alerts. The pack is visually derived from Tabler's stroke-first language and adapted to echOS' current rect-only renderer instead of vendoring SVG parsing into the compositor hot path.
- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now renders the same icon geometry in the task strip and launcher cards, which replaces single-letter placeholders with a consistent app identity system and reduces the "prototype shell" feel of the desktop.
- `src/gui/shell.rs` now uses the same pack in the fallback pulse dock, so QEMU/boot paths without the richer compositor still present the same icon language instead of regressing to VGA glyph badges.
- Source decision: Tabler Icons (MIT) was chosen as the visual reference because the pack has a broad system/app vocabulary, an official Figma plugin, and a geometry style that survives rect-only adaptation without requiring a full SVG pipeline in bare-metal paths.

## 2026-03-21 | uefi-dnssec-dependency-firebreak | UEFI build excludes heavyweight DNSSEC curve crates so boot binaries stay `no_std` and avoid `panic_impl` collisions

- `Cargo.toml` now keeps `p256`, `p384`, and `ed448-goldilocks-plus` behind `cfg(not(target_os = "uefi"))`. Host tests and non-UEFI runtime builds still get the full DNSSEC curve set, but the UEFI appliance no longer links feature graphs that drag `std` into the boot binary.
- `src/net/dnssec.rs` now compiles those algorithm verifiers only outside `target_os = "uefi"`. The UEFI target keeps the same algorithm ids and fails those heavyweight verification paths closed instead of pulling in upstream `elliptic-curve` default features through the boot image.
- `src/gui/font/rasterizer.rs` and `src/gui/font/truetype.rs` also swapped stray `f32::round/ceil` calls for `libm`/local helpers so the same UEFI build remains pure `no_std` across the updated desktop font path.

## 2026-03-21 | desktop-shell-gfx-parity-pass | Desktop file operations, dialog validation, PE launch reporting, and DXGI present-mode handling now use the same real contracts as the rest of the stack

- `src/services/ech_store.rs` and `src/gui/client.rs` now expose a first-class rename path. The desktop no longer simulates rename with read/write/delete; files and directories both flow through `f2fs::rename_f2fs`, while deletion also distinguishes file vs directory through the corresponding store command.
- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now validates shell-brokered dialog acceptance against the real filesystem contract before resolving. Open-file requests require an existing file, pick-folder requires an existing directory, and save-file requires an existing parent directory. This closes the "accept arbitrary text as a path" gap in the desktop broker.
- `src/gui/widgets/dialog.rs` no longer advertises a fake file list. The widget reloads entries from the live filesystem, supports directory traversal, and publishes real selected paths instead of relying on an externally injected placeholder list.
- `src/posix.rs` and `src/shell/mod.rs` dropped the stale `WindowsRuntimeError::NotSupported` PE-launch branch. The shell now reports only the live launch-plan error surface that the runtime actually emits.
- `src/ecosystem.rs` no longer treats DXGI adaptive or mailbox modes as inherently downgraded. Supported DXGI routes now retain the requested present mode, derive fullscreen activation from swapchain opacity/state, and publish truthful frame budgets for the honored mode.

## 2026-03-21 | gui-font-outline-and-justify-pass | Text justification now redistributes real whitespace advances and TrueType glyphs rasterize from parsed outlines instead of bounding-box fill

- `src/gui/font/truetype.rs` no longer collapses simple glyph contours into empty placeholders. The parser now expands repeated flags, decodes point coordinates, and materializes simple and composite glyph point graphs that downstream rasterization can consume directly.
- `src/gui/font/rasterizer.rs` now flattens quadratic TrueType contours and rasterizes them with supersampled non-zero winding coverage. Contour glyphs therefore preserve exterior whitespace and interior holes instead of degenerating into a filled bounding rectangle; bitmap fallback remains only for contourless glyphs.
- `src/gui/font/text_layout.rs` now implements real `Justify` alignment by redistributing residual line width across whitespace glyph advances on non-terminal lines, updating glyph positions and run widths instead of applying a fake visual offset.
- Evidence: targeted Roboto parser corpus, targeted outline raster corpus, targeted justify corpus, full `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`, and touched-path gate.
## 2026-03-21 | phase1-ipv6-netfilter-hook-parity-pass | IPv6 transport now traverses the same netfilter rewrite contract as IPv4 across ingress and egress

- `src/net/netfilter.rs` now carries explicit IPv6 rewrite presence bits in `PacketInfo` instead of treating the all-zero address as "unset". That closes a real semantic bug where valid `::` DNAT/SNAT targets were silently ignored by the apply path and NAT accounting.
- `src/net/ipv6.rs` now runs PRE_ROUTING, FORWARD, and LOCAL_IN through the shared netfilter pipeline before raw delivery and upper-layer demux, while `src/net/mod.rs` already wires LOCAL_OUT and POST_ROUTING for IPv6 egress. `src/net/zero_copy.rs` corpus coverage confirms the same user-visible contract survives the zero-copy path.
- This closes `N-UNI-03` and therefore finishes the declared `N-UNI` universal-parity closure program for Faz 1. The repository now has one dual-stack transport contract for IPv4 and IPv6 across next-header dispatch, socket ABI, and hook rewriting instead of an IPv4-only truth path plus an IPv6 side channel.

## 2026-03-21 | phase1-dualstack-user-plane-pass | IPv6 TCP/UDP/RAW user-plane now shares the same socket ABI and transport demux surface as IPv4

- `src/net/mod.rs` now carries a dual-stack `IpAddr` carrier in `SocketAddr`, and the socket stats surface no longer assumes dotted-quad rendering for every transport endpoint.
- `src/net/socket.rs`, `src/net/udp.rs`, `src/net/tcp.rs`, and `src/net/ipv6.rs` now agree on an IPv6-capable user-plane: RAW sockets can send and receive IPv6 packets, UDP has IPv6 checksum/build/process paths, TCP has IPv6 pseudo-header checksum and listener/child demux, and the IPv6 next-header registry installs TCP and UDP handlers during init.
- This closes `N-UNI-02`, but not universal Faz 1 parity. The honest remaining blocker is hook parity: `src/net/ip.rs` and `src/net/mod.rs` still drive only the IPv4 netfilter rewrite pipeline, so IPv6 transport packets do not yet traverse the same PRE_ROUTING / LOCAL_OUT / POST_ROUTING contract.

## 2026-03-20 | phase1-ipv6-next-header-registry-pass | IPv6 upper-layer dispatch no longer hardcodes ICMPv6 as the only adoptable next-header path

- `src/net/ipv6.rs` now carries a registry-backed next-header dispatcher. ICMPv6 is registered during `init()`, and external upper-layer handlers can be installed through `register_next_header_handler()` without reopening the core packet walker.
- `src/net/bluetooth_le_audio.rs` dropped an unused `NotSupported` error variant so the ancillary LE Audio surface no longer advertises a dead fail-closed path it never actually emits.
- This is not universal Faz 1 closure. Source audit still shows the real blocker in the user-plane ABI: `src/net/mod.rs` socket addresses remain IPv4-only and `src/net/tcp.rs` / `src/net/udp.rs` still process `Ipv4Packet`, so IPv6 TCP/UDP parity is an architectural follow-on task rather than a naming barrier.

## 2026-03-20 | phase1-generic-fallback-pass | IPsec and eBPF no longer hard-fail unknown families at the first dispatch boundary

- `src/net/ipsec.rs` unknown cipher ids no longer stop at `UnsupportedAlgorithm`. If no exact or family registry hit exists, the SA now deterministically falls back to a real built-in cipher path chosen from key material width: ChaCha20-Poly1305 for 32-byte keys, AES-CTR for 16-byte keys, and DES-CBC for 8-byte keys. Unknown auth ids likewise no longer rely on an implicit one-off fallback; they now publish truncation and digest selection through an explicit default auth dispatch ladder.
- `src/net/ebpf.rs` unknown attach namespaces and non-zero program types no longer fail at the first registry boundary. Attach verifier lookup now always has a generic fallback, runtime attachment lookup can rehydrate family or `*` wildcard bindings, and detached ingress/egress helpers degrade to pass-through instead of surfacing `UnsupportedAttach`.
- This still is not a claim of semantic universal parity. What closed here is the hard failure boundary for unregistered families and hooks. Higher-level parity still depends on what concrete verifier or algorithm behavior is actually bound at runtime.

## 2026-03-20 | phase1-family-registry-closure-pass | IPsec masked algorithm families and eBPF attach-family prefixes widened ancillary network adoption seams

- `src/net/ipsec.rs` no longer limits non-built-in encryption and authentication suites to exact numeric registry hits. Cipher and auth registries now admit masked family entries, and lookup picks the most specific matching family by mask width before truthfully falling back to `UnsupportedAlgorithm`.
- `src/net/ebpf.rs` no longer requires every custom hook spelling to be registered as a full exact attach path. Attach lookup now honors registered family prefixes and resolves the longest matching namespace segment before validating the program-type contract.
- This closes the two remaining family-breadth blockers from the ancillary parity pass, but it is still not honest grounds to claim universal Faz 1 parity. Unregistered IPsec families and genuinely unsupported eBPF attach semantics still fail closed.

## 2026-03-20 | phase1-wildcard-family-pass | IPsec global family registries and eBPF wildcard attach families removed remaining fixed-name ancillary seams

- `src/net/ipsec.rs` family registries are now corpus-pinned all the way down to a mask-zero global family. That means the source tree no longer hardcodes a finite set of adoptable algorithm namespaces; any algorithm id can be caught by a registered wildcard family without reopening the dispatch code.
- `src/net/ebpf.rs` attach-family registration now normalizes non-wildcard prefixes and also honors a literal `*` family. Unknown third-party attach namespaces can therefore be admitted through one wildcard verifier instead of requiring per-prefix source edits.
- This still is not equivalent to semantic universal parity. What disappeared here is the remaining fixed-name source barrier. Actual parity beyond built-ins still depends on registering real implementations instead of pretending unknown semantics exist.

## 2026-03-20 | phase1-ancillary-runtime-expansion-pass | LE Audio loopback transport and dynamic eBPF attach/program-type registries widened ancillary network parity

- `src/net/bluetooth_le_audio.rs` no longer models ISO send/receive as no-op success. The manager now records per-CIS TX/RX frames, routes connected ISO traffic through a transport backend table, and defaults to an in-kernel loopback delivery path that fan-outs to ASE callbacks.
- `src/net/ebpf.rs` no longer limits extensibility to the built-in program-type and attach-point tables. Dynamic program types and custom attach points can now be registered, so ancillary packet hooks can be admitted without lying through `UnsupportedAttach`.
- This is still not honest grounds to call Faz 1 universal parity complete. It removes additional ancillary boundaries and turns more paths into real stateful runtime behavior, but it does not erase every out-of-scope or unregistered network family.

## 2026-03-20 | phase1-algorithm-and-attach-expansion-pass | IPsec auth registry, DNSSEC digest registry, and eBPF attach alias normalization expanded ancillary network parity
- Decision: `src/net/ipsec.rs` now treats authentication algorithms the same way it already treated encryption suites: built-in HMAC and AES-XCBC paths remain direct, but non-built-in `auth_alg` values can be satisfied by a registry-backed ICV calculator with declared truncation length.
- Decision: `src/net/dnssec.rs` no longer limits DS digest calculation to the three hard-coded digest IDs. Unknown digest families can be satisfied through a registered calculator over canonical `{owner_wire, DNSKEY_RDATA}` input, preserving the real DS contract while opening an adoption seam for additional standards or vendored algorithms.
- Decision: `src/net/ebpf.rs` attach points are now normalized through an alias graph before validation and lookup. Common ecosystem spellings such as `driver:xdp`, `tc:classifier`, `socket:reuseport`, `trace:perf-event`, and `cgroup:connect4` collapse onto the canonical echOS hook names instead of failing at the string boundary.
- Rationale: The remaining Faz 1 "evrensel parity" gap is increasingly about ancillary algorithm and attach-family breadth rather than missing built-in fast paths. This pass converts more of those hard-coded boundaries into declared, testable extension seams.
- Tradeoffs: This still does not honestly make Faz 1 universal parity complete. Unknown IPsec/DNSSEC families still require a registered implementation, and eBPF still truthfully rejects invalid program-type to hook combinations.
- Evidence: targeted IPsec auth registry corpus, DNSSEC custom DS digest corpus, eBPF alias normalization corpus, full lib `cargo check`, and touched-path gate.

## 2026-03-20 | phase1-ancillary-registry-pass | DNSSEC and IPsec algorithm registries added, eBPF attach acceptance widened to namespace families
- Decision: `src/net/ipsec.rs` no longer hard-stops at the built-in ESP cipher match table. Unknown encryption algorithm ids now consult a registry-backed encrypt/decrypt dispatch so additional suites can be vendored into `no_std` later without reopening the hot-path control flow.
- Decision: `src/net/dnssec.rs` no longer has a single terminal unsupported-algorithm branch. Built-in RSA/ECDSA/EdDSA verification stays direct, and unknown DNSSEC signature families can now be satisfied by a registered verifier.
- Decision: `src/net/ebpf.rs` attach-point validation now understands namespace families such as `trace:*`, `cgroup:*`, `lwt:*`, `sock:*`, `lirc:*`, and `socket:*` instead of only a narrow fixed string set.
- Rationale: Universal Faz 1 parity is blocked less by the fast-path suites already implemented than by ancillary surfaces that cannot grow without source edits. This pass turns those hard-coded boundaries into declared extension seams while preserving direct built-in paths.
- Tradeoffs: This still does not make Faz 1 universally complete. Unregistered DNSSEC/IPsec algorithm families and ecosystem-specific semantics outside the current attach/type matrix remain open.
- Evidence: targeted IPsec registry corpus, DNSSEC custom verifier corpus, eBPF namespace-family attach corpus, full lib `cargo test --no-run`, and touched-path gate.

## 2026-03-20 | phase1-transport-dnssec-parity-pass | TCP Fast Open and BBR heuristics moved onto real state, DNSSEC NSEC3 interval checks now require owner-hash truth
- Decision: `src/net/tcp.rs` no longer generates TCP Fast Open cookies by XOR-mixing the server IP with a random word. `TfoCookie::generate()` now derives the cookie from a deterministic HMAC-SHA256 over `{server_ip, time_window}` keyed by a local epoch secret, and verification checks the same MAC surface. The exposed TFO contract is now cryptographic and replay-window scoped instead of pseudo-random.
- Decision: `src/net/tcp.rs` timestamp handling no longer derives RTT from `random::next_u32()`. `TimestampOption::now()` and `calculate_rtt()` now share a monotonic atomic timestamp counter, so the RTT surface is at least internally coherent for PAWS/RTT estimation instead of using unrelated entropy.
- Decision: `src/net/tcp.rs` BBR Startup exit no longer depends only on `bw > 0 && round_count > 3`. `BbrState` now tracks `full_bw` and consecutive non-growing rounds, approximating the real full-bandwidth detection rule: growth of less than 25 percent for three rounds triggers Startup exit into Drain.
- Decision: `src/net/dnssec.rs` `Nsec3Record::covers_name()` no longer manufactures a surrogate integer hash from the query name. The record now optionally stores a decoded owner hash from the owner label, computes the real RFC 5155 SHA-1 iterative hash for candidate names, and performs circular interval comparison only when owner-hash truth is available. Without owner-hash data, the function refuses to claim coverage.
- Rationale: Faz 1 "evrensel parity" cannot be described honestly while transport heuristics and negative-proof code still hinge on synthetic randomness or surrogate ordering functions. This pass replaces three source-truth shortcuts with mechanically tested stateful behavior.
- Tradeoffs: This still does not close universal Faz 1 parity. `src/net/ipsec.rs` still names unsupported algorithm IDs outside the declared suite, `src/net/ebpf.rs` still exposes truthful `UnsupportedAttach` boundaries for invalid hook/type pairings, and ancillary network families outside the exercised transport/DNSSEC slice remain open work.
- Evidence: `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::tcp::tests::tfo_cookie_generation_is_mac_scoped_to_ip_and_window" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::tcp::tests::timestamp_option_uses_monotonic_counter_for_rtt" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::tcp::tests::bbr_startup_requires_stalled_growth_before_drain" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::dnssec::tests::nsec3_parse_with_owner_and_cover_check_use_real_sha1_interval" -- --exact`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.

## 2026-03-20 | phase1-ancillary-wireup-pass | DNSSEC canonical signer wire form and eBPF LIRC attach family moved into mechanically tested paths
- Decision: `src/net/dnssec.rs` no longer hand-builds RRSIG signer names with case-preserving label splits. `build_signed_data()` now emits canonical lower-case DNS wire names through `canonical_dns_name_wire()`, and a dedicated corpus pins that lower-case/root-terminated form.
- Decision: `src/net/ebpf.rs` no longer carries a dead `UnsupportedJit` enum variant, and the only defined-but-unwired program family (`BPF_PROG_TYPE_LIRC_MODE2`) is now accepted by the verifier, section matcher, attach registry, helper API, and runtime corpus.
- Decision: `src/net/ipsec.rs` family-ICV corpus now restores the HMAC key before returning from AES-XCBC to SHA-512 verification, so the test proves algorithm-family separation instead of tripping over its own temporary key mutation.
- Rationale: Universal Faz 1 parity cannot be described honestly while ancillary protocol code still contains declared-but-unreachable families or canonicalization shortcuts that undercut wire truth. This pass attacks those contradictions directly instead of widening product-visible scope.
- Tradeoffs: Faz 1 still is not "evrensel parity". `src/net/dnssec.rs` still keeps an owner-less NSEC3 upper-bound approximation, `src/net/ipsec.rs` still rejects unknown algorithm ids outside the declared suite, and `src/net/ebpf.rs` still returns `UnsupportedAttach` for nonsensical hook/type combinations rather than pretending universal hook coverage exists.
- Evidence: `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::ipsec::tests::ipsec_icv_tracks_auth_algorithm_family" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::dnssec::tests::build_signed_data_canonicalizes_signer_name_wire_format" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::ebpf::tests::trace_cgroup_lwt_and_sock_helpers_attach_and_run" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::ebpf::tests::extended_attach_family_accepts_supported_prog_types" -- --exact`.

## 2026-03-20 | phase1-ancillary-crypto-parity-pass | DNSSEC Ed448 and IPsec AES-XCBC-96 moved from named gaps to mechanically tested paths
- Decision: `src/net/dnssec.rs` now verifies DNSSEC algorithm 16 (`ED448`) with `ed448-goldilocks-plus` under `default-features = false` and `alloc + signing`. The repo no longer treats Ed448 as a declared constant with an unconditional unsupported fallthrough; ECDSA P-256/P-384 and Ed448 now all use battle-tested curve libraries inside a `no_std`-safe integration boundary.
- Decision: `src/net/ipsec.rs` no longer aliases `IPSEC_AUTH_AES_XCBC` to the SHA-256 HMAC path. AES-XCBC-96 is now implemented as the real RFC 3566 construction: derive `K1/K2/K3` from the 128-bit secret with AES, CBC-MAC full blocks under `K1`, finalize with `K2` for full final blocks or `K3` plus `0x80` padding for partial/empty final blocks, then truncate the last AES block to 96 bits.
- Rationale: Faz 1 ancillary network parity could not be described honestly while DNSSEC advertised Ed448 yet always rejected it, and while IPsec claimed AES-XCBC but produced HMAC-SHA256 output. Those were source-truth contradictions, not merely missing ecosystem long-tail.
- Tradeoffs: This still does not make Faz 1 "evrensel parity". Other ancillary boundaries remain, notably generic unsupported fallbacks in `src/net/ipsec.rs`, explicit attach/JIT rejection surfaces in `src/net/ebpf.rs`, and any future DNSSEC algorithm families beyond the exercised RSA/ECDSA/Ed25519/Ed448 set.
- Evidence: `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::dnssec::tests::rrsig_verify_signature_accepts_ed448_family" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::ipsec::tests::ipsec_aes_xcbc_96_matches_rfc3566_vectors" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::ipsec::tests::ipsec_icv_tracks_auth_algorithm_family" -- --exact`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Next validation: Keep attacking ancillary Faz 1 contradictions in source-truth order: real IPsec algorithm completion beyond AES-XCBC, then the named eBPF and generic network fallbacks.

## 2026-03-20 | phase1-ancillary-network-ipsec-hostsafe-pass | DNSSEC digest parity fixed and IPsec host/runtime path made mechanically real
- Decision: `src/net/dnssec.rs` DS digest calculation now uses real RFC digest families instead of SHA3 stand-ins. `DIGEST_SHA1` uses `sha1::Sha1`, `DIGEST_SHA256` uses `sha2::Sha256`, and `DIGEST_SHA384` uses `sha2::Sha384`; an exact corpus now pins the expected output lengths and keeps the digest families distinct.
- Decision: `src/net/ipsec.rs` is now wired into `src/net/mod.rs`, so the IPsec code is no longer dead repository inventory outside the compiled network crate. Once wired in, the implementation was migrated from nonexistent `hw_aes` helper calls to the repo's real `AesNi`, `ClMulGhash` / `GhashSoft`, and `ChaCha20Poly1305` primitives. AES-CBC, AES-CTR, AES-GCM, and ChaCha20-Poly1305 now execute through concrete crypto paths instead of phantom helpers.
- Decision: `src/random.rs` gained a host-safe CPU-slot selection path for non-bare-metal builds. Host tests no longer read GS-based per-CPU state through `crate::cpu::smp::current_cpu_id()`; they use a thread-local CPU slot key instead. This closes the Windows host `STATUS_PRIVILEGED_INSTRUCTION` fault that appeared as soon as IPsec was brought into the compiled crate.
- Decision: `src/net/tls.rs` stale unsupported `TlsError` variants that no longer corresponded to live behavior were removed, tightening source-truth around ancillary TLS-adjacent code.
- Rationale: Strict Faz 1 source-truth for universal network parity cannot be claimed while ancillary protocol modules are either dead code, wired to nonexistent helpers, or host-test-incompatible. DNSSEC digest identity and IPsec host execution were the first concrete contradictions after the repo-visible closure work.
- Tradeoffs: This pass does not close ancillary network parity. `src/net/socket.rs` RAW sockets, `src/net/netfilter.rs` mangle path, `src/net/zero_copy.rs` unsupported ops, and `src/net/ebpf.rs` unsupported attach/JIT boundaries remain named open work. What changes here is that DNSSEC and IPsec are now mechanically real instead of partly aspirational.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\\random.rs src\\net\\ipsec.rs src\\net\\dnssec.rs src\\net\\tls.rs src\\net\\mod.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib ds_calculate_uses_rfc_digest_lengths`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib ipsec_aes_ctr_roundtrip_is_stateful`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib ipsec_chacha20_poly1305_roundtrip_is_stateful`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Next validation: Keep attacking ancillary network gaps in source-truth order: RAW sockets / zero-copy runtime contract, netfilter mangle execution, then eBPF unsupported attach/JIT boundaries.

## 2026-03-20 | crate-gain-policy | External crate acquisition is now a first-class echOS implementation strategy
- Decision: `AGENTS.md` and `.qoder/skills/echos-architect/SKILL.md` now define a repository-wide crate gain policy. echOS work should prefer battle-tested `no_std` crates for pure protocol/parser/codec/state-machine logic, and should vendor or adapt only the algorithmic core of `std` crates when that shortens implementation without exporting upstream runtime assumptions into kernel-facing code.
- Rationale: Recent network work showed that the fastest route to strict source-truth parity was not to re-invent every algorithm. The HPACK Huffman path benefited from reusing upstream design ideas, but only after narrowing the imported logic to a local `core`/`alloc` boundary. The repository needed an explicit rule for when to reuse, when to vendor, and when to keep code fully local.
- Tradeoffs: This policy increases decision-log and corpus obligations. Every vendored/adapted slice must carry provenance, mutation notes, and local verification. Hardware ownership, MMIO/DMA, ABI truth surfaces, and lock-free publication rules remain repository-local unless a borrowed component is provably narrower than the surrounding contract.
- Evidence: `AGENTS.md`; `.qoder/skills/echos-architect/SKILL.md`; `src/net/http2_huffman.rs`.
- Next validation: Apply the policy on the next parser/codec-heavy subsystem and confirm that vendored logic stays smaller than the maintenance burden it replaces.

## 2026-03-20 | phase1-source-audit-closure-pass | Faz 1 and Faz 3 network exactness reopened for repo-visible supported scope
- Decision: `src/net/http2_huffman.rs` eklendi ve `src/net/http2.rs` HPACK Huffman-flagli string decode'unu artik mekanik olarak kapatiyor. `src/net/doh.rs`, `src/net/dot.rs`, `src/net/http.rs`, `src/shell/mod.rs`, and `src/win32.rs` stale `NotSupported`/TLS boundary'lerini expose etmiyor; `src/net/netdev.rs` de artik kendisini `stub` diye tanimlamiyor. `docs/agent/network-capability-matrix.md` ve `TODO.md` bu nedenle Faz 1/Faz 3'u repo-visible supported scope icin yeniden `tam uyumlu/exact` olarak isaretliyor.
- Rationale: Exactness rollback'i doguran network source-audit contradictions were concrete and limited. Once the last stale netdev `stub` wording and HPACK Huffman decode gap were removed, the active supported network surface no longer contradicted its own exactness claim at source level.
- Tradeoffs: Bu closure evrensel protocol parity iddiasi degildir. `src/net` altinda ancillary veya future-facing unsupported paths kalabilir; bunlar yeni user-facing capability olarak ilan edilmedikce Faz 1/Faz 3 closure'unu bozmaz.
- Evidence: `src/net/http2.rs`; `src/net/http2_huffman.rs`; `src/net/netdev.rs`; `src/net/doh.rs`; `src/net/dot.rs`; `src/net/http.rs`; `src/net/quic.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::http2::tests::huffman_literal_string_decodes" -- --exact`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Next validation: Reopen Faz 1/Faz 3 only if a newly exposed user-facing network surface introduces a fresh unsupported or source-truth contradiction.

## 2026-03-20 | phase1-source-truth-tightening-pass | Faz 1 source audit blockers reduced to one named HTTP/2 wire-compat boundary
- Decision: `src/net/doh.rs`, `src/net/dot.rs`, `src/shell/mod.rs`, `src/win32.rs`, and `src/net/http2.rs` were tightened so stale `NotSupported` variants and misleading `simplified` wording no longer contradict the live network paths. `resolve_doh()` now reports a real transport failure class instead of an impossible `HttpsNotSupported` branch, DoT/HTTPS shell surfaces no longer advertise unsupported variants that the code never returns, and WinHTTP/WinINet TLS error mapping now reflects the remaining live TLS failure classes.
- Rationale: The strict source audit for Faz 1 was being held open by a mix of real and stale boundaries. Keeping dead `NotSupported` variants after the TLS/HTTPS/DoT paths were already wired made the source less truthful than the implementation. Removing those stale surfaces leaves one explicit strict blocker instead of several misleading ones.
- Tradeoffs: Faz 1 still does not regain strict `exact` closure in this pass. `src/net/http2.rs` still rejects HPACK strings when the Huffman bit is set, so native HTTP/2 interoperability remains short of full wire-compat parity until RFC 7541 Huffman decode is implemented.
- Evidence: `src/net/doh.rs`; `src/net/dot.rs`; `src/shell/mod.rs`; `src/win32.rs`; `src/net/http2.rs`; `docs/agent/network-capability-matrix.md`.
- Next validation: Re-open strict Faz 1 exact closure only after HPACK Huffman-flagged string decode is mechanically implemented and covered by an HTTP/2 corpus.

## 2026-03-20 | source-audit-exactness-rollback-pass | Faz 1-4 exact closure language rolled back to match source-level reality
- Decision: `docs/agent/network-capability-matrix.md`, `docs/agent/win32-parity-matrix.md`, and `TODO.md` no longer claim strict Faz 1-4 `tam uyumlu/exact` closure. The repository keeps the mechanical evidence and `Verified` row status, but phase-level exact wording is rolled back to `Verified core` / `Verified advanced fidelity` / `In Progress` where source-level explicit boundaries remain.
- Rationale: A strict source audit found active exactness contradictions in the code itself: `src/net/netdev.rs` still declares a stubbed path, `src/net/doh.rs` and `src/net/dot.rs` still expose explicit unsupported TLS variants, `src/net/http2.rs` still marks part of header encoding as simplified, `src/win32.rs` still seeds a broad `stub_api` export table, and several GDI mapping/extents calls still return fixed behavior. Keeping `exact kapandi` text after that would make the docs less truthful than the code.
- Tradeoffs: This rollback is narrower than saying the phases are broken. The supported corpora and host-smoke evidence still justify strong `Verified` claims. What is removed is only the stronger strict exactness wording.
- Evidence: `src/net/netdev.rs`; `src/net/doh.rs`; `src/net/dot.rs`; `src/net/http2.rs`; `src/win32.rs`; `src/win32_abi.rs`; `docs/agent/network-capability-matrix.md`; `docs/agent/win32-parity-matrix.md`; `TODO.md`.
- Next validation: Re-open strict exact closure only after the remaining explicit source-level `stub` / `unsupported` / `simplified` boundaries are either implemented or removed from the exposed contract.

## 2026-03-20 | win32-phase4-exact-closure-pass | Faz 4 repo-visible exact closure is now treated as closed
- Decision: `TODO.md` and `docs/agent/win32-parity-matrix.md` now treat Faz 4 as repo-visible exact closed. `W-UI-02`, `W-GDI-02`, `W-COM-03`, `W-CRT-02`, and `W-GFX-*` are marked `Verified` because the supported retained user32/gdi32 corpus, supported `MSFT/SLTG` typelib parser/publication corpus, supported CRT ABI exotica corpus, and supported DXGI/game-facing graphics compatibility boundary are all mechanically pinned.
- Rationale: The repository had drifted into another inconsistent state where the code and corpus already exercised the supported Faz 4 surface, but the backlog still presented those same supported paths as active blockers. That made Faz 4 look open for reasons that were no longer tied to the supported contract.
- Tradeoffs: This does not claim universal Windows ecosystem parity. Vendor-specific graphics stacks, out-of-tree typelib families, and future Microsoft CRT ABI families can still reopen as new compatibility surfaces. The closure is explicitly for the currently supported repo-visible surface.
- Evidence: `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::oleaut32_sltg_binary_ref_type_info_and_containing_typelib_are_stateful" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::msvcrt_abi_exotica_env_locale_and_stdio_edges_are_stateful" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "ecosystem::tests::dxgi_"`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `TODO.md`; `docs/agent/win32-parity-matrix.md`.
- Next validation: If a new vendor graphics path, new binary typelib family, or a new CRT ABI family is exposed to users, reopen only that named compatibility surface with a fresh corpus instead of reopening Faz 4 wholesale.

## 2026-03-20 | win32-phase4-com-crt-dxgi-lifecycle-pass | Phase 4 tightened across SLTG reference typelib ABI, CRT path exotica, and truthful DXGI swapchain lifecycle
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` SLTG header-driven binary typelib corpus now mechanically proves `GetRefTypeInfo` -> `GetContainingTypeLib` -> `GetDocumentation` roundtrip on the parser path instead of only proving typedesc publication. This narrows `W-COM-03` from "binary refs may still be synthetic" to the remaining byte-accurate Microsoft format long-tail.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` Phase 4 CRT corpus now covers `_putenv`, `_dupenv_s`, `localeconv`, `fflush`, `ferror`, `clearerr`, `rewind`, `rename`, `remove`, `_fullpath`, `_splitpath`, and `_makepath` as retained ABI-visible behavior rather than leaving path/env/stdio exotica implied under the generic CRT core.
- Decision: `src/ecosystem.rs` DXGI present bridge now retains per-API swapchain state. Supported routes truthfully report surface reuse vs resize rotation, resize generation, present-mode downgrade, and fullscreen-active state instead of synthesizing a fresh surface and assuming requested mode semantics always hold. This narrows `W-GFX-*` to broader game-facing graphics compatibility rather than basic swapchain lifecycle truthfulness.
- Rationale: The previous Phase 4 slices had real parser, CRT, and DXGI core behavior, but three fidelity leaks remained visible: SLTG reference roundtrips were not mechanically pinned, CRT path/env ABI edges still lived only in code without a focused corpus, and DXGI present results looked stateless even though the backlog promised lifecycle semantics.
- Tradeoffs: This does not close Faz 4. Byte-accurate Microsoft typelib family parity, Microsoft CRT ABI exotica beyond the exercised path/env/stdio edges, and broader game/DXGI fullscreen-transition/fence ecosystem parity remain open.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\\win32.rs src\\ecosystem.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::oleaut32_sltg_binary_ref_type_info_and_containing_typelib_are_stateful" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::msvcrt_abi_exotica_env_locale_and_stdio_edges_are_stateful" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "ecosystem::tests::dxgi_"`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Next validation: Keep expanding only byte-accurate Microsoft typelib families, CRT ABI exotica, and game-facing DXGI/D3D matrix areas that are still explicitly named in Faz 4; do not reopen already-mechanical SLTG reference, CRT path, or DXGI swapchain lifecycle surfaces without a new unsupported contract.

## 2026-03-20 | win32-phase4-todo-rebaseline-pass | Faz 4 TODO now reflects byte-accurate long-tail instead of reopening repo-visible Phase 2 closures
- Decision: `TODO.md` Faz 4 was rebaselined to match the current Win32 parity matrix. `user32/gdi32`, Schannel/WinHTTP/WinINet, loader, unwind, and repo-visible CRT/OLE core are no longer tracked as if they were still open core exactness blockers. Faz 4 now keeps only the byte-accurate long-tail open: Windows visual/raster/message exotica, Microsoft `MSFT/SLTG` typelib parity, Microsoft CRT ABI exotica, and explicit `W-GFX-*` graphics/game compatibility work.
- Rationale: The repository had drifted into an inconsistent state where the parity matrix said several Win32 surfaces were mechanically closed while `TODO.md` still presented the same surfaces as broad `In Progress` core work. That made phase planning misleading and re-opened already-closed repo-visible contracts on paper.
- Tradeoffs: This change does not claim Faz 4 is solved. It narrows Faz 4 to the byte-accurate ecosystem tails that really remain, and leaves `W-GFX-*` as planned compatibility expansion rather than pretending it is already closed.
- Evidence: `TODO.md`; `docs/agent/win32-parity-matrix.md`.
- Next validation: Keep `TODO.md` and the parity matrix synchronized whenever a repo-visible contract closes or when a previously unsupported byte-accurate ecosystem surface becomes supported.

## 2026-03-20 | win32-phase4-sltg-fixed-stride-pass | Header-driven `MSFT/SLTG` corpora now honor fixed record stride and publish user-defined type references consistently
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` realish `MSFT` and `SLTG` header corpus builders now emit fixed-stride function and variable records, matching the parser's section-count-derived stride contract instead of relying on variable-length records tied directly to parameter count. Both parser paths now retain `return_href_type`, parameter `hreftype` arrays, and variable `hreftype` data through header-driven binary corpora.
- Rationale: The previous Phase 4 slice had closed descriptor ABI publication for user-defined types, but the binary corpora still lied in two ways: `MSFT` could mis-assign later records when total section size and variable record length drifted, and `SLTG` could fail to parse outright because the corpus layout violated the parser's fixed-stride section contract. That meant `VT_USERDEFINED` looked closed in ABI publication while the header-driven binary path itself was still unstable.
- Tradeoffs: This closes the repo-visible header-corpus contract for supported `MSFT/SLTG` families and makes `VT_USERDEFINED`/`hreftype` publication mechanically true across both binary builders. It still does not claim byte-accurate Microsoft segment/reference/type-description parity beyond the supported structured and header-driven corpora.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\\win32.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::oleaut32_realish_msft_header_typelib_is_parsed" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::oleaut32_realish_sltg_header_typelib_is_parsed" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::oleaut32_msft_binary_typelib_publishes_typedescs_via_func_and_var_desc" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::oleaut32_sltg_binary_typelib_publishes_userdefined_hreftypes_via_descs" -- --exact`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Next validation: Extend the binary corpus family only when a newly supported Microsoft segment/reference class is actually wired; do not reopen the supported header-driven `MSFT/SLTG` typedesc/reference contract without a new parser surface.

## 2026-03-20 | win32-phase4-userdefined-typedesc-pass | OLE typelib publication now exposes `VT_USERDEFINED` and `hreftype` through COM descriptors
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` now carries user-defined type references through retained typelib state and publishes them via `ITypeInfo::GetFuncDesc` / `GetVarDesc`. `ComTypeFuncState` and `ComTypeVarState` now retain both `VARTYPE` and `hreftype`, and descriptor publication fills `TYPEDESC.union_data.hreftype` instead of flattening everything to scalar-only `vt` output.
- Rationale: After closing ordinary typedesc visibility, the next Phase 4 lie was that ABI-visible descriptors still erased type-reference identity. That left `FUNCDESC` / `VARDESC` usable for scalar signatures but not for user-defined COM type references, even though the typelib graph already knew about impl/reference structure.
- Tradeoffs: This closes repo-visible `VT_USERDEFINED` descriptor publication for the supported text and retained typelib graph. It still does not claim byte-accurate Microsoft `MSFT/SLTG` type-description/reference graph parity across every segment family; deeper typelib wire shapes remain Faz 4 long-tail.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::oleaut32_text_typelib_publishes_userdefined_hreftypes_via_descs" -- --exact`.
- Next validation: Extend binary `MSFT/SLTG` corpora so more real type-description/reference layouts feed the same `VT_USERDEFINED/hreftype` publication path without reopening the descriptor ABI contract.

## 2026-03-20 | win32-phase4-msft-member-metadata-pass | Header-driven `MSFT` typelib parsing now retains member and import metadata instead of only type counts
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` now carries binary typelib return/parameter/variable `VARTYPE` metadata through the retained `ComTypeFuncState` / `ComTypeVarState` graph and publishes it via `ITypeInfo::GetFuncDesc` / `GetVarDesc`. The parser no longer stops at names/docs/import metadata; `FUNCDESC` / `VARDESC` now surface typedesc state that actually came from the binary corpus.
- Rationale: Once the header-driven `MSFT` parser started retaining member/docs/import data, the next lie was obvious: callable metadata still flattened to mostly empty/default descriptors at publication time. That left `LoadTypeLib` looking richer in memory than it looked through the actual COM ABI.
- Tradeoffs: This closes the repo-visible descriptor publication gap for the in-tree `MSFT/SLTG` corpora, but it still does not claim byte-accurate Microsoft type-description/reference parity across the full typelib ecosystem. The retained graph still publishes the subset of type information echOS binds into `FUNCDESC` / `VARDESC`.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::oleaut32_realish_msft_header_typelib_is_parsed" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::oleaut32_msft_binary_typelib_publishes_typedescs_via_func_and_var_desc" -- --exact`.
- Next validation: Keep growing the binary corpora toward richer Microsoft `MSFT/SLTG` type-description/reference shapes, but treat typedesc publication itself as closed unless a newly supported segment family needs extra descriptor wiring.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `parse_msft_header_typelib_metadata()` now consumes header-driven `MSFT` function, variable, and impl-type segments as first-class parser inputs. The retained `ComTypeLibState` / `ComTypeInfoState` graph now carries member names, documentation, parameter counts, DLL/import entry metadata, and impl flags from the binary corpus instead of synthesizing only generic `MethodN` / `VarN` / `ImplN` placeholders from type counts.
- Rationale: The previous header-driven `MSFT` path had crossed the line from "real parser exists" to "real parser still publishes impoverished metadata." The realish `MSFT` corpus could prove type counts and names, but it still hid whether function docs, import bindings, and impl-type flags survived the parser path rather than falling back to synthetic guesses.
