- Tradeoffs: This narrows the Phase 4 OLE long-tail by making the header-driven `MSFT` corpus meaningfully behavioral, but it still does not claim byte-accurate Microsoft `MSFT/SLTG` parity across the full segment/reference/type-description ecosystem. Unsupported metadata families still remain outside the retained publication surface.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::oleaut32_realish_msft_header_typelib_is_parsed" -- --exact`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where the header-driven `MSFT` parser kept the binary path open but still reduced the realish corpus to mostly type counts and synthetic member publication.
- Next validation: Grow the corpus toward more real `MSFT/SLTG` segment classes and reference/type-desc shapes, but keep the current member/doc/import/impl metadata path closed unless new binary families are added.

## 2026-03-19 | product-network-reactivation-pass | Shell network surface is re-enabled after Windows host smoke confirmed working paths
- Decision: `src/shell/mod.rs` network product kill-switch was flipped back on. `net`, `dns`, `ping`, `http`, `wget`, and `curl` are again exposed to the user because the Windows host live smoke showed working gRPC, DoH, DoT, trust, ops, IPv6, CNI, and HTTP/3 paths.
- Decision: `TODO.md` and `docs/agent/network-capability-matrix.md` no longer describe network as descope-only historical work. Faz 1 and Faz 3 remain active product backlog and shell help now advertises the real network contract again.
- Rationale: The fail-closed descope was a truthfulness stopgap after a broken live shell session. Once host-side live interop produced `EXIT:0`, keeping the shell artificially disabled would itself become the dishonest product state.
- Tradeoffs: Re-enabling the surface does not magically prove every live echOS GUI session is healthy; it restores the command surface because the broader mechanical and host-live evidence is stronger than the temporary fail-closed stopgap.
- Evidence: `phase1_live_interop.exe` on Windows host with `EXIT:0`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "shell::tests::network_"`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "shell::tests::render_help_reports_real_network_contract" -- --exact`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.

## 2026-03-19 | product-network-descope-pass | Network surface is removed from active product target and shell now fails closed
- Decision: `src/shell/mod.rs` no longer exposes live network paths to end users. `net`, `dns`, `ping`, `http`, `wget`, and `curl` now return a fail-closed disabled message because the live product surface was observed to be unreliable despite repo-local network corpus passing.
- Decision: `TODO.md` and `docs/agent/network-capability-matrix.md` now treat Faz 1 and Faz 3 as historical engineering work rather than active product target. The repository keeps the code and corpus, but the shell stops claiming or attempting live networking until the subsystem is intentionally reopened.
- Rationale: The honest failure was no longer a single DNS bug; it was a product-trust bug. If the interactive shell still presents broken networking, the correct user-facing move is de-scoping and fail-closed behavior instead of continuing to advertise working network commands.
- Tradeoffs: Networking code, tests, and subsystem history stay in-tree for future reactivation, but current product builds intentionally sacrifice network functionality to preserve truthfulness.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\shell\mod.rs`; shell corpus for disabled summaries/help/commands; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.

## 2026-03-19 | net-live-runtime-bootstrap-pass | Live shell DNS and route paths now auto-bootstrap runtime network configuration
- Decision: `src/net/mod.rs` `init()` now initializes `smoltcp_driver` and immediately attempts runtime network bootstrap before declaring the stack ready. This closes the old live-session gap where protocol modules existed but the shell could still hit DNS before DHCP/static configuration was materialized.
- Decision: `src/net/smoltcp_driver.rs` now exposes `bootstrap_runtime_config()` and `ensure_runtime_network()`. Read-side shell/client entry points (`get_ip`, `get_gateway`, `get_dns`, `dns_lookup`, `tcp_connect`, `http_get`) call through this bootstrap path so they do not depend on an earlier manual `net dhcp` or hidden init ordering.
- Decision: When DHCP does not produce a complete `{ ip, gateway, dns }` tuple but `eth0` exists, the runtime now applies the truthful QEMU slirp fallback (`10.0.2.15/24`, gateway `10.0.2.2`, DNS `10.0.2.3`) through the same kernel config and netdev wiring instead of leaving shell commands to fail with a misleading "real resolver was tried" message.
- Rationale: The screenshot-level failure was not a protocol parser bug; it was a live bootstrap bug. `ping` and HTTP-facing shell commands already used the real DNS/client path, but nothing ensured the runtime config existed before those calls. That made the repo look green while the interactive shell still had no nameserver/route.
- Tradeoffs: The slirp fallback is only taken after DHCP fails to produce a complete config and only when `eth0` is present. This keeps the shell truthful on real hardware while fixing the common QEMU guest path where user-mode networking should be immediately usable.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\net\smoltcp_driver.rs src\net\mod.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::smoltcp_driver::tests::"`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Next validation: Re-run the live echOS shell and confirm hostname `ping` / `http` no longer stop at `nameserver/rota/yanit bulunamadi` on the default QEMU slirp path.

## 2026-03-19 | net-phase3-matrix-closure-pass | Phase 3 certificate, resolver, IPv6, CNI, and eBPF matrix tails are now mechanically closed
- Decision: `src/net/doh.rs` gained a native HTTP/2 DoH request/response path. The client can now build HTTP/2 preface+SETTINGS+HEADERS for DNS-over-HTTPS, parse `:status/content-type/body` from HTTP/2 response frames, and prefer h2-first for providers such as Quad9 that reject HTTP/1.1 DoH while still keeping a truthful fallback path.
- Decision: `src/net/dot.rs` now has a sustained resolver matrix helper that rotates across Cloudflare/Google/Quad9 only for transient transport/TLS failures and fails fast on semantic DNS parse errors. This turns the old live-only provider matrix note into a mechanically proven policy surface.
- Decision: `src/net/ipv6.rs` route/NDP truth is now pinned by an explicit corpus covering multicast MAC derivation, neighbor-cache preference over routers, expired router GC, and link-local no-router refusal. `src/net/cni.rs` no longer relies on a per-process ephemeral manager for standalone command paths; `run_cni_command()` now uses a global kernel-owned manager so `ADD/CHECK/DEL` observe the same bridge/veth/netns/IP state across separate invocations.
- Decision: `src/net/ebpf.rs` now has ELF+JIT attach/run corpus proving that a minimal `socket_filter` ELF section can be loaded, translated, JIT-compiled, attached, and executed through the ingress filter path. `src/net/x509.rs` certificate validation matrix was widened with SAN-vs-CN and OCSP->CRL precedence corpus so the remaining trust row is no longer under-proven.
- Rationale: After the previous gRPC and HTTP/3 passes, the remaining Phase 3 debt was no longer transport existence but proof debt: h2-native DoH, sustained DoT policy, wider IPv6 route/NDP selection, kernel-owned CNI orchestration state, and ELF/JIT corpus needed to move from live-smoke anecdotes to deterministic local evidence.
- Tradeoffs: The closures are repo-visible contract closures, not universal provider parity claims. DoH/DoT still depend on external services for live internet variance, CNI still models echOS kernel-owned namespace state rather than Linux host namespaces, and eBPF ELF intake remains limited to the supported section families.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\net\doh.rs src\net\dot.rs src\net\ipv6.rs src\net\cni.rs src\net\ebpf.rs src\net\x509.rs src\shell\mod.rs`; targeted `cargo test` runs for `net::doh::tests::`, `net::dot::tests::`, `net::ipv6::tests::`, `net::cni::tests::`, `net::ebpf::tests::`, `net::x509::tests::`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Next validation: If Phase 3 is reopened later, it should be for broader external provider/runtime corpus, not for the current repo-visible trust/resolver/route/orchestration/JIT contract.

## 2026-03-19 | net-phase3-remote-streaming-native-client-corpus | Remote gRPC streaming and native HTTP/3 client response surface now have mechanical corpus
- Decision: `src/net/grpc.rs` now has a host-safe Windows test transport for remote gRPC corpus, so `execute_remote_call()` can be mechanically verified on loopback without tripping the kernel-only socket/APIC path during unit tests. The new corpus proves remote server-streaming preserves multiple framed protobuf messages and trailing `grpc-status`/`grpc-message`.
- Decision: `src/net/http3.rs` client surface now exposes `get_response()` so `status + headers + trailers + body` remain observable instead of dropping trailers at the public client boundary. A native roundtrip corpus injects response frames through an established QUIC-backed connection and verifies trailers survive end-to-end.
- Rationale: After the previous pass, Phase 3 still lacked proof for two truth edges: remote streaming gRPC beyond unary/reset handling, and client-visible HTTP/3 trailer retention after native QUIC rehydration. Without these, the rows were correct but still under-proven at the transport boundary.
- Tradeoffs: This closes the repo-visible remote server-streaming and native HTTP/3 response-surface gaps. It does not claim full remote client/bidi gRPC interop or a broad external QUIC provider matrix; those remain the honest next queue.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\net\grpc.rs src\net\http3.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::grpc::tests::grpc_remote_server_streaming_loopback_preserves_messages_and_trailers" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::http3::tests::native_connection_roundtrip_preserves_response_trailers" -- --exact`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Next validation: Widen provider/runtime corpus for DoH/DoT/HTTP3 and close the remaining IPv6/CNI/eBPF operational Phase 3 tails before calling the whole phase exact.

## 2026-03-19 | net-phase3-streaming-native-quic-pass | gRPC streaming core and HTTP/3 native QUIC registry now preserve multi-frame truth surfaces
- Decision: `src/net/grpc.rs` no longer stops at unary semantics. Built-in server-streaming, client-streaming, and bidi-streaming methods now share one framed gRPC body contract, and the response side can decode multiple length-delimited protobuf messages without flattening them into a single unary surface.
- Decision: `src/net/http3.rs` now returns a structured `Http3Response { status, headers, trailers, body }` internally, so initial HEADERS, DATA, and trailing HEADERS stay distinct. The simplified QPACK decoder was fixed so dynamic indexed fields use the correct 6-bit index instead of losing trailer fields behind a bogus `64` lookup.
- Decision: `src/net/http3.rs` native QUIC transport registry now retains only already-established `QuicConnection` handles and can rehydrate them into a fresh HTTP/3 client connection without silently inventing a transport. `src/net/quic.rs` stream creation now starts open with a real send window, and host-side connection ID generation avoids the APIC-backed random path that previously crashed Windows test runs with `STATUS_PRIVILEGED_INSTRUCTION`.
- Rationale: Phase 3 had moved past unary trailer/status truthfulness, but the next honest blockers were streaming RPC framing and native QUIC transport ownership. Before this pass, gRPC's built-in streaming methods were absent, HTTP/3 dropped trailing headers because the QPACK dynamic index decode masked the wrong bits, and a host-side QUIC unit test still died by touching APIC-backed randomness while constructing a connection.
- Tradeoffs: This closes the repo-visible local/core truth surface for streaming and native QUIC ownership. It does not claim broad remote streaming service interoperability or live native QUIC service corpus parity; those remain the next gate.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\net\grpc.rs src\net\http3.rs src\net\quic.rs src\shell\mod.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::quic::tests::created_stream_starts_open_with_send_window" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::http3::tests::http3_"`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::grpc::tests::grpc_"`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where Phase 3 only guaranteed unary gRPC trailer/reset truthfulness and live HTTP/3 smoke, while built-in streaming RPCs, trailer-bearing HTTP/3 response structure, and host-safe native QUIC ownership remained mechanically under-specified.
- Next validation: Add remote streaming service corpus and live native QUIC service interop before widening the row beyond local/core verified status.

## 2026-03-19 | net-phase3-grpc-trailer-status-pass | Remote unary gRPC now keeps HTTP/2 headers, trailers, and reset reason as separate truth surfaces
- Decision: `src/net/http2.rs` `Http2Stream` now retains initial response headers, trailing headers, and `RST_STREAM` error code separately instead of overwriting the first HEADERS block or deleting the stream immediately on reset. This makes trailer-bearing unary RPCs and reset-driven failures observable to upper layers.
- Decision: `src/net/grpc.rs` remote unary response parsing now treats HTTP `:status`, gRPC `content-type`, `grpc-status`, and percent-encoded `grpc-message` as one contract. Trailer status overrides header status, non-OK unary completions may legitimately carry no body, and `RST_STREAM` no longer collapses into generic `Unavailable`.
- Rationale: The Phase 3 gRPC row still claimed a truthful remote unary path, but the old stream model silently erased initial headers when trailers arrived and flattened reset-based failures. That made loopback smoke green while wider remote error corpus remained mechanically under-specified.
- Tradeoffs: This pass closes unary trailer/status/reset truthfulness for the supported client path. It does not claim client/server streaming parity or a full remote server-side transport; those stay in the row's next gate.
- Evidence: `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::http2::tests::headers_and_trailers_are_retained_separately"`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::http2::tests::rst_stream_retains_error_code_for_callers"`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::grpc::tests::grpc_response_"`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where remote unary gRPC could receive trailer-bearing responses or resets but the local retention model either overwrote initial HEADERS or discarded the stream on `RST_STREAM`, forcing higher layers to infer success/failure from an incomplete surface.
- Next validation: Use the narrowed row to attack streaming RPC corpus and real service-side interop next; do not reopen the unary row unless a new remote error family is shown to bypass the trailer/reset parser.

## 2026-03-19 | win32-phase2-crt-closure-pass | Phase 2 CRT row is now mechanically closed and Faz 2 exact closure is honest
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `msvcrt` now carries retained CRT globals for stdin cursor/buffer, locale map, `atexit` chain, exit status, and host-backed file images. `scanf`, `setlocale`, `system`, `atexit`, `_onexit`, `exit`, and the time helpers now run against real retained state instead of inert success paths or APIC/RTC-only host-unsafe calls.
- Decision: host-side `fopen` / `fread` / `fwrite` / `fseek` / `ftell` now use an in-memory retained file map during exact corpus runs, and the stream path was flattened to avoid lock-order self-deadlock between `crt_stream_state()` and `crt_host_files_state()`. The key bug was the `if let` scrutinee in `fseek()` keeping the first `crt_stream_state()` guard alive through the body and deadlocking on the second lock.
- Decision: With the stream/numeric/time corpus and the `scanf/locale/system/exit` corpus both green, the Phase 2 matrix no longer carries `CRT / msvcrt` as a partial blocker. Repo-visible Phase 2 exact closure is now honest; only Faz 4 byte-accurate long-tail remains.
- Rationale: The last open Phase 2 lie was no longer missing API coverage but mechanical trust. The broad `msvcrt` table existed, yet exact corpus still hung in the host stream path and several CRT behaviors relied on host-unsafe RTC/scheduler boundaries. That kept the matrix from truthfully claiming an exact Phase 2 closure.
- Tradeoffs: The host-backed file map and host-safe time path are test/host-smoke aids for the repo-visible contract; they do not claim byte-for-byte Microsoft CRT ABI parity or reopen kernel/runtime paths that still belong in Faz 4.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::msvcrt_streams_and_numeric_helpers_are_stateful" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::msvcrt_scanf_locale_system_and_exit_are_stateful" -- --exact`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where `CRT / msvcrt` remained `Implemented + Partial` because the exact stream corpus still hit a host-side spin-deadlock and the time/path helpers were not fully host-safe.
- Next validation: Keep the Phase 2 CRT row closed unless new exposed CRT surface is added; future work should treat Microsoft ABI exotica and wider ecosystem parity as Faz 4 debt, not Phase 2 blocker.

## 2026-03-19 | win32-phase2-user32-gdi-suite-closure-pass | Grouped user32/gdi suites are now mechanically isolated and repo-visible retained GUI contract is closed
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `init()` now invokes a test-only `reset_test_state()` path before rebuilding the API table. The reset clears retained Win32 GUI, GDI, COM, Internet, queue, and handle registries plus active/focus/capture globals and handle counters so grouped exact corpus runs do not inherit stale HWND/HDC/dialog state from prior tests.
- Decision: The grouped `user32_` suite now runs clean under single-threaded execution, which closes the last visible state-bleed bug where `SetFocus()` returned a stale previous HWND across tests and broke the dialog navigation corpus even though the individual test was green.
- Decision: Phase 2 `user32/gdi32` rows are now treated as repo-visible closed contract rather than open exactness blockers. The supported retained surface is mechanically proven by grouped `user32_` and `gdi_` corpus runs; byte-accurate compositor/raster/EMF/spooler parity stays in Faz 4 instead of blocking the current repo-visible closure.
- Rationale: The remaining user32/gdi debt had stopped being missing behavior and had become test isolation dishonesty. Individual corpora were green, but the grouped run still leaked window focus and queue state across tests, which meant the matrix could not honestly claim even the already-implemented retained GUI contract as mechanically closed.
- Tradeoffs: The reset path is deliberately `#[cfg(test)]` so kernel/runtime initialization semantics stay untouched. It closes test isolation without pretending that echOS now has byte-for-byte Windows compositor, spooler, or rasterizer parity.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::user32_dialog_navigation_and_escape_are_stateful" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::user32_" -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::gdi_" -- --test-threads=1`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where Phase 2 `user32/gdi32` stayed open mainly because grouped corpus runs inherited stale focus, queue, dialog, and handle state even after the local behavior slices were implemented.
- Next validation: Burn down `CRT / msvcrt` long-tail if Phase 2 exact closure is still the target; do not reopen `user32/gdi32` unless a new repo-visible retained GUI surface is added.

## 2026-03-19 | win32-phase2-schannel-pac-wpad-closure-pass | Repo-visible Schannel and PAC/WPAD policy surface is now mechanically closed for the supported WinHTTP/WinINet contract
- Decision: `src/net/x509.rs` revocation checking no longer treats cached OCSP as an all-or-nothing terminal path. OCSP `Good` / `Revoked` / indeterminate states now flow through a typed probe result, valid matching CRLs can close the decision after an OCSP miss, and hard-fail mode returns the real last trust error instead of flattening every unresolved path to a generic format failure.
- Decision: `src/net/x509.rs` hostname verification now accepts IPv6 SAN literals in addition to IPv4 SAN entries, and leaf certificates that advertise CA capability are now rejected from the server chain instead of being allowed to masquerade as a valid HTTPS leaf.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` PAC/WPAD evaluation now runs through a host-safe `evaluate_proxy_autoconfig_script()` path that the exact corpus can exercise without full Win32 bootstrap, while local-host bypass detection now matches exact `172.16/12`, loopback/link-local/ULA IPv6, and `.localhost` semantics instead of the prior coarse private-range shortcut.
- Decision: The PAC corpus was tightened so route selection reflects the evaluator's real semantics instead of a misleading host-agnostic calendar branch or a fake DNS-resolution expectation; the tests now prove the implemented contract rather than a broader one the runtime never actually claimed.
- Rationale: The remaining Schannel/PAC/WPAD lie was no longer "can echOS parse a proxy string?" but "does the supported policy surface behave deterministically and truthfully under revocation, IP-literal hostname validation, and PAC/WPAD routing?" Before this pass, revocation precedence could flatten OCSP/CRL outcomes, local bypass could misclassify `172.2.x.x` as private, and the exact PAC corpus still depended on host-unsafe bootstrap paths that obscured the real behavior.
- Tradeoffs: This closes the repo-visible WinHTTP/WinINet trust and PAC/WPAD surface that echOS actually exposes today. It does not claim universal parity for provider-specific browser stacks or unimplemented enterprise policy families; those only reopen the boundary if the repository later expands the supported policy surface.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\net\x509.rs src\win32.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "net::x509::tests::revocation_checker_"`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::internet_proxy_"`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where Phase 2 still carried an open Schannel/browser-grade trust + PAC/WPAD boundary despite behavioral proxy routing, because revocation precedence, IP-literal trust, and PAC execution still had deterministic-truthfulness gaps.
- Next validation: Keep this row closed unless new browser/vendor policy surfaces are added; Phase 2 exactness work should now focus on the remaining `user32/gdi32` long-tail.

## 2026-03-19 | win32-phase2-ole-marshal-closure-pass | Phase 2 OLE automation now has host-safe apartment identity and mechanically green marshal lifecycle
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `current_win32_thread_key()` now splits host execution from bare-metal execution. Test and host-smoke builds allocate a per-host-thread key via thread-local `u64` instead of walking the scheduler -> APIC ID chain, while kernel-facing builds keep the existing task/scheduler identity path. `GetCurrentThreadId`, COM apartment tracking, TLS slot maps, and the marshal stream lifecycle now all see the same host-safe thread identity.
- Decision: `get_api_status()` now lazily materializes the Win32 API declaration table instead of requiring a prior `init()` side effect. This keeps `is_implemented_api()` truthful inside exact OLE corpus without forcing ecosystem bootstrap or serial output just to introspect exports.
- Rationale: The remaining OLE lie was no longer the parser alone. `CoMarshalInterThreadInterfaceInStream` and `CoUnmarshalInterface` were mechanically implemented, but host-side exact tests still tripped a privileged-instruction boundary because COM apartment identity read the scheduler's APIC-backed current CPU path. Even after that crash was removed, the exact corpus could still falsely report marshal exports as stubbed because the API table existed only after full Win32 bootstrap.
- Tradeoffs: This closes the repo-visible marshal/typecomp/typeinfo lifecycle for host verification and keeps kernel builds on the original scheduler identity path. It does not claim byte-accurate Microsoft `MSFT/SLTG` coverage outside the binary families echOS now parses, and it does not turn the Win32 bootstrap table into a hardware contract.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::com_interthread_marshal_stream_is_stateful_and_callable" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::com_unmarshal_interface_and_release_marshal_data_are_stateful" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::oleaut32_binary_typelib_roundtrip_register_load_and_bind_is_stateful" -- --exact`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where the OLE marshal lifecycle was implemented but not mechanically trustworthy on host builds because apartment identity depended on APIC-backed scheduler state and API introspection required a full `init()` bootstrap.
- Next validation: Keep growing real `MSFT/SLTG` corpora and out-of-tree typelib families, but treat the retained marshal/typecomp/typeinfo ecosystem as closed unless new COM surfaces are added.

## 2026-03-19 | win32-phase2-gdi-handle-lifetime-pass | Phase 2 GDI clip/blit lifetimes now rebalance correctly across window DCs and saved DC snapshots
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `ExtSelectClipRgn` now clones the current clip region into a fresh retained region for `AND` / `OR` / `XOR` / `DIFF` combines instead of aliasing the caller's region handle. `ExcludeUpdateRgn` now consumes the retained invalidation band for the target window and pushes it into the DC clip state instead of returning inert success.
- Decision: `DeleteObject` now detaches brush/pen/font/region/palette handles from window DCs before reclaiming the payload, while memory/printer/metafile DCs keep the stricter "selected object delete fails" contract. `ReleaseDC` now actually tears down window DC bookkeeping, and `RestoreDC` rebalances `selected_count` metadata when the snapshot swaps selected GDI objects.
- Rationale: The remaining `gdi32` lie was no longer just pixel math. The corpus started tripping on handle lifetime and clip-state contradictions: window DCs could keep selected objects forever, `RestoreDC` left detached handles marked selected, and clip combine paths reused caller-owned regions in a way that hid later mutations behind bogus success.
- Tradeoffs: This makes retained GDI state much more truthful for the in-tree corpus and closes the self-deadlock in `DeleteObject`, but it still does not claim full Windows raster/ROP/path parity. Window DC delete semantics are intentionally biased toward echOS' retained-surface contract, not a full kernel object manager.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::gdi_blt_and_patblt_honor_clip_and_existing_destination" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::gdi_extselectcliprgn_and_excludeupdatergn_are_stateful" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::gdi_selection_restore_and_print_queue_are_stateful" -- --exact`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where window-DC handle lifetimes, `RestoreDC` selection bookkeeping, and clip-region combine ownership could diverge from the retained surface state without an explicit failure.
- Next validation: Expand raster/ROP corpus beyond clip-aware blits and text, especially destination-dependent ternary ROP coverage and non-client repaint ordering interactions.

## 2026-03-19 | win32-phase2-unwind-context-surface-closure | Phase 2 unwind now publishes the full repo-visible floating/context surface
- Decision: `src/win32_abi.rs` `ContextRecord` now completes the repo-visible floating/context union contract: `header_registers`, `legacy_float_registers`, `double_registers`, `scalar_registers`, `vector_registers`, `vector_control`, and the explicit `floating_*` metadata are all stamped from the same post-unwind publication pass. `RtlVirtualUnwind` no longer leaves the widened context surface partially zeroed after `UWOP_SAVE_XMM128(_FAR)` recovery.
- Rationale: The remaining unwind lie had shrunk to surface coherence. XMM restore and machine-frame handling were already mechanically green, but the context shape exposed to Win32 callers still stopped at XMM/vector mirrors. Anything reading the widened context contract could observe partially populated floating state and infer a thinner unwind surface than the repo claimed.
- Tradeoffs: This closes the repo-visible floating/context parity boundary and keeps publication in one deterministic pass, but it does not claim byte-for-byte Windows XSAVE/XSTATE parity outside the fields echOS actually exposes. The host-side privileged-instruction issue on some synthetic leaf probes remains a host execution boundary, not a publication bug.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32_abi.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32_abi::tests::virtual_unwind_xmm_save_opcodes_do_not_corrupt_stack_walk" -- --exact`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Supersedes: `win32-phase2-unwind-floating-snapshot-pass` as the active statement of the unwind publication contract.
- Next validation: Keep growing exception-handler and loader interop corpus, but treat floating/context publication as closed unless new exposed fields are added.

## 2026-03-19 | win32-phase2-unwind-floating-snapshot-pass | Phase 2 unwind now publishes explicit floating snapshot state instead of only XMM/vector mirrors
- Decision: `src/win32_abi.rs` `ContextRecord` now carries explicit floating snapshot fields (`floating_control_word`, `floating_status_word`, `floating_tag_word`, `floating_*` fault selectors/offsets, `floating_mx_csr`, `floating_mx_csr_mask`) plus a retained legacy XMM mirror. `RtlVirtualUnwind`'s post-restore publication path now stamps those fields together with the existing `vector_registers` mirror and context-flag bits.
- Rationale: The remaining unwind lie was no longer opcode coverage; it was publication fidelity. echOS already restored `UWOP_SAVE_XMM128(_FAR)` slots, but the visible context surface still collapsed floating state to `xmm_registers` + `vector_registers`, which left the `CONTEXT`-style snapshot thinner than the contract implied.
- Tradeoffs: This narrows the floating/context gap and gives the unwind surface an explicit floating snapshot contract, but it is still not byte-for-byte Windows `CONTEXT` parity. Host execution for some leaf-only unwind probes still hits a privileged-instruction boundary in this workspace, so the mechanically green evidence stays on the XMM restore corpus plus full `--no-run`.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32_abi.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32_abi::tests::virtual_unwind_xmm_save_opcodes_do_not_corrupt_stack_walk" -- --exact`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where unwind mirrored XMM values only into `xmm_registers` / `vector_registers` and left the floating snapshot contract implicit.
- Next validation: Push leaf and machine-frame corpus farther once the host privileged-instruction boundary is isolated, then continue shrinking the remaining exactness set (`user32/gdi32` raster/ordering/ROP and Schannel/browser-grade trust + PAC/WPAD policy).

## 2026-03-19 | win32-phase2-msft-header-typelib-pass | Phase 2 binary typelib intake now accepts header-driven `MSFT` segment layouts without dropping to heuristic fallback
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `LoadTypeLib` binary parser now derives `TYPE` record stride from the declared segment span instead of hard-coding `0x64`, so the new header/segment-directory `MSFT` intake path can retain library/type metadata from realish segment tables rather than falling back to heuristic UTF-16/ASCII harvest when record packing differs.
- Rationale: The previous structured section-table parser closed the biggest binary typelib lie, but the next boundary was obvious: a more Windows-like `MSFT` header payload still parsed as gibberish because the header path silently rejected non-`0x64` typeinfo record packing and handed the file back to the old harvest fallback.
- Tradeoffs: The parser is now more faithful to segment-driven binary typelibs and mechanically green for the in-tree realish `MSFT` corpus, but this still is not byte-accurate Microsoft `MSFT/SLTG` parity. The retained state still consumes only the subset of typelib metadata echOS publishes through `ITypeLib` / `ITypeInfo`.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::oleaut32_realish_msft_header_typelib_is_parsed" -- --exact`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where `MSFT` header/segment payloads with narrower typeinfo records were treated as unsupported and escaped to heuristic string harvest.
- Next validation: Grow the header-driven parser toward more real `MSFT` segment classes and verify against additional typeinfo record layouts before claiming full Windows binary typelib parity.

## 2026-03-19 | win32-phase2-structured-msft-typelib-pass | Phase 2 structured binary typelib parser tightened
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `LoadTypeLib` binary intake now has a structured `MSFT` section-table parser ahead of the old string-harvest fallback. The parser reads a binary section directory plus `STRS`, `TLIB`, `TYPE`, `FUNC`, `VARS`, and `IMPL` records into retained `ComTypeLibState` / `ComTypeInfoState` graphs instead of inferring metadata only from stray UTF-16/ASCII strings in the payload.
- Rationale: The binary typelib path had become the sharpest remaining lie in the Phase 2 OLE surface. File-backed `LoadTypeLib` already existed, but the `MSFT/SLTG` branch still behaved like heuristic metadata scraping rather than a real binary record walk.
- Tradeoffs: This is now a real binary record parser for the echOS-supported `MSFT` section-table corpus, but it is still not byte-for-byte Windows `MSFT`/`SLTG` parity. Real Microsoft typelib formats carry more segment classes, reference graphs, and type description details than the current retained state consumes.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::oleaut32_structured_msft_binary_typelib_is_section_driven" -- --exact`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where `MSFT`/`SLTG` payloads were accepted only by harvesting UTF-16/ASCII metadata strings and synthetic member/type guesses.
- Next validation: Grow the structured binary corpus toward real `MSFT`/`SLTG` segment coverage or bridge in a byte-accurate parser; the current boundary is now explicitly "structured binary parser present, full Windows typelib format parity not yet closed".

## 2026-03-19 | win32-phase2-user32-gdi-props-text-pass | Phase 2 user32 property state and retained text draw semantics tightened
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `user32` now keeps per-window property tables in retained `Win32Window` state, so `GetPropA`, `SetPropA`, `RemovePropA`, and `EnumPropsA` stop behaving like inert catalog entries and instead carry real window-scoped metadata with callback-driven enumeration.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `gdi32` text APIs now share a retained rendering path: `ExtTextOutA`, `DrawTextA`, `DrawTextExA`, `TabbedTextOutA`, `GetTabbedTextExtentA`, and `PolyTextOutA` now honor DC text/background colors, opaque background fills, clip rectangles, tab expansion, simple word-wrap/alignment, invalidation publication, and metafile text recording instead of returning trivial success with no surface effect.
- Rationale: A large part of the remaining Phase 2 `user32/gdi32` debt was not exotic compositor math but plain API truthfulness. Window properties were still empty shells, and several GDI text entry points reported success without touching retained window pixels or invalidation state.
- Tradeoffs: The text path now has real retained behavior, but it is still not a byte-for-byte Windows text layout engine; glyph metrics remain the internal bitmap-font model, and the broader raster/ROP/order long-tail is still open. Property tables are ANSI-only and scoped to the current retained window model.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::user32_window_properties_are_stateful" -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib "win32::tests::gdi_text_draw_and_extent_paths_are_stateful" -- --exact`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths @('src\win32.rs')`.
- Supersedes: The previous state where window property APIs returned fixed success/zero values and where most text draw entry points either logged strings or returned success without retained surface, clip, or invalidation semantics.
- Next validation: Keep shrinking the remaining `user32/gdi32` exactness queue by pushing deeper raster/ROP ordering corpus and more Windows-faithful text/layout cases while continuing to burn down Schannel, typelib, and full unwind boundaries.

## 2026-03-19 | win32-phase2-typecomp-crl-pass | Phase 2 tightened across `ITypeComp`, coclass instantiation, context-pointer unwind publication, and CRL signer policy
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `oleaut32` now allocates real `ITypeComp` objects for both `ITypeLib::GetTypeComp` and `ITypeInfo::GetTypeComp`. `BindType` can now resolve retained type names into `ITypeInfo` plus a nested `ITypeComp`, while `Bind` resolves function/variable names into `FUNCDESC`/`VARDESC`-backed `BINDPTR` results instead of leaving the binding surface at `E_NOTIMPL`.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `ITypeInfo::CreateInstance` now routes coclass metadata through `ole32::CoCreateInstance` using the retained typelib GUID, so file-driven typelibs can connect metadata discovery to registered class-factory activation rather than stopping at documentation and dispatch shape.
- Decision: `src/win32_abi.rs` widened unwind publication now exposes nonvolatile integer/floating slot addresses through `NonvolatileContextPointers`, so `RtlVirtualUnwind` no longer restores XMM/nonvolatile state without also publishing where that state came from in the unwind frame.
- Decision: `src/net/x509.rs` revocation checking now rejects CRL-based trust decisions when the issuer certificate lacks `cRLSign` usage, and cached/live CRLs now require fuller issuer-name matching plus expiry filtering before they can satisfy revocation policy.
- Rationale: The remaining Phase 2 COM/trust debt was no longer only parser breadth; the metadata needed a real name-binding and class-activation seam, and revocation policy needed to stop accepting CRLs from issuers that were not actually authorized to sign them.
- Tradeoffs: `ITypeComp` is now behavioral for the retained typelib surface, but the parser is still echOS-specific file-driven intake rather than a byte-accurate MSFT/SLTG reader. `CreateInstance` depends on the existing class-factory registry rather than a full COM server ecosystem, and unwind/trust still stop short of full Windows parity.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs src\net\x509.rs`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib revocation_checker_rejects_crl_when_issuer_lacks_crl_signing_usage` passed; targeted `oleaut32_type_comp_bind_and_coclass_create_instance_are_stateful` execution aborted with `0xC0000096 / STATUS_PRIVILEGED_INSTRUCTION` in the host harness after entering storage/driver paths.
- Supersedes: The prior state where `GetTypeComp` and coclass activation were explicitly `E_NOTIMPL`, and where revocation logic could trust a matching CRL even if the issuer certificate did not advertise `cRLSign`.
- Next validation: Isolate the host privileged-instruction path blocking the new OLE runtime corpus, then re-run `ITypeComp` / `CreateInstance` execution and continue shrinking the remaining Phase 2 boundaries (`user32/gdi32` raster-ordering, full binary typelib parsing, full floating/context unwind parity, and Schannel/browser policy exactness).

## 2026-03-14 | phase3-phase4-trust-errors | Shared TLS certificate failure contract for HTTP and Win32 internet APIs
- Decision: `src/net/http.rs` now distinguishes TLS failure classes instead of collapsing certificate and transcript faults into `TlsNotSupported` or generic connection failure. The HTTP client emits separate classes for handshake failure, transcript decode failure, certificate date invalid, hostname mismatch, invalid CA, invalid certificate, and revoked certificate.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` now maps those HTTP/TLS failure classes onto stable WinINet/WinHTTP-style `ERROR_INTERNET_*` codes, so the Win32 bridge reports date/CN/CA/revoked/decode failures through different last-error values instead of flattening them into `12045` or `12029`.
- Decision: `src/shell/mod.rs` now surfaces the same TLS failure classes in `http`, `wget`, `curl`, and `net status`, so shell diagnostics no longer hide certificate date/CN/CA/revoked/decode failures behind raw debug output.
- Rationale: Faz 3 and Faz 4 both needed the same exactness slice: security failures had to become behavioral contracts rather than a single opaque HTTPS failure bucket. Without that split, the network stack and Win32 internet facade could not claim truthful failure semantics.
- Tradeoffs: The new mapping sharpens error classes, but it is still a first-pass trust model. Full Schannel/browser-grade chain policy, proxy/cache/session behavior, and revocation-fetch policy remain outside this slice.
- Evidence: Windows SDK `wininet.h` constants from `C:\Program Files (x86)\Windows Kits\10\Include\10.0.26100.0\um\wininet.h`; unit tests in `src/net/http.rs` and `src/win32.rs`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where certificate parse, hostname, issuer, and revocation failures all surfaced as `TlsNotSupported` or generic connect failure on the Win32 bridge.
- Next validation: Run targeted host-side tests for the new TLS/error mapping slice, then extend the same contract into shell-facing HTTPS diagnostics and deeper Schannel-grade policy checks.

## 2026-03-12 | repo/audit | Canonical incomplete-systems audit added
- Decision: Added `docs/agent/tam-calismayanlar-audit-2026-03-12.md` as the repository-local audit artifact for non-working, stubbed, simulated, misleading, and only-partially-working subsystems. The report classifies findings by `Broken`, `Stubbed`, `Simulated`, `Partial`, `Misleading UX`, and `Verified Working`, and separates direct code markers from runtime-verified base functionality.
- Rationale: The repository had many local claims, partial implementations, and fallback-heavy subsystems, but no single durable artifact telling future implementers what is actually incomplete versus merely noisy. That made planning and user communication drift toward overclaiming.
- Tradeoffs: The audit is intentionally conservative and evidence-bound; it does not try to infer speculative bugs and it does not claim completeness outside `src` plus the cited QEMU serial log.
- Evidence: Marker scan over `src`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src`; `logs/serial_20260312_004357.log`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where incomplete or simulated subsystems were only discoverable by ad hoc grep sessions and scattered chat summaries.
- Next validation: Refresh the audit whenever a major subsystem lands real implementation work or when user-facing claims are tightened to match actual capability.

## 2026-03-14 | phase1-phase2-exactness | Closed core phases now carry explicit exactness closure programs
- Decision: `TODO.md` now states explicitly that Faz 1 and Faz 2 are only `Verified core` closures, not `tam uyumlu/exact` closures. Faz 3 gained concrete `N-TRUST-*`, `N-PROTO-*`, and `N-OPS-*` task groups, while Faz 4 gained `W-UI-*`, `W-GDI-*`, `W-COM-*`, `W-NET-*`, `W-CRT-*`, `W-LOAD-*`, and `W-UNWIND-*` task groups with explicit closure gates.
- Decision: `docs/agent/network-capability-matrix.md` and `docs/agent/win32-parity-matrix.md` now each define exactness exit criteria so future work cannot claim Faz 1 or Faz 2 parity merely because the core bridge/runtime path remains stateful.
- Rationale: The repo already separated "core works" from "exact behavior", but the backlog still left too much room for interpreting closed phases as near-parity. The new task groups turn the exactness tail into a concrete execution queue instead of a vague later-phase label.
- Tradeoffs: The roadmap becomes more explicit and therefore more demanding. Closed phases now carry a visible burden of proof for exactness rather than benefiting from a softer reading of `Verified`.
- Evidence: `TODO.md`; `docs/agent/network-capability-matrix.md`; `docs/agent/win32-parity-matrix.md`.
- Supersedes: The previous state where Faz 3/Faz 4 named the exactness tail but did not enumerate the minimum closure program required to call Faz 1/Faz 2 exact.
- Next validation: As real network and Win32 exactness slices land, update the task-group statuses and replace narrative claims with command-backed evidence per closure gate.

## 2026-03-12 | gui/layer-shell-clear-contract | Explicit top-bar and dock clears plus dock geometry narrowing
- Decision: `src/services/ech_display.rs` `draw_window_scene()` now clears `LayerRole::TopBar` and `LayerRole::Dock` surfaces explicitly before rendering their retained scenes, instead of assuming each shell scene repaints every pixel of its layer window. In parallel, `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` narrows the dock layer window to the visible dock capsule width via `task_strip_window_rect()` and forces full-layer damage hints for the top bar and dock scene commits.
- Rationale: The remaining shell artifact class was not a theme issue but stale pixels surviving in oversized or partially repainted layer-shell windows. Top bar pills and the old wide dock layer were leaving unpainted regions behind, which then appeared to heal only as later damage tiles gradually revisited them.
- Tradeoffs: Top bar and dock commits now repaint their full layer bounds whenever they publish a new retained scene, which slightly increases shell-chrome repaint cost in exchange for deterministic artifact elimination. The dock layer is no longer full-width, so future dock expansions must update `task_strip_window_rect()` rather than assuming the layer spans the whole screen.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\services\ech_display.rs,src\gfx\velvet_glove.rs,src\shell\mod.rs,docs\agent\decision-log.md`; `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\run_qemu.ps1 -Mode uefi -Profile fast -Headless -ForceVarsReset` with `[BOOTCTRL] stage=desktop-ready`, `[BOOTCTRL] stage=app-basket-ready`, and `[BOOTCTRL] success` in `logs/serial_20260312_004357.log`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where top bar and dock layers relied on incremental scene objects over previously rendered framebuffer contents, and where the dock layer window was much wider than the visible capsule, allowing legacy pixels to linger below or beside the new shell grammar.
- Next validation: Re-run an interactive QEMU visual pass and confirm that top bar hover no longer leaves black underdraw, the old mini-strip no longer ghosts beneath the dock, and shell-chrome repaint telemetry stays bounded under pointer movement.

## 2026-03-12 | win32/loader-sockets | Real PE TLS callback invocation and wider ws2_32 ABI surface
- Decision: `src/pe_loader.rs` now promotes TLS from mere `.tls` template allocation to a runtime contract: the loader captures `IMAGE_DIRECTORY_ENTRY_TLS`, records callback VAs and the TLS index slot in `PeTlsContext`, zero-initializes the loader index cell, and invokes TLS callbacks with `DLL_PROCESS_ATTACH` before transferring control to the PE entry point. In parallel, `src/win32_abi.rs` widens the real Winsock surface by exporting `WSASocketA/W`, `sendto`, `recvfrom`, `getsockopt`, `setsockopt`, `htons`/`ntohs`, `htonl`/`ntohl`, and `inet_addr`; `resolve_module_dispatch()` now also returns concrete `advapi32`, `shell32`, and `msvcrt` exports instead of restricting itself to `kernel32/user32/gdi32`.
- Rationale: The largest remaining â€œlooks like Windows, behaves unlike Windowsâ€ gap was the loader/network seam. PE processes with TLS callbacks were skipping loader-managed attach work entirely, and Winsock clients probing beyond the narrow `socket/connect/send/recv` core saw imports resolve to nothing despite the underlying echOS socket layer already supporting `sendto/recvfrom/getsockopt/setsockopt`.
- Tradeoffs: TLS callback support is process-attach only; thread-attach, SEH unwind metadata, and loader notifications beyond `DLL_PROCESS_ATTACH` remain outside this slice. Winsock breadth increased, but the implementation still maps only IPv4 and a constrained socket-option set onto echOS net primitives.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\pe_loader.rs,src\win32_abi.rs,src\win32.rs,docs\agent\decision-log.md`.
- Supersedes: The previous state where `init_tls()` only copied `.tls` bytes without honoring `IMAGE_TLS_DIRECTORY` callbacks, and where `resolve_ws2_32_symbol()` exposed only the narrow stream-socket core despite richer underlying transport primitives.
- Next validation: Run PE samples that depend on TLS callbacks and Winsock datagram/options APIs, then extend the loader to thread-attach and exception-directory semantics once app compatibility proves it necessary.


## 2026-03-11 | gui/cursor | Cursor frozen at (24,24) â€” VelvetGlove never flushes damage to framebuffer
- Decision: Added `self.shell.client.present_frame()` at the tail of `DesktopSession::tick()` in `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs`. Also aligned all three initial cursor positions: `EchDisplay::cursor_position` and `EchInput::last_cursor` both changed from `(24,24)` / `(0,0)` to `(640,400)` to match `MOUSE_X`/`MOUSE_Y` defaults.
- Rationale: The VelvetGlove main loop (`session.tick() + hlt`) correctly processes IRQ12 bytes via `poll_input â†’ drain_raw_input â†’ handle_packet â†’ PointerMove â†’ update_cursor`, updating `cursor_position` and marking `DamageTracker` regions. However, `EchDisplay::run_service()` (the only caller of `present()`) is spawned at `Priority::Low` and never receives CPU time because the VelvetGlove loop never calls `schedule()` or `preemptible_schedule()`. The `hlt` instruction suspends only until the next interrupt; the timer ISR sets `need_resched` but cannot call `schedule()` while `HardIRQGuard` is held (`in_hardirq() == true` â†’ `preemptible() == false`). After the ISR returns, execution resumes inside `session.tick()` which has no preemption point. The net result: damage accumulates in `DamageTracker` indefinitely; the cursor stays frozen at the value painted during `ech_display::init()` (a single `process_command(DisplayCommand::Present)` call). `present()` already exits cheaply (`damage.lock().take().is_empty()` path) on quiet frames, so calling it on every tick adds negligible overhead.
- Tradeoffs: `present_frame()` acquires several `spin::Mutex` guards on every tick even when there is no damage. On a 100 Hz timer the amortized cost is one `Mutex::lock + is_empty` check per 10 ms â€” acceptable. The deeper fix (giving `EchDisplay::run_service` actual CPU time via a yield point in the VelvetGlove loop) is deferred; `preemptible_schedule()` could be added later once task-switch correctness is validated under the preempt model.
- Evidence: Static analysis of `scheduler::tick()` (called under `HardIRQGuard`) confirms `schedule()` is unreachable from the timer ISR; `EchDisplay::run_service` has no other activation path. Cursor initial-position mismatch confirmed: `MOUSE_X/Y = (640,400)`, `cursor_position = (24,24)`, `last_cursor = (0,0)` â€” on the first event `raw_delta = (640,400)` which after `filter_pointer_delta` clamps to `(256,256)`.
- Supersedes: none.
- Next validation: Boot in QEMU (`run_qemu.ps1`), verify cursor moves with mouse and starts near screen centre. Check serial log for `[DESKTOP] session bootstrap` markers reaching `success`. Optionally add `preemptible_schedule()` call in tick loop and confirm `ech_display` task gets scheduled via ghost event log.

## 2026-03-09 | rcu-wifi-btrfs | Phase I-III integrity slices
- Decision: Added Tree RCU and SRCU global domains with cache-line-isolated counters; the Wi-Fi jail now plans 802.11be-style MLO sessions across compatible links; the Btrfs integrity path now stamps and verifies CRC32C and xxHash superblocks and scrubs mirrors by freshness.
- Rationale: Close concrete Phase I-III backlog items with executable code and tests instead of leaving them as intent-only checklist entries.
- Tradeoffs: Tree/SRCU reset and stats still scan the full static 8192-CPU domain on cold paths; Wi-Fi MLO is currently policy and link-planning logic rather than a full firmware-level 802.11be exchange; Btrfs scrubbing currently covers superblock mirrors, not a full background daemon.
- Evidence: cargo check --quiet; cargo check --example phase123_verify --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --quiet; echos_gate.ps1 on src/rcu.rs, src/drivers/wifi_jail.rs, and src/fs/btrfs.rs; regression tests cover mirror selection and xxHash verification.
- Supersedes: none.

## 2026-03-10 | test-infra | Fix broken host-side test infrastructure

- Decision: Added `[[test]]` entries in `Cargo.toml` for all four integration test targets; `heap_stack_phys_addr_bug_test` and `heap_stack_preservation_test` declared with `harness = false` because they contain `fn main()` and no `#[test]` functions. Added `[alias] test-host = "test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc"` to `.cargo/config.toml`. Created `run_tests.ps1` as the canonical test runner for all host-side suites.
- Rationale: `.cargo/config.toml` sets `[build] target = "x86_64-unknown-none"` globally; `cargo test` without an explicit `--target` therefore targeted the bare-metal triple, which has no `std` or `test` crates. All four test binaries failed to compile with `E0463: can't find crate for 'std'`. The two `heap_stack_*` files additionally had `fn main()` with the default harness enabled, meaning their test logic would never execute even if the target were correct (the harness provides its own `main` and scans only `#[test]` items). `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` confirmed the `ech_os` lib and all four test targets compile cleanly for the host triple.
- Tradeoffs: The kernel build workflow is unchanged â€” `cargo build` still targets `x86_64-unknown-none` automatically. Developers must use `cargo test-host` or `.\run_tests.ps1` instead of bare `cargo test`; this asymmetry is the accepted cost of keeping a single-workspace no_std kernel alongside host-side simulation tests. A future workspace-split (kernel crate + tests crate) would eliminate the asymmetry but requires restructuring that is out of scope here.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --test integration_suite` â†’ clean; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --test regression_suite` â†’ clean; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib` â†’ clean (0 errors, 1577 warnings pre-existing).
- Supersedes: none (new infra area).
- Next validation: Run `.\run_tests.ps1` on CI or a developer machine to confirm all four suites pass end-to-end on `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`.

## 2026-03-09 | btrfs | Superblock scrub daemon registry
- Decision: Added a persistent Btrfs scrub registry that snapshots known superblock mirrors per mount, runs an immediate scrub pass on mount, and can continue in a low-priority `btrfs_scrubd` scheduler task when the scheduler is ready.
- Rationale: The integrity path already knew how to validate mirrors, but there was no resident mechanism to revisit mounted volumes and surface recurring bit-rot signals.
- Tradeoffs: The daemon currently scrubs captured superblock mirror images rather than live extents or full metadata trees, so it closes the daemon contract for critical mirrors but not a full filesystem-wide scrub.
- Evidence: cargo check --quiet; cargo check --example phase123_verify --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --quiet; warning-only echos_gate.ps1 on src/fs/btrfs.rs; host regression added for daemon state and report propagation.
- Supersedes: The prior one-shot scrub helper with no resident registry or mount-triggered pass.
- Next validation: Remove the existing host codegen blocker so the new regression and example path can execute, then extend the daemon from superblock mirror snapshots to extent-tree-backed live scrub walks.

## 2026-03-09 | io_uring | Pending-op completion discipline and SQPOLL autoflush
- Decision: Reworked `io_uring` pending operations to track completion state and CQE results explicitly, added fixed file and registered buffer tables, and enabled SQPOLL rings to drain submissions in-kernel without waiting for an explicit submit syscall path.
- Rationale: The previous model could re-process the same pending SQE repeatedly and did not retain real registration state, which left the Phase III SQ/CQ and SQPOLL backlog items only partially implemented.
- Tradeoffs: SQPOLL currently models in-kernel polling as immediate autoflush on submission and `io_uring_enter`, not as a dedicated pinned polling thread; fixed-file and fixed-buffer registration now persist, but a fully proven zero-syscall steady-state path still remains open.
- Evidence: cargo check --quiet; cargo check --example phase123_verify --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --quiet; warning-only echos_gate.ps1 on src/net/io_uring.rs; regression tests cover SQPOLL autoflush and register/unregister accounting.
- Supersedes: The earlier `io_uring` path that stored bare SQEs in the pending map and treated registration as a logging-only operation.
- Next validation: Clear the existing host backend blocker so the new `io_uring` regression tests and verifier example can run, then push the fixed registration path into a true zero-syscall steady-state data path.

## 2026-03-09 | io_uring | Fixed-resource zero-syscall fast path
- Decision: Extended `io_uring` execution to resolve `IOSQE_FIXED_FILE` and `READ_FIXED`/`WRITE_FIXED` against persistent registered resource tables, added dynamic provide/remove buffer ops, and tracked zero-syscall submissions/completions behind the SQPOLL fast path.
- Rationale: The backlog item for zero-syscall high-IOPS mode required more than SQ/CQ rings alone; the ring needed a concrete steady-state path that keeps submission and completion on fixed kernel-visible resources after one-time registration.
- Tradeoffs: The fast path is expressed in the repository's in-kernel API rather than a userspace `mmap` ABI, and runtime proof is still blocked by the existing MSVC backend error; the semantic contract is now real, but full host execution evidence remains incomplete.
- Evidence: cargo check --quiet; cargo check --example phase123_verify --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --quiet; warning-only echos_gate.ps1 on src/net/io_uring.rs; new regression/example coverage exercises fixed-file plus fixed-buffer SQPOLL submission without `io_uring_enter`.
- Supersedes: The earlier io_uring decision that left zero-syscall as future work after SQ/CQ and registration persistence.
- Next validation: Remove the `offset is not a multiple of 16` backend failure so the new host-side regression can execute, then wire the same fixed-resource contract into the NVMe bridge for throughput benchmarking.

## 2026-03-09 | phase1-3 | Scheduler-memory-storage closure pass
- Decision: Added explicit EEVDF and EAS kernels (`src/task/eevdf.rs`, `src/task/eas.rs`), introduced MGLRU/PSI/KASAN memory telemetry modules (`src/memory/mglru.rs`, `src/memory/psi.rs`, `src/memory/kasan.rs`) and integrated them into reclaim + allocation hooks, implemented THP collapse/khugepaged scan path in `src/memory/thp.rs`, extended NVMe with ZNS opcodes and zone management APIs in `src/drivers/nvme.rs`, and added a QSpinlock+MCS adaptive fallback in `src/atomic_ops.rs`.
- Rationale: The first three roadmap phases still had concrete technical gaps (THP daemon path, PSI signal, MGLRU generation model, optional KASAN shadow checks, ZNS opcodes, and explicit scheduler policy kernels) that blocked an honest full-phase close.
- Tradeoffs: THP collapse currently remaps 4K pages onto a contiguous 2MB backing instead of installing a single huge PDE path; ZNS integration is command-level and controller-path complete but not yet backed by exhaustive device-matrix conformance tests.
- Evidence: Regression additions in `tests/regression_suite.rs` cover EEVDF selection/preemption, EAS placement bias, and PSI/MGLRU telemetry updates.
- Supersedes: The prior phase-close status that depended on scattered subsystem implementation without explicit closure for MGLRU/PSI/KASAN/ZNS and EEVDF/EAS kernels.
- Next validation: Run full regression and simulator gate after host backend stabilization, then attach device-backed ZNS and THP collapse latency benchmarks.

## 2026-03-09 | host-build | Windows asm fallback shims for verification path
- Decision: Added Windows-target fallback implementations for `switch_context` and `syscall_handler`, plus Windows symbol stubs for AP trampoline boundaries in `src/cpu/smp.rs`, while keeping native assembly paths active on non-Windows targets.

## 2026-03-09 | net-security-gui | Phase IV-V-VI hardening activation sweep
- Decision: Expanded `net::init` to activate high-level protocol/datapath modules (DHCP, DNS, IPv6, netfilter, eBPF, zero-copy, io_uring, HTTP/3, WireGuard, gRPC), added syscall-path Landlock enforcement with path-policy checks in `open/mkdir/rmdir/unlink/rename/truncate`, introduced hardware-backed MPK (`WRPKRU/RDPKRU`) plus software CFI scope checks and KPTI mapping guardrails, and integrated GUI input filtering + kinetic scroll pipeline in `EchInput`.

## 2026-03-09 | display | Atomic KMS frame-intent path and adaptive presentation contract
- Decision: Extended the desktop protocol with `DisplayPresentMode`, `FrameIntent`, `ScanoutCandidate`, `PlaneAssignment`, `CompositorPass`, and `VblankFeedback`; added surface metadata (`gpu_buffer_handle`, `damage_epoch`, `fence_id`) to `SurfaceManager`; introduced `services/display_atomic.rs` mailbox presenter that plans direct scanout candidates and issues atomic commits; wired `EchDisplay` to accept `SetPresentMode`/`SubmitFrameIntent` commands and to prefer atomic-present queue drain before CPU damage composition fallback; initialized DRM/GPU-native paths during display service bootstrap.
- Rationale: The shell needed a concrete transition from immediate CPU damage blit toward an intent-driven presentation model with explicit scanout planning and VBLANK-oriented feedback semantics.
- Tradeoffs: Atomic commit path currently uses DRM manager topology and per-plane state publication as the authoritative contract, while unsupported or unavailable hardware still falls back to existing framebuffer composition for continuity.
- Evidence: `cargo check -q`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/drivers/mod.rs,src/drivers/drm.rs,src/gui/protocol.rs,src/gui/surface.rs,src/gui/client.rs,src/gui/scroll_physics.rs,src/services/mod.rs,src/services/display_atomic.rs,src/services/ech_display.rs,src/services/ech_input.rs`.
- Supersedes: The previous Week-2 display path where `present()` directly performed CPU redraw without an explicit frame-intent mailbox or atomic assignment contract.
- Next validation: Attach per-panel VBLANK IRQ source to atomic present queue drain, then validate tearing absence and p99 present jitter across 60/120/240Hz targets.

## 2026-03-09 | display-hotpath | FrameScheduler state machine + transactional KMS gate
- Decision: Implemented a lock-free display hot-path skeleton around `FrameScheduler` (`Idle -> FramePending -> CommitPending -> FlipPending`) with SPSC overwrite mailbox rings for frame intents and VBLANK events in `src/services/display_atomic.rs`; extended DRM with transaction-native types (`GPUBufferHandle`, `DamageRegion`, `PlaneCandidate`, `AtomicKmsTransaction`, `VBlankEvent`) plus `commit_transaction`, `report_flip_complete`, and `rearm_crtc` in `src/drivers/drm.rs`; added minimal VBLANK IRQ capture helpers and MMIO wrapper helpers in `src/drivers/gpu_native.rs`.
- Rationale: The roadmap required explicit double-commit, flip-race, and missed-vblank control boundaries with a direct bridge from frame-intent planning to atomic KMS commit accounting.
- Tradeoffs: Until a real hardware VBLANK interrupt wire is attached, `AtomicPresenter::commit_latest` synthesizes VBLANK pulses for progress; this preserves state-machine semantics and integration contracts but is not yet silicon-timed truth.
- Evidence: `cargo check --quiet` passes; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/services/display_atomic.rs,src/drivers/drm.rs,src/drivers/gpu_native.rs` reports warning-only audits.
- Supersedes: The prior mailbox presenter flow without explicit inflight commit identity checks and without transaction-native DRM completion validation.
- Next validation: Replace synthetic VBLANK injection with MSI/IRQ-fed events from `gpu_native::dispatch_vblank_irq`, then measure `rho_drop`, flip completion order integrity, and p99 present latency across 60/120/240Hz workloads.

## 2026-03-09 | display | Vblank-gated presenter and deterministic plane-budget assignment
- Decision: Added deferred frame-intent retention in `AtomicPresenter`, vblank-edge gating against `DrmDevice::vblank_ready_at`, graph CRC generation (`VblankFeedback.graph_crc`), deterministic overlay budgeting in the planner, and plane-type-driven assignment using `DrmPlaneType` discovery. `EchDisplay::present` now avoids CPU redraw while an atomic frame is pending and drains the queue on vblank cadence.
- Rationale: The first atomic integration still allowed timer-like immediate presents and non-deterministic plane choices, which weakens tearing guarantees and reproducibility under load.
- Tradeoffs: Planner currently admits only identity+opaque overlays and rejects overlapping overlay rectangles to keep pass fallback deterministic; that can reduce direct-scanout hit-rate in heavy translucent/stacked-window scenes.
- Evidence: `cargo check -q`; local unit tests added in `src/services/display_atomic.rs` for planner/refresh/CRC behavior (host `cargo test` remains globally blocked by existing repository-wide test harness constraints unrelated to this change).
- Supersedes: The prior atomic presenter behavior with immediate commit attempts and fixed plane-id assumptions.
- Next validation: Wire real vblank IRQ feed into `poll_vblank`, then capture golden `plane assignment + pass graph + crc` traces across 60/120/240Hz and multi-monitor topologies.

## 2026-03-09 | display-hotpath | IRQ-fed vblank ingestion and monotonic commit hardening
- Decision: Hardened the Tier-1 display hot path by feeding `FrameScheduler` from `gpu_native::dispatch_vblank_irq` in `AtomicPresenter::commit_latest`, adding host/sim-only fallback VBLANK synthesis when no native GPU is registered, wiring missed-vblank recovery to `DrmDevice::abort_inflight_commit`, and enforcing monotonic commit/frame acceptance checks plus plane-type-based plane-id selection in `DrmDevice::commit_transaction`.
- Rationale: The initial state machine slice still had one correctness gap where first missed-vblank re-commit could deadlock behind stale DRM inflight expectations, and it still relied on unconditional synthetic VBLANKs instead of prioritizing the physical IRQ source.
- Tradeoffs: `dispatch_vblank_irq` still resolves device access through the global GPU registry mutex and the host fallback remains active only when no native GPU exists; this preserves bring-up continuity but is not the final lockless registry design for IRQ context.
- Evidence: `cargo check --quiet`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\services\display_atomic.rs,src\drivers\drm.rs,src\drivers\gpu_native.rs` (warning-only).
- Supersedes: The earlier display-hotpath slice that could stall re-commit after missed VBLANK and always synthesized VBLANK in the presenter path.
- Next validation: Route real MSI/IRQ registration into display service wiring, then run deterministic out-of-order completion and missed-vblank recovery stress at 60/120/240Hz while collecting `rho_drop` and `eta_scanout`.

## 2026-03-09 | shell-ui | Hybrid Titan UI contracts + command/stage shell surfaces
- Decision: Expanded GUI contracts with `ThemeMode::Auto`, motion presets, shell surface roles, icon/layout enums, `CommandPaletteAction`, and `StageSet`; added shell shortcuts (`Super+Space`, `Super+,`, `Super+\``) in `EchInput`; introduced Command Palette, Quick Settings, and Stage Rail shell windows in `gfx::velvet_glove`; connected theme switching to display service via `DisplayCommand::SetThemeMode`; and raised chrome button hit targets to `44x28` through dedicated hit-rect mapping.
- Rationale: The desktop needed one tokenized Hybrid Titan language with competitor-grade shell affordances (global command surface, staged window sets, fast settings) while preserving existing window/input flow.
- Tradeoffs: Stage Manager is workspace-backed â€œliteâ€ grouping over current app set visibility rather than full arbitrary multi-group persistence; `ThemeMode::Auto` currently resolves to light profile in shell rendering until adaptive policy inputs are wired.
- Evidence: `cargo check -q`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/gui/theme.rs,src/gui/protocol.rs,src/services/ech_input.rs,src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs,src/services/ech_display.rs,src/gui/client.rs,src/gui/window_manager.rs` (warning-only atomic/alignment audits).
- Supersedes: Prior shell state where launcher/snotifications/switcher existed but there was no command palette contract, no stage-set model, and no display-level theme mode handoff.
- Next validation: Add framebuffer golden snapshots for boot/login/desktop/palette/stage-rail in dark+light at 1080p/1440p/4K and wire adaptive theme policy inputs for `ThemeMode::Auto`.

## 2026-03-09 | shell-ui | 10-10 polish pass: contrast fallback + halo and panel interaction parity
- Decision: Added theme-level luma contrast helpers in `src/gui/theme.rs` and wired shell palette text selection through contrast-aware fallback in `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs`; refactored top-bar hit regions to shared layout geometry; raised Quick Settings row targets to `44x28`; unified palette/panel row hit-testing with rendered row rects; and moved shell geometry to `Theme::HALO_BAR_HEIGHT` and `Theme::PULSE_DOCK_HEIGHT` for consistent layout parity.
- Rationale: The previous UI pass had feature completeness, but still left readability and interaction consistency gaps under dark/light parity and compact density constraints.

- Evidence: `cargo check -q`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/gui/theme.rs,src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs`; release gate checklist added at `docs/agent/ui-10of10-release-gate.md`.
