## 2026-03-17 | win32-phase2-bound-import-metadata-pass | PE loader now retains bound-import metadata instead of treating the directory as invisible
- Decision: `src/pe_loader.rs` now parses `IMAGE_DIRECTORY_ENTRY_BOUND_IMPORT` into retained `PeBoundImport` / `PeBoundForwarderRef` metadata on `PeImage`, carries that metadata into `PeProcessDescriptor`, and pins the contract with an in-tree synthetic PE corpus. The loader no longer drops bound-import descriptors on the floor once the normal import graph has been built.
- Decision: the retained metadata is intentionally parse-only in this pass. It captures DLL name, timestamp, and forwarder-ref chain so downstream policy and visibility can reason about the bound-import directory without re-parsing the payload.
- Rationale: After forwarded exports, delay imports, and unwind visibility landed, the remaining loader truthfulness gap was that bound-import metadata still vanished during `load()`. That kept the matrix boundary honest, but it meant the repository had no mechanical retention path for one of the remaining exactness gates.
- Boundary: Faz 2 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. Bound-import timestamp validation / fallback policy is still not enforced during import resolution, and deep x64 unwind opcode and handler-data corpus closure remains open. `cargo test --lib` is still blocked by unrelated repo failures in `src/rcu.rs`, `src/memory/fibonacci_pmm.rs`, `src/task/signal.rs`, and `src/personalization.rs`.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `src/pe_loader.rs` synthetic bound-import corpus.

## 2026-03-17 | win32-phase2-com-marshaling-pass | OLE automation now carries real inter-thread marshaling state instead of only same-thread dispatch helpers
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` now exports `CoMarshalInterThreadInterfaceInStream` and `CoGetInterfaceAndReleaseStream` with a retained in-memory `IStream` implementation. The stream object supports real `IUnknown`, `ISequentialStream`, and `IStream` calls over a marshaled payload and keeps the referenced interface alive until the marshal stream is released or consumed.
- Decision: marshal streams are now stateful repository contracts, not opaque handles. `Read`, `Write`, `Seek`, `SetSize`, `CopyTo`, `Stat`, and `Clone` all operate against retained per-stream payload and ownership, while `CoGetInterfaceAndReleaseStream` tears down the one-shot marshal ticket and releases the marshal-held interface reference exactly once.
- Rationale: After apartment init/refcount and `IDispatch` helpers landed, the biggest remaining COM lie in Faz 2 was cross-thread/interface handoff. The repo could advertise COM class factories and automation dispatch, but there was still no truthful bridge for inter-thread COM transfer, which left the COM row more catalog-like than runtime-like.
- Boundary: Faz 2 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. This slice closes the marshaling-core gap but does not yet provide type-library parsing, `ITypeInfo` publication, or broader COM/OLE metadata ecosystem parity. `cargo test --lib` remains blocked by unrelated repo failures in `src/rcu.rs`, `src/memory/fibonacci_pmm.rs`, `src/task/signal.rs`, and `src/personalization.rs`.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; in-tree COM marshal stream corpus in `src/win32.rs`.

## 2026-03-17 | win32-phase2-unwind-handler-pass | NTDLL unwind bridge now returns real exception-handler metadata instead of flattening every non-leaf frame
- Decision: `src/win32_abi.rs` now understands x64 unwind handler flags beyond plain leaf-pop behavior. `RtlVirtualUnwind` preserves chained runtime-function behavior, computes the aligned unwind payload tail, returns the exception handler entry point for `UNW_FLAG_EHANDLER` / `UNW_FLAG_UHANDLER`, and surfaces the language-specific handler-data pointer through `handler_data`.
- Decision: the in-tree unwind corpus now covers a handler-bearing runtime function in addition to the leaf frame path. This pins the contract that non-leaf x64 unwind metadata is no longer reduced to "pop RIP and continue".
- Rationale: After runtime-function lookup and leaf unwind landed, the biggest remaining SEH lie was handler visibility. Windows consumers that ask `RtlVirtualUnwind` for the active handler and handler-data pointer would still see a flattened null-handler answer even when the unwind metadata explicitly carried a language handler.
- Boundary: Faz 2 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. The bridge now carries handler metadata, but deeper opcode coverage (`UWOP_SAVE_XMM*`, `UWOP_PUSH_MACHFRAME`, and wider handler-data ecosystems) remains open, and repo-wide `cargo test --lib` is still blocked by unrelated failures in `src/rcu.rs`, `src/memory/fibonacci_pmm.rs`, `src/task/signal.rs`, and `src/personalization.rs`.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; in-tree unwind handler corpus in `src/win32_abi.rs`.

## 2026-03-17 | win32-phase2-bound-import-policy-pass | PE loader now enforces bound-import timestamp fallback instead of only retaining metadata
- Decision: `src/pe_loader.rs` `PeImage` now retains the COFF `time_date_stamp`, and the normal + delay IAT resolvers now compare import descriptor timestamps against the timestamp of the already-loaded target DLL before deciding whether to keep the copied bound slot.
- Decision: matching descriptor timestamps now preserve the copied bound IAT entry instead of overwriting it, while mismatching timestamps explicitly invalidate the copied slot and fall back to fresh symbol resolution. The new synthetic corpus pins both directions with host-side `load_into_memory()` images.
- Rationale: The previous bound-import pass made the directory visible but still left loader behavior incomplete. Bound imports were parse-visible yet behaviorally inert because timestamp policy never affected IAT materialization.
- Boundary: Faz 2 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. Loader exactness no longer leaks on bound-import timestamp fallback, but deep x64 unwind opcode coverage, `user32/gdi32` long-tail, COM type-library parity, and Schannel-grade trust/proxy behavior remain open. `cargo test --lib` is still blocked before reaching the new loader corpus by unrelated repo failures in `src/rcu.rs`, `src/memory/fibonacci_pmm.rs`, `src/task/signal.rs`, and `src/personalization.rs`.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; attempted `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib load_into_memory_keeps_bound_iat_when_timestamp_matches_registered_dll -- --exact` (blocked by unrelated existing lib-test compile failures outside touched files); in-tree bound-import match/mismatch corpus in `src/pe_loader.rs`.

## 2026-03-17 | win32-phase2-http-proxy-pass | WinHTTP/WinINet proxy state now affects HTTP transport instead of only being retained metadata
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` now parses protocol-aware proxy strings (`http=...;https=...`), honors `<local>` and exact-host bypass entries, and routes plain HTTP WinHTTP/WinINet requests through the configured proxy endpoint instead of ignoring retained proxy state.
- Decision: `src/net/http.rs` gained absolute-form request-target generation for proxy requests plus HTTPS `CONNECT host:port` tunnel setup. Win32 internet wrappers now send proxy-shaped HTTP requests for cleartext traffic and establish a CONNECT tunnel before the TLS handshake on proxied HTTPS traffic.
- Rationale: Retaining proxy strings without affecting transport was still a behavioral lie. This pass moves proxy semantics from catalog state into the real request path for the subset the repo can truthfully support today.
- Boundary: Faz 2 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. Proxy routing is now behavioral for both HTTP and HTTPS, but proxy authentication/policy parity and Schannel-grade trust remain open, along with the other remaining Phase 2 blockers.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; in-tree proxy selection/bypass corpus in `src/win32.rs` and request-target corpus in `src/net/http.rs`.

## 2026-03-17 | win32-phase2-nc-typelib-unwind-pass | Phase 2 long-tail shrank across non-client UI, OLE metadata, proxy auth, and x64 unwind opcodes
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `DefWindowProcA` now applies `WM_NCCALCSIZE` against both raw `RECT*` and `NCCALCSIZE_PARAMS*`, and `WM_NCPAINT` invalidation now unions the real non-client bands instead of painting a fixed top strip. The retained invalidation map now carries the full outer-frame bounds when caption, borders, and menu chrome change.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` proxy parsing now accepts embedded credentials in `user:pass@host:port` form, translates them into Basic `Proxy-Authorization`, and `src/net/http.rs` propagates that header into HTTPS `CONNECT` tunnel setup instead of discarding proxy auth on the TLS path.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `oleaut32` now exports `LoadTypeLib` with a file-backed `ITypeLib` / `ITypeInfo` object model. The loaded typelib keeps real path/name/documentation identity, returns retained `TLIBATTR` / `TYPEATTR`, exposes `GetTypeInfoCount` / `GetTypeInfo` / `GetTypeInfoType` / `GetTypeInfoOfGuid` / `GetDocumentation` / `GetContainingTypeLib`, and no longer leaves COM metadata at the prose-only boundary.
- Decision: `src/win32_abi.rs` unwind parsing now recognizes `UWOP_SAVE_XMM128`, `UWOP_SAVE_XMM128_FAR`, and `UWOP_PUSH_MACHFRAME`. `RtlVirtualUnwind` now lets machine-frame unwinds restore `RIP`/`RSP` directly instead of always applying the generic pop-return tail after every opcode walk.
- Rationale: After proxy routing, bound-import timestamp policy, and marshal-stream work landed, the biggest remaining Phase 2 lie was no longer one subsystem but four medium ones: non-client geometry was still too coarse, proxy auth evaporated on CONNECT, COM metadata stopped at dispatch/marshal helpers, and unwind opcode coverage still flattened machine-frame paths.
- Boundary: Faz 2 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. The new typelib path is truthful and file-backed, but it is not yet a full Windows typelib parser or full COM marshaling ecosystem. Non-client behavior is much closer but still not the whole `user32/gdi32` long-tail, and Schannel/browser-grade trust parity is still open. Repo-wide `cargo test --lib` also remains blocked by unrelated compile failures outside touched files.

## 2026-03-17 | repo-libtest-compile-gate-restored | Repo-wide Windows lib-test compilation no longer fails on stale test-to-runtime contract drift
- Decision: `src/rcu.rs` test support now imports `alloc::boxed::Box` explicitly, so the RCU corpus no longer depends on an implicit prelude item that is absent in the current test configuration.
- Decision: `src/memory/fibonacci_pmm.rs` test initialization now feeds the PMM with an iterator over borrowed descriptors instead of producing copied values, restoring the API contract that `init()` consumes descriptor references rather than owned frames.

Clearing those four drift points restores the main lib-test compile gate, which is a prerequisite for truthful repo-wide validation.

## 2026-03-17 | win32-phase2-typelib-xmm-path-trust-pass | Phase 2 long-tail tightened across typelib parsing, full-context unwind restore, GDI region truthfulness, and RTC-based trust policy
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `oleaut32!LoadTypeLib` now attempts file-backed typelib metadata parsing instead of always fabricating a single synthetic type. The new `ECHOS-TLB1` text format publishes retained library name/doc/version/LCID/SYSKIND and multiple `ITypeInfo` entries, so `GetTypeInfoCount`, `GetTypeInfo`, `GetTypeInfoOfGuid`, `GetDocumentation`, and `GetContainingTypeLib` can expose file-driven identity instead of a fixed fallback blob.
- Decision: `src/win32_abi.rs` `ContextRecord` now carries `M128A` XMM state, and `RtlVirtualUnwind` restores `UWOP_SAVE_XMM128` / `UWOP_SAVE_XMM128_FAR` payloads into the context instead of only preserving scalar register state. The unwind corpus now checks both stack walk continuity and restored XMM payload contents.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` retained GDI regions now distinguish rectangular and polygon-backed shapes, preserve exclusion rectangles in clip state, and route `PtInRegion`, `RectInRegion`, `PtVisible`, `PathToRegion`, and clip-box behavior through actual region geometry instead of only bounding boxes.
- Decision: `src/net/x509.rs` certificate validation now derives wall-clock time from the RTC path instead of a fixed synthetic epoch, and revocation hard-fail semantics now treat missing live revocation evidence as a trust/policy failure (`InvalidFormat`) rather than falsely labeling the certificate as positively revoked.
- Rationale: After proxy routing, marshal streams, and machine-frame unwind landed, the remaining Phase 2 gaps were no longer "missing API" gaps but fidelity leaks: `LoadTypeLib` was file-backed without parsing, unwind restored control flow without floating-state, GDI path/clip semantics collapsed polygons into bounding boxes, and trust policy still relied on synthetic time with a misleading revocation failure code.
- Boundary: Faz 2 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. `LoadTypeLib` is now file-driven but not yet a full Windows binary typelib parser, `RtlVirtualUnwind` still does not carry full floating/context parity, `user32/gdi32` still has ordering/raster/ROP long-tail, and Schannel/browser-grade trust plus full proxy policy remain open.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs src\win32_abi.rs src\net\x509.rs`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; in-tree corpus updates in `src/win32.rs`, `src/win32_abi.rs`, and `src/net/x509.rs`.

## 2026-03-17 | win32-phase2-binary-typelib-clip-rop-pass | Phase 2 long-tail tightened across binary typelib intake, widened unwind publication, clip-aware ROP, and stricter proxy policy
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `oleaut32!LoadTypeLib` now has a binary-file-driven metadata path in addition to the `ECHOS-TLB1` text format. When the payload looks like a binary typelib container (`MSFT`, `SLTG`, or null-heavy data), echOS now harvests UTF-16LE and ASCII string candidates from the file bytes and publishes retained `ITypeLib` / `ITypeInfo` identity from those bytes instead of always collapsing to a single synthetic type.
- Decision: `src/win32_abi.rs` `ContextRecord` now mirrors restored XMM state into `vector_registers`, and `RtlVirtualUnwind` publishes that widened vector/context view after unwind application. The in-tree unwind corpus now asserts both scalar stack-walk continuity and vector-state publication for `UWOP_SAVE_XMM*` frames.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` retained GDI blit paths now use existing destination pixels plus destination clip regions during `BitBlt`, `PatBlt`, and `StretchBlt`. ROP evaluation no longer flattens destination state to zero for the exercised paths, and exclusion clips now gate writeback instead of only geometry queries.
- Decision: WinHTTP / WinINet proxy policy now treats `direct` / `none`, `all=...`, suffix wildcards, prefix wildcards, and RFC1918-style local patterns as first-class routing inputs. Proxy selection can now bypass proxied transport for loopback and private-address hosts without lying about route policy.
- Rationale: The previous Phase 2 pass made typelib/typeinfo, XMM unwind, and path/clip state more truthful, but three fidelity leaks remained obvious: binary typelibs still degenerated to stringless fallback, blit/ROP paths still ignored destination clip and destination pixels, and proxy bypass semantics were narrower than the retained policy surface advertised. This pass narrows all three without claiming the remaining Windows-long-tail as solved.
- Boundary: Faz 2 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. `LoadTypeLib` is now file-driven across both text and binary-shaped payloads, but this is still not a full Windows binary typelib parser. `RtlVirtualUnwind` now publishes wider vector state, but full floating/context parity is not closed. `user32/gdi32` still has ordering/raster/ROP long-tail beyond the exercised clip-aware blit paths, and Schannel/browser-grade trust plus PAC/WPAD/full proxy policy remain open.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs src\win32_abi.rs`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; attempted targeted `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib ... -- --exact` invocations returned `0 tests` filtered rather than executing the named corpora.

## 2026-03-17 | win32-phase2-guid-unwind-proxy-pass | Phase 2 tightened across structured binary typelib metadata, unwind transition publication, pattern-aware blits, and stricter revocation/proxy truthfulness
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` binary `LoadTypeLib` intake now understands structured metadata tokens (`guid=`, `version=`, `lcid=`, `syskind=`) in addition to free-form string harvest. File-driven binary typelibs can now publish stable library GUID/version/LCID identity instead of always hashing the file path and defaulting to `1.0`.
- Decision: `src/win32_abi.rs` `RtlVirtualUnwind` now publishes transition metadata after unwind application. `vector_control` mirrors `mx_csr`, and `last_branch_*` / `last_exception_*` are updated from the pre-unwind RIP/RSP to the post-unwind RIP/RSP so consumers can observe a wider context transition contract.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` retained blit paths now route destination DC brush color into pattern-sensitive ROP evaluation instead of forcing `pat=0` on `BitBlt` / self-blit / `StretchBlt`. This closes one of the last obvious lies in the exercised raster path: `MERGECOPY`-class ops now actually see the destination pattern input.
- Decision: WinHTTP / WinINet proxy policy now rejects PAC/WPAD-style autoconfig strings truthfully instead of silently pretending direct/proxy routing exists for them, and `src/net/x509.rs` hard-fail revocation mode now also rejects cached OCSP `Unknown` / unsuccessful statuses instead of silently accepting them.
- Rationale: The previous pass made the remaining Phase 2 tails narrower, but three fidelity leaks were still obvious in the implemented scope: binary typelibs still lost structured identity, unwind restored registers without publishing the transition strongly enough, and pattern-sensitive blits plus PAC/autoconfig proxy semantics still overclaimed support. This pass narrows those without pretending the full Windows-long-tail is solved.
- Boundary: Faz 2 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. Binary typelibs are more structured but still not parsed with a full Windows typelib reader; unwind now publishes more transition state but full floating/context parity remains open; GDI pattern/clip behavior is stronger but not the whole raster/ordering long-tail; and Schannel/browser-grade trust plus PAC/WPAD implementation parity are still open.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs src\win32_abi.rs src\net\x509.rs`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; targeted `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib ... -- --exact` invocations still returned `0 tests` filtered rather than executing the named corpora.

## 2026-03-17 | win32-phase2-typelib-member-lookup-pass | Phase 2 tightened across member-level OLE metadata lookup instead of only type-level typelib identity
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `ITypeLib::IsName` and `ITypeLib::FindName` now search member names in addition to top-level type names. When a binary/text file-driven typelib publishes `member_names`, `FindName` now returns the owning `ITypeInfo` plus the resolved member `DISPID` instead of flattening every lookup to `MEMBERID_NIL`.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `ITypeInfo::GetTypeAttr` now publishes `cFuncs` from retained member metadata, so `TYPEATTR` no longer claims a zero-function dispatch/interface surface when the file-backed typelib already exposes callable names.
- Decision: text and binary typelib corpora in `src/win32.rs` now pin `GetNames`, `GetIDsOfNames`, `IsName`, `FindName`, member documentation, and `TYPEATTR.cFuncs` together on the same retained typelib state.
- Rationale: After file-driven type-library identity and binary metadata harvesting landed, the next remaining COM/OLE lie was that name resolution still stopped at the type boundary. Metadata consumers could see types but could not discover or resolve member names through `ITypeLib`, which kept the implemented surface short of its own advertised `ITypeInfo` contract.
- Boundary: Faz 2 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. `LoadTypeLib` remains a file-driven echOS parser rather than a full Windows binary typelib reader, full COM marshaling/type-library ecosystem parity is still open, and the other Phase 2 tails (`user32/gdi32`, Schannel/browser-grade trust, deep full-context unwind) remain outside this pass.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.

## 2026-03-17 | win32-phase2-funcdesc-proxyauth-activation-pass | Phase 2 tightened across callable typelib metadata, proxy-auth semantics, and user32 activation ordering
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `ComTypeInfoState` now carries retained function/variable metadata, not only flat member-name strings. `ITypeInfo::GetFuncDesc`, `GetVarDesc`, `GetNames`, `GetIDsOfNames`, `GetDocumentation`, `ITypeLib::IsName`, and `FindName` now resolve against that retained metadata, and `TYPEATTR.cFuncs/cVars` reflects the published callable surface instead of reporting a zero-function dispatch type.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `user32::SetForegroundWindow` now emits `WM_NCACTIVATE` and `WM_ACTIVATE` transitions around active-window switches, while `SetFocus` reuses that activation path before posting `WM_SETFOCUS`. The in-tree user32 corpus now pins the observed activation/focus ordering instead of only proving retained focus state.
- Decision: `src/net/http.rs` now distinguishes proxy-auth failure from generic connection failure. Plain HTTP proxied requests and HTTPS `CONNECT` both raise `ProxyAuthenticationRequired` on `407`, `src/win32.rs` maps that class to `ERROR_INTERNET_LOGIN_FAILURE`, and `src/shell/mod.rs` reports it explicitly instead of flattening it into a generic network failure.
- Rationale: After member-level typelib lookup landed, three Phase 2 leaks were still immediate in the exercised path: callable typelib metadata still stopped short of `FUNCDESC`-style publication, user32 activation changes mutated state without telling the message queue enough, and proxy-auth failures still looked like opaque connect failures even when the proxy contract itself was what broke.
- Boundary: Faz 2 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. The typelib path remains echOS-file-driven rather than a full Windows binary typelib reader, full Schannel/browser-grade trust and PAC/WPAD implementation parity remain open, and deeper `user32/gdi32` ordering/raster long-tail plus full floating/context unwind parity still sit outside this pass.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs src\net\http.rs src\shell\mod.rs`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; attempted targeted `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib ... -- --exact` still filtered to `0 tests` instead of executing named corpora.

## 2026-03-17 | win32-phase2-pac-helpcontext-iphost-pass | Phase 2 tightened across file-driven typelib metadata, PAC/WPAD evaluation, activation repaint truthfulness, and IP hostname verification
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `LoadTypeLib` retained state now carries `help_context` and `help_file` through both text and binary file-driven metadata paths. `ITypeLib::GetDocumentation` and `ITypeInfo::GetDocumentation` no longer flatten those outputs to zero/path defaults when the typelib payload already publishes them.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` PAC/WPAD evaluation now handles top-level boolean composition (`&&`, `||`, `!`), `dnsResolve(host)` value expansion, and multiline `if { return ... }` PAC blocks instead of only single-line return expressions. WPAD autoconfig is still bounded, but it is no longer limited to one-line predicates.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `SetForegroundWindow` / `SetFocus` now post `WM_NCPAINT` alongside activation/focus transitions so non-client repaint intent becomes visible in the retained message queue instead of relying on downstream callers to infer it.
- Decision: `src/net/x509.rs` hostname verification now recognizes IPv4 subjectAltName entries (`GeneralName [7]`) and refuses to CN-fallback IP literals when SAN is absent. The WinHTTP/WinINet trust bridge therefore no longer overclaims hostname success for IP-based HTTPS endpoints.
- Rationale: After function-level typelib metadata, clip-aware blits, and proxy-auth mapping landed, three Phase 2 leaks remained obvious in the implemented surface: typelib help metadata was still discarded, PAC/WPAD evaluation was narrower than the accepted configuration surface, and HTTPS hostname checks still treated IP literals like DNS names. This pass narrows each of those while making user32 non-client repaint intent more explicit.
- Boundary: Faz 2 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. `LoadTypeLib` remains file-driven rather than a full Windows binary typelib parser, `RtlVirtualUnwind` full floating/context parity is still open, `user32/gdi32` raster-ordering long-tail is still open beyond the exercised repaint/blit paths, and Schannel/browser-grade trust plus full PAC/WPAD/browser policy parity are still not closed.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src\win32.rs src\net\x509.rs`; `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; attempted targeted `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib verify_hostname_prefers_ip_san_for_ipv4_literals -- --exact`, `... internet_proxy_multiline_pac_blocks_are_stateful -- --exact`, and `... oleaut32_binary_typelib_metadata_is_file_driven -- --exact` still returned `0 tests` filtered in this repo harness.

- 2026-03-28 | pe-win32-bootstrap-state-and-gs-contract
  - Scope: `src/task/task.rs`, `src/task/scheduler.rs`, `src/task/user.rs`, `src/pe_loader.rs`, `src/win32.rs`, `src/win32_abi.rs`
  - Change: Added first-class `Win32ThreadState` to task cold data, scheduler-visible `GS_BASE` save/restore helpers for Win32 ring3 tasks, a Win32 user-mode `iretq` bootstrap helper, and a concrete PE bootstrap memory contract (`Win32Teb`, `Win32Peb`, `Win32ProcessParameters`, `Win32BootstrapBundle`). PE process descriptors and `CreateThread` now allocate TEB/PEB/process-parameter state and publish it into task metadata; `GetProcessHeap`, `GetCurrentThreadId`, `TlsSetValue`, `TlsGetValue`, `GetLastError`, `SetLastError`, `NtCurrentTeb`, and `RtlGetCurrentPeb` consume that contract instead of only task-id keyed side maps.
  - Why: The previous PE path could parse `.pdata` and spawn tasks, but Win32 bootstrap state lived nowhere truthful. That blocked a real `GS:[0x60] -> PEB` contract, thread-local last-error/TLS ownership, and scheduler-owned GS restore seams. This pass closes the memory contract first so the launch/runtime path stops depending on ad hoc thread-key maps.
  - Boundary: PE tasks in the current repository still do not complete the full `packaged PE -> user-mapped image -> user-callable import veneer -> ring3 steady state` chain. The new `enter_win32_user_mode()` path and scheduler GS restore are wired for Win32 ring3 tasks, but the existing PE loader still resolves imports to kernel thunks and allocates images with `win32_alloc`, so full user-mode PE execution remains gated on user-address-space image mapping plus user-callable import veneers.
  - Validation: `rustfmt src\task\task.rs src\task\scheduler.rs src\task\user.rs src\pe_loader.rs src\win32.rs src\win32_abi.rs`; `cargo test -p ech_os --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib pe_loader::tests::win32_teb_keeps_peb_pointer_at_gs_0x60_contract -- --exact`; `cargo test -p ech_os --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib pe_loader::tests::spawn_process_contract_populates_win32_bootstrap_bundle -- --exact`; `cargo test -p ech_os --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib pe_loader::tests::thread_bootstrap_reuses_process_peb_and_process_parameters -- --exact`; `cargo test -p ech_os --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --bin echsdk --no-run`
- 2026-03-28 | pe-user-mapped-image-and-user-abi-veneers
  - Scope: `src/memory/mod.rs`, `src/pe_loader.rs`, `src/win32.rs`, `src/syscall.rs`, `src/task/scheduler.rs`
  - Change: Reworked packaged PE spawn so the initial image is relocated for a chosen user virtual base, staged in owned backing, and alias-mapped into a user page table instead of only living behind kernel thunks. Added a Win32 user ABI veneer layer: imported `kernel32/ntdll/user32/gdi32` call targets are now assigned service ids, emitted as tiny user-mode `syscall` veneers, and patched into the PE IAT during the user-mapped load path. The bootstrap bundle, TEB/PEB/process parameters, and veneer blob are also materialized as user-visible mappings, while `syscall_dispatcher()` routes the reserved Win32 ABI range back into repository-local Win64 target functions.
  - Why: The previous GS/TEB/PEB pass made bootstrap state truthful but still left the main Ring3 blocker intact: the PE image itself was not user-mapped and imports still pointed at kernel-address thunks. This pass closes the first coherent user-mode slice without rewriting the whole VMA/loader subsystem, so packaged PE launch now has a real user virtual image, user-callable imports, and address-space ownership that can be reused by later steady-state work.
  - Boundary: This still is not full packaged PE steady-state. `CreateThread` now inherits the active user page table/address space for Ring3 callers, but secondary-thread TEB bootstrap still reuses the older per-thread construction path and has not yet been converted into a fully user-mapped TEB blob. Delay-load imports are not yet patched through the new veneer map, and no guest packaged-PE smoke/sample has been run in this pass.
  - Validation: `cargo test -p ech_os --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --bin echsdk --no-run`; `cargo test -p ech_os --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib pe_loader::tests::win32_teb_keeps_peb_pointer_at_gs_0x60_contract -- --exact`; `cargo test -p ech_os --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib pe_loader::tests::spawn_process_contract_populates_win32_bootstrap_bundle -- --exact`; `cargo test -p ech_os --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib pe_loader::tests::thread_bootstrap_reuses_process_peb_and_process_parameters -- --exact`

- 2026-03-28 | gui-bootstrap-sync-ipc-fairness-fix
  - Scope: `src/ipc/service_ipc.rs`, `src/gfx/velvet_glove/bootstrap.rs`
  - Change: Sync service waits now yield cooperatively every 64 polling iterations instead of remaining pure spin-only, desktop bootstrap retry backoff now uses `preemptible_schedule()` instead of `scheduler::sleep(1)`, and compat sync wrappers dispatch directly into kernel-resident bound endpoints before falling back to queued runtime IPC. A timed-out sync wait also emits a precise serial marker with pid, service, and request id so wallpaper-only boots can be tied to the blocking service edge.
  - Why: QEMU logs showed the desktop freezing immediately after `[DESKTOP] session bootstrap step=connect` while services were already initialized. The old path entered sync IPC before the compositor main loop began yielding, then used an idle-sleep backoff that is invalid in this bootstrap context. That combination starved low-priority service tasks and left the system rendering only the wallpaper pass.
  - Boundary: This closes the early bootstrap starvation path for kernel-resident services; isolated user-published endpoints still use the queued runtime path by design. The change does not remove sync IPC from desktop bootstrap or rebalance service priorities globally. If a service still fails to answer, the new timeout marker should identify the blocking edge directly in serial logs.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi -q`; `.\run_qemu.ps1 -Mode uefi -Profile fast -Headless -NoBuild`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\ipc\service_ipc.rs,src\gfx\velvet_glove\bootstrap.rs,docs\agent\decision-log.md`

This pass closes the first runtime accessibility slice where the control plane and the actual key-routing semantics agree.

Slow keys currently gate non-modifier key presses with a fixed hold threshold and intentionally avoid delaying pure modifier keys so shell shortcuts and focus recovery do not become unusable.

- 2026-03-29 | gui-window-rule-stage-set-display-policy-slice
  - Scope: `src/gui/protocol.rs`, `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs`, `src/gfx/velvet_glove/app_runtime.rs`, `src/gfx/velvet_glove/launch.rs`, `src/gfx/velvet_glove/session_runtime.rs`, `src/services/ech_display.rs`
  - Change: Wired the first real product slice for the remaining GUI parity backlog. `WindowRule` state is now actually consumed during app launch/restore so matched rules can drive `floating`, `pinned`, `scratchpad`, `pseudotile`, snap-template geometry, and per-app restore behavior instead of sitting idle in `EchShell`. Pinned windows now survive workspace switches visibly, relayout preserves existing window flags, and manual snap now hardens windows into floating snap-state so tiling does not immediately erase the user's action. `StageSet` was widened from a thin workspace label into a richer session snapshot carrying `workspace_id`, `monitor_id`, `snap_groups`, and `restore` semantics; the overview rail now renders live mini-layout previews and syncs its stage-set state back into the shell service and stage-set policy. On the display side, `DisplayProfile` sanitize/apply is now capability-first and runtime-effective: outputs are normalized per connected-output count, invalid primary/mirror state is repaired deterministically, primary scale is pushed into the global scaling runtime, and effective `VRR`/`HDR` policy is derived explicitly and surfaced in debug diagnostics instead of being implied by raw user preference alone.
  - Why: The previous GUI work had the right nouns but too many of them were still decorative. This pass closes the highest-value wiring seam by making window rules, stage sets, overview state, and display profile policy alter actual runtime behavior rather than just exist as persisted shell metadata.
  - Boundary: This is still not the whole Titan Warpath backlog. It does not yet implement true multi-head scanout, real HDR metadata programming, hardware-backed VRR state transitions, Win32 DPI virtualization inside IronShim, or a full user-facing rule editor. The monitor policy path is real at the shell/display policy layer, but on single-framebuffer/QEMU paths it projects outputs onto partitioned logical regions rather than true independent scanout heads.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`
- 2026-03-29 | gui-display-runtime-policy-and-bridge-dpi-contract
  - Scope: `src/gui/launch_pipeline.rs`, `src/gfx/velvet_glove/launch.rs`, `src/runtime_layer/{runtime_model.rs,runtime_state.rs}`, `src/ironshim_app.rs`, `src/services/ech_display.rs`
  - Change: Promoted external-runtime display metadata into a typed launch contract instead of leaving Win32/POSIX bridge launches blind to shell scale state. `LaunchSession` now carries an `ExternalDisplayContract` with output binding, UI/text/cursor scale, and explicit DPI virtualization mode, and that contract is copied into `BrokeredLaunch` and the IronShim launch envelope so bridge consumers can stay geometrically correct even when the shell runs fractional scale or accessibility text/cursor overrides. On the display side, `EchDisplay` now maintains an explicit runtime activity model (`idle`, `interactive`, `animation`, `fullscreen`), computes per-output effective VRR/HDR state from capability plus activity instead of raw preference alone, projects that state into presenter mode/debug diagnostics, and applies monitor-policy rescue when output topology or workspace binding changes would otherwise strand windows off-screen. Mirror targets now inherit workspace binding from their source output deterministically.
  - Why: The previous display-profile slice still treated runtime behavior as a mostly static preference surface. That was not enough for the harder GUI parity goals: Win32 bridge launches needed typed scale truth, and display policy needed a real effective-mode layer plus rescue semantics when monitor policy changes land at runtime.
  - Boundary: This closes the shell/display policy slice, not the whole hardware wall. HDR remains fail-closed without real connector metadata programming, VRR is still a compositor/runtime policy state rather than a proven driver-level adaptive-sync path, and IronShim still does not execute full Windows DPI virtualization logic internally. The new contract makes those next steps explicit and typed instead of implicit.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\gui\launch_pipeline.rs,src\gfx\velvet_glove\launch.rs,src\runtime_layer\runtime_model.rs,src\runtime_layer\runtime_state.rs,src\ironshim_app.rs,src\services\ech_display.rs,docs\agent\decision-log.md`

The Files app now preloads small thumbnails for previewable image entries during refresh and renders those actual pixels in-row through a new `Canvas::draw_argb_bitmap` path instead of always falling back to synthetic letter badges. File association and thumbnail classification now recognize `.qoi` alongside existing image extensions.
  - Why: The GUI stack already had strong scene, chrome, and policy layers, but image-bearing shell surfaces were still mostly placeholders. This slice gives the shell a real asset decode and bitmap-scale path that is bare-metal-safe, useful for DPI fallback work, and immediately visible in the built-in Files surface.
  - Boundary: This is not yet a full wallpaper or Win32 DPI virtualization solution. PNG/JPEG/WebP are still unsupported in the kernel path, and the new scaler is currently exercised through shell thumbnails rather than a generalized desktop wallpaper or IronShim blit pipeline. `edid-rs` was intentionally not wired this round because the current UEFI/display path does not yet expose a trustworthy raw EDID blob source to the GUI stack.

The old procedural fallback remains only as a safety path if the profiled wallpaper surface cannot render.

- 2026-03-29 | gui-native-scene-bitmap-virtualization-slice
  - Scope: `src/posix/native_scene_bridge.rs`, `src/runtime.rs`, `src/runtime_layer/{runtime_model.rs,runtime_state.rs,window_session_contract.rs,native_scene_contract.rs}`
  - Change: Bound per-window external display metadata into the runtime session spine and taught the native scene bridge to honor `BitmapScale` at runtime. `WindowSessionSpec` now carries an `ExternalDisplayContract`, the runtime/window-session contracts expose an explicit `attach_window_session_with_display(...)` path, and native-scene window attachment now records the launch-time display contract instead of dropping it on the floor. In the bridge itself, `sys_native_window_create` now scales physical create geometry while preserving logical content dimensions for the app-facing handle, `sys_native_scene_commit` scales bounds, clips, radii, text widths, and raster payloads through the shared ARGB resize lane, and `sys_native_event_poll` maps pointer/key coordinates back into logical space so input geometry stays coherent under bitmap virtualization.
- 2026-04-12 | p0-wireguard-ebpf-syscall-dispatch-hardening
  - Scope: `src/net/wireguard.rs`, `src/net/ebpf.rs`, `src/posix.rs`, `src/win32.rs`
  - Change: Closed four release-plan P0 security gaps in one pass. WireGuard transport now uses authenticated ChaCha20-Poly1305 framing with AAD-bound 16-byte headers, fail-closed decrypt (`CryptoError` on tag mismatch), strict receiver-index checks, and X25519-based handshake key derivation with pending-initiator state tracking. eBPF execution and verification now reject invalid jump targets, enforce stack access-size bounds for load/store, and remove the permissive nonzero program-type bypass. POSIX `sys_read`/`sys_write` now reject non-user buffer ranges before user memory access. Win32 user ABI dispatch now rejects non-kernel function targets before indirect call dispatch.
  - Why: P0 findings were centered on fail-open crypto paths, verifier/runtime control-flow escapes, unchecked user-pointer ingress, and indirect-call target trust. These are high-impact primitives that can turn parser or ABI bugs into direct privilege or memory-safety failures.
  - Boundary: This slice hardens the identified P0 paths but does not claim full protocol parity (WireGuard remains a repository implementation, not full upstream feature equivalence) and does not resolve pre-existing global warning inventories (atomic ordering/alignment/raw allocation reviews).
  - Validation: `cargo check --workspace`; `get_errors` on touched files (`src/net/wireguard.rs`, `src/net/ebpf.rs`, `src/posix.rs`, `src/win32.rs`) -> no file diagnostics; `./.qoder/skills/echos-architect/scripts/echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/net/wireguard.rs,src/net/ebpf.rs,src/posix.rs,src/win32.rs`
  - Metrics: touched-file gate run reported warnings only (no hard failures); workspace compile-check finished successfully.
  - Why: The earlier display-policy work made external display scale a typed launch contract, but the native scene bridge still behaved as if every runtime were always 100%. That left DPI virtualization decorative and guaranteed a geometry mismatch once scaled shells or bridge-compatible runtimes entered the path.
  - Boundary: This closes the bridge-compatible scene/input slice, not the full Win32/IronShim DPI war. The current consumer is the native scene bridge; it does not yet prove that packaged Win32/ELF runtimes route through the same ABI handler, and it does not add HDR metadata programming or driver-level VRR behavior. The contract is now preserved end-to-end and the scene bridge has deterministic scaling semantics ready for wider bridge adoption.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`
- 2026-04-06 | gui-shell-completeness-overlay-watchdog-slice
  - Scope: `src/gui/protocol.rs`, `src/gfx/shell_invalidation.rs`, `src/gfx/velvet_glove/{bootstrap.rs,session_runtime.rs}`, `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs`
  - Change: Closed a large shell-completeness gap by adding two new real shell-owned overlays, `Clipboard History` and `Screenshot Shelf`, and then wiring them through bootstrap window creation, invalidation, animation, visibility restore, accessibility publication, command-palette actions, quick-settings toggles, pointer/keyboard routing, and raster paint paths. Clipboard entries can now be promoted back to current clipboard state from a dedicated shell surface, and screenshot entries can be exported directly to `/workspace/capture-<id>.ppm` from a dedicated capture shelf. The quick-settings control plane now exposes those two lanes directly, and command-palette actions gained explicit open/clear/save routes for them. In parallel, the session runtime gained a watchdog stabilization pass that collapses stale transient overlays, recovers top-bar/task-strip visibility, force-hides lock screen leakage into the desktop state, and repairs wallpaper-only / shell-sprawl drift without introducing a second composition truth.
  - Why: The shell already had strong services for clipboard and capture plus an increasingly complete overlay system, but the product still lacked visible first-class shell surfaces for those lanes and remained vulnerable to state drift where multiple transient overlays or an accidental wallpaper-only desktop could survive longer than one tick. This slice converts those latent capabilities into actual product surfaces and hardens the shell discipline around them.
  - Boundary: This does not finish the entire parity war. The new overlays are shell-native control surfaces, not a full screenshot editor or a persistent clipboard manager with pinning/search. The watchdog heals transient shell drift, but it is not a substitute for exhaustive multi-hour soak or full packaged Win32/IronShim parity. HDR/VRR, true multi-monitor scanout parity, and complete bridge-side DPI virtualization are still separate battles.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\gui\protocol.rs,src\gfx\shell_invalidation.rs,src\gfx\velvet_glove\bootstrap.rs,src\gfx\velvet_glove.rs,src\gfx\velvet_glove\session_runtime.rs`
