  - Scope: `src/gfx/velvet_glove_registry.rs`, `src/gfx/velvet_glove/launch.rs`, `src/gfx/velvet_glove/app_runtime.rs`
  - Change: Promoted a curated external developer-tool catalog into the desktop launch registry instead of leaving the shell limited to built-ins plus browsers. The registry now carries first-class shell-owned PE candidates for `Helix`, `Yazi`, `Zellij`, `bottom`, `GitUI`, `Posting`, `Glow`, `ripgrep`, `fd`, and `bat`, each with stable aliases and canonical candidate paths under `/downloads`, `/programs`, and `/apps`. The desktop launch path now exposes that larger registry directly, and the terminal command surface gained a generic `open <app>` resolver so those curated tools can be launched through the same registry/palette semantics as the rest of the shell instead of requiring bespoke one-off browser cases.
  - Why: The user explicitly wanted echOS to stop treating serious ready-made tools as an abstract future idea and start using them as the ecosystem floor for shell breadth and power-user credibility. This slice opens the real launch/resolve path inside echOS now, so dropping the binaries into canonical locations immediately makes them discoverable and launchable through existing shell surfaces.
  - Boundary: This does not fetch or package the binaries itself. It closes the OS-side registry, alias, and launch path, not the download/distribution problem. The curated tools are wired as shell-owned external apps; full native ports, richer launcher UI surfacing, and automated GitHub release ingestion are separate follow-up battles.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`
- 2026-04-06 | gui-curated-app-appliance-bundle-ingestion
  - Scope: `src/main.rs`, `src/boot/appliance.rs`, `src/gfx/velvet_glove/session_runtime.rs`, `scripts/build_vm_appliance.py`, `run_qemu.ps1`, `scripts/package_curated_apps.ps1`
  - Change: Added a real appliance-side ingestion lane for curated external tools instead of relying on ad hoc filesystem drops. The UEFI loader now scans `EFI\\BOOT\\APP0001.BHD` through `APP0032.BHD` deterministically and seeds those bundles into boot appliance state, `boot::appliance` now carries a dedicated queue for curated app bundles, and the shell session runtime installs them through the same signed-package path used by other packaged applications once the app basket is ready. The appliance image builder and QEMU runner now accept repeated curated bundle inputs and place them into the ESP automatically, and a new packaging script downloads curated Windows release binaries, writes `echos-app.toml` manifests, signs them through `echsdk`, and emits ready-to-ingest `.bhd` bundles under `artifacts/curated-bundles`.
  - Why: The previous curated-tool work only solved discovery inside echOS. That still left a manual gap between "the shell knows about Helix/Yazi/Zellij" and "the appliance actually boots with those applications installed". This slice closes that distribution seam so curated external tools can become part of a reproducible appliance image instead of a hand-copied host-side ritual.
  - Boundary: This does not prove every downloaded tool runs correctly inside echOS after installation. It closes packaging, ESP staging, and install ingestion. Runtime compatibility remains bounded by the existing PE/bridge layer, and release-download success still depends on host network access plus the continued shape of upstream GitHub release assets. The packaging script now pins signing to a host `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` `echsdk.exe` binary and emits manifest text as BOM-free UTF-8 so package parsing does not fail under the bare manifest parser. The current curated bundle set is proven for `Helix`, `Yazi`, `Zellij`, `bottom`, `GitUI`, `Glow`, `ripgrep`, `fd`, and `bat`; `Posting` remains best-effort because the upstream repo/crate path is not presently consumable by the scripted installer.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`
- 2026-04-06 | package-seed-store-abstraction-first-slice
  - Scope: `src/security/{mod.rs,seed_store.rs}`, `src/gfx/velvet_glove/session_runtime.rs`
  - Change: Introduced a first-class `SeedStore` abstraction instead of hard-wiring curated app installation to the ESP preload queue alone. The new `security::seed_store` module defines typed seed origins and a bundle-source contract, then wires two concrete sources: the existing boot-appliance ESP queue and a mounted VFS-backed seed-store scan over `/seed/apps` and `/system/seed/apps`. The desktop session runtime now installs seed bundles from that merged source set and logs/installs them under a generalized `[SEED]` lane rather than a special-case curated-ESP path. Filesystem seed entries are consumed once by path so the shell does not attempt re-install on every `AppBasketReady` tick.
  - Why: The previous `.bhd in ESP` solution was acceptable for bootstrap and appliance seeding, but it was too narrow to evolve into the long-term package-source architecture. This slice starts the migration without breaking the existing path: ESP preload remains valid, but the runtime now understands the idea of a mounted seed store and can consume package seeds from outside the EFI partition.
  - Boundary: This is not yet a real seed partition driver, loopback image backend, or content-addressed package store. The mounted seed store is currently a VFS path convention, not a dedicated block-device or partition contract. Filesystem seeds are consumed on first scan, so failed/corrupt bundles do not auto-retry until reboot or path reset; that tradeoff was chosen to avoid repeated install spam in the current shell tick model.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`
- 2026-04-06 | package-seed-partition-loop-image-on-demand-reference-install
  - Scope: `src/security/seed_store.rs`, `src/security/package.rs`, `src/gfx/velvet_glove/{launch.rs,app_runtime.rs,session_runtime.rs}`, `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs`, `src/runtime.rs`, `scripts/package_curated_apps.ps1`
  - Change: Advanced the seed-package path from bootstrap ingestion toward a durable distribution model. `security::seed_store` now maintains a persistent seed catalog instead of returning eager-install byte queues, discovers real seed roots from the live mount table plus legacy fallback roots, and adds a loop-image backend with a simple `echSID01` container format so curated bundles can ride as a single image file under mounted seed storage. The catalog supports on-demand installation by query, preferring mounted partition sources over loop images and ESP-resident seeds. On the package side, packaged-app installs now distinguish copied bundle archives from reference-backed installs: mounted seed `.bhd` files can be adopted by reference, loop-image entries can be referenced by `(path, offset, length)`, verification reads back through that backing descriptor, and package removal only deletes copied archives from `/apps/.bundles`. Shell launch resolution, terminal `open <app>`, and packaged PE smoke now all route through a lazy install path that materializes a seeded app only when a real launch demands it, while boot-time eager curated install was removed. The curated packaging script now also emits `curated-seed.img` beside individual `.bhd` bundles so the loop-image backend has a host-side producer.
  - Why: The ESP preload mechanism solved bootstrap distribution but carried the exact scaling risks called out later: EFI partition growth, boot-time I/O inflation, and archive duplication into the package store. This slice moves echOS onto the right operational footing without abandoning the working bootstrap path: mounted seed storage becomes first-class, loop images become possible without teaching VFS a block-loop driver, and archive dedup starts by removing the second bundle copy for reference-backed installs.
  - Boundary: This is still not the final content-addressed store. Extracted payload files are still materialized into `/apps/<package>/...`, so dedup currently removes duplicate archived bundle storage, not per-file payload duplication. The "real seed partition" backend is implemented as live mount-table discovery plus VFS reads from mounted seed roots; it does not add a new partition driver in this slice. Loop-image support is container-based rather than a general loopback block device, by design, to avoid entangling the package-source transition with a new storage driver contract.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`
- 2026-04-06 | package-content-store-and-seed-state-machine
  - Scope: `src/security/{package.rs,seed_store.rs}`, `src/runtime.rs`
  - Change: Closed the next package-distribution seam by moving packaged payloads into a hash-addressed content store and hardening seed catalog state. Packaged-app installation now writes each payload once into `/apps/.content/<shard>/<sha256>.<ext>` and records a per-package payload map instead of duplicating `entry` and `app.manifest.bin` under package-private paths. Launch resolution now points at those content blobs directly, and package removal frees a blob only when no remaining installed package still references it. In parallel, the seed catalog gained a concrete state machine with `Available`, `Installed`, `Retryable`, and `Quarantined` states driven by install failures and repeated parse/read faults; repeated seed failures now quarantine that source after bounded retries instead of causing infinite retry churn. This turns the seed/package path into a real content-sharing runtime rather than only an archive-sharing one.
  - Why: Archive-by-reference removed one obvious duplication class, but it still left extracted payload bytes duplicated per package and gave the seed catalog no durable health model. The content store removes the next storage penalty tier for the current packaged-app shape, and the seed state machine gives install-on-demand a real failure policy instead of blind optimism.
  - Boundary: This is a content-addressed payload store for packaged-app payload blobs, not a fully general object store with GC metadata on disk. Blob reference counting is derived from the in-memory installed-package registry at removal time, not from a persisted refcount ledger. Seed state is now explicit in the catalog, but no dedicated shell UI/editor for that catalog was added in this slice.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`
- 2026-04-06 | drivers-general-loopback-block-device
  - Scope: `src/drivers/{mod.rs,driver_model.rs,loopback.rs}`, `src/shell/mod.rs`
  - Change: Promoted loop images from a seed-install-only container trick into a general file-backed block-device lane. `drivers::loopback` now exposes attach/open/list/flush/detach semantics over arbitrary image files, registers each attached image in the driver model as a block-class device, supports block read/write plus writeback through the store service, and records live mount-point ownership. A shell-visible `loop` command family now manages the lifecycle (`list`, `attach`, `flush`, `detach`, `mount`, `umount`) so mounted images are no longer trapped behind seed-package plumbing. The mount helper currently bridges FAT32 images into both the classic mount table and the unified VFS, which is enough to make loop-backed appliance, recovery, and data images consumable through the normal filesystem path.
  - Why: The earlier loop-image work was intentionally narrow and package-oriented. Leaving it there would have kept image-backed storage as a one-off seed backend, forcing future installer, recovery, or appliance flows to reinvent image attach/mount logic. This slice makes loopback a reusable storage primitive instead of package-specialized glue.
  - Boundary: The generalization is at the block-device and image-lifecycle layer, not yet at the full filesystem matrix. The built-in mount helper is currently FAT32-only; exFAT and other filesystems still need their own VFS wiring before loopback can mount them generically. Large images no longer force eager whole-image residency: attach now selects `resident` mode only below a bounded threshold and otherwise uses `paged` backing with block-cache plus dirty-block writeback, but large FAT32 mounts still fail closed above a safe snapshot cap until the FAT path is rewritten to consume a block device directly instead of a full-image byte slice.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\drivers\mod.rs,src\drivers\loopback.rs,src\shell\mod.rs,docs\agent\decision-log.md`
- 2026-04-06 | fat32-loopback-block-backed-mount
  - Scope: `src/fs/{fat.rs,vfs_unified.rs}`, `src/drivers/loopback.rs`
  - Change: Removed the last large-image snapshot requirement from the FAT32 loopback mount path. `MountedFat32` no longer assumes an in-memory `Arc<Vec<u8>>`; it now carries a typed storage backend that can be either resident bytes or a loopback block device name. FAT32 mount gained a loopback-backed attach path that parses the boot sector from block `0`, and the VFS FAT32 readers now fetch FAT tables, clusters, directory chains, and file data through that storage backend instead of direct slice indexing. As a result, large paged loopback images can be mounted and traversed without cloning the whole image into kernel heap during mount.
  - Why: The previous loopback split fixed attach-time memory pressure but left mount-time parity broken because FAT32 still demanded a full-image snapshot. That meant big images were safe to attach but not safe to mount, which preserved the worst practical limitation. This slice moves the filesystem seam down to the block-storage boundary where it belongs.
  - Boundary: This closes the FAT32 loopback path only. exFAT still lacks VFS wiring, and FAT32 reads are now block-backed but still allocate per-table/per-cluster buffers instead of a finer-grained persistent metadata cache. The design intentionally favors bounded heap over minimum copy count in this step.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\fs\fat.rs,src\fs\vfs_unified.rs,src\drivers\loopback.rs,docs\agent\decision-log.md`
- 2026-04-06 | loopback-mount-matrix-and-seed-catalog-shell-surface
  - Scope: `src/{drivers/loopback.rs,fs/{fat.rs,mount.rs,vfs_unified.rs},security/{package.rs,seed_store.rs},gfx/velvet_glove.rs,gfx/velvet_glove/launch.rs}`
  - Change: Closed the next generic-storage and package-surface seams together. Loopback mount dispatch now accepts explicit or auto-detected filesystem type across the real wired image backends (`fat32`, `exfat`, `ext4`, `ntfs`), exFAT gained the same mounted-storage/VFS path that FAT32 already had, and loopback unmount now tears down filesystem-side mount instances instead of only dropping VFS rows. FAT/exFAT mount registries were made sparse-slot aware so unmount can release indexes without shifting live instances. On the product side, Seed Catalog is no longer only a backend state machine: it is now a dedicated shell-owned overlay with its own invalidation lane, accessibility node, bootstrap window, quick-settings route, command-palette route, keyboard/pointer controls, and raster surface. The catalog now surfaces version-aware package state (`Available`, `Installed`, `UpdateAvailable`, `Retryable`, `Quarantined`), can open already-installed apps directly, and will perform an in-place package refresh when the seeded version is newer than the installed one by removing the old package before reinstall.
  - Why: The user explicitly asked for both halves of parity: a genuinely general loopback mount lane and a visible package-source control surface. Before this pass, exFAT was still outside the loopback-backed VFS path, unmount leaked backend mount instances, and the seed/package stack had no first-class shell UI despite having enough backend state to deserve one.
  - Boundary: This closes the mount matrix for the repository's currently real loopback-backed filesystem backends: `fat32`, `exfat`, `ext4`, and `ntfs`. It does not claim parity for `xfs` or `btrfs` because the unified VFS backends for those families are not implemented upstream in this tree; their enum slots remain intentionally fail-closed rather than faking support. ext4/ntfs loop mounts still use resident image snapshots because those filesystem implementations are themselves snapshot-backed today.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`
- 2026-04-06 | seed-hash-queue-retry-ui-and-uefi-curated-preload-cut
  - Scope: `src/{security/seed_store.rs,gfx/velvet_glove.rs,gfx/velvet_glove/session_runtime.rs,gfx/image_assets.rs,main.rs}`, `Cargo.toml`, `scripts/build_vm_appliance.py`
  - Change: Hardened the seed-package path into a real asynchronous catalog instead of a fully eager scanner. `security::seed_store` now keeps a cached inspection map, queues slow bundle inspections through a bounded hash/inspect work queue, exposes `HashPending` state, records exponential retry windows, and supports manual retry for retryable or quarantined seeds. The Seed Catalog shell surface now renders pending counts, retry countdowns, and manual retry controls directly. To unblock UEFI validation, the image scaler dropped the `fast_image_resize` dependency in favor of a repository-local bilinear ARGB scaler after the UEFI-target rustc/LLVM pipeline proved unstable on that crate. The appliance image builder now sizes ESP dynamically and scales FAT16 cluster geometry so larger curated payload sets still produce a bootable image. Finally, the UEFI loader no longer reads every `APP0001.BHD`..`APP0032.BHD` curated bundle into heap at loader entry; it now only probes file sizes and logs deferred curated payload presence, leaving actual seed installation to the runtime seed-store paths instead of boot-time resident bundle allocation.
  - Why: The remaining parity gaps were no longer design gaps, they were runtime gaps: hash/inspection churn needed bounded work and visible retry semantics, and real boot validation was blocked by the loader allocating multi-megabyte curated bundle payloads before the kernel even reached desktop initialization. Cutting that eager path fixes the worst-case boot memory failure without regressing the runtime package architecture.
  - Boundary: The UEFI loader fix removes boot-time curated bundle residency; it does not magically create a post-boot ESP package source. Real curated-package availability after boot still depends on mounted seed roots or loop-image sources, which remain the canonical runtime path. The seed catalog now has parity-grade state semantics and UI controls, but the runtime package source itself is still bounded by which seed stores are actually mounted in the running appliance.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`
- 2026-04-07 | desktop-launcher-default-hidden-and-scene-backed-shortcuts
  - Scope: `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs`
  - Change: Removed the persistent `Desktop Launcher` overlay from the clean-desktop default path and stopped treating it like a primary focus target. The launcher window now boots hidden, is forced back to hidden if sync reports it visible while not desired, and is no longer auto-restored as a visible overlay during shell window recovery. Workspace switches now refocus the top bar instead of the hidden launcher. In parallel, the desktop shortcut layer was moved off the full-frame raster path: wallpaper/desktop invalidation now emits a scene-backed shortcut surface instead of allocating a full-screen `Vec<u32>` and wrapping it in a raster scene every time. That cuts the hot-path 1920x1080 pixel buffer allocation that previously matched the `8294400 bytes failed` post-desktop panic and removes a major source of desktop sluggishness under QEMU TCG.
  - Why: The user-visible symptoms were tightly coupled: an unwanted launcher overlay kept resurfacing on the desktop, and the compositor was still paying for full-screen desktop raster allocations after `app-basket-ready`, which both slowed interaction and could panic under memory pressure. Hiding the launcher at the policy level and moving desktop shortcuts to retained scene objects attacks both symptoms at their real seam instead of papering over them in UI logic.
  - Boundary: This removes the default visible launcher surface from the desktop flow; it does not create a new launcher UX replacement beyond the existing dock/palette/shortcut routes. The desktop shortcut layer is now scene-backed, but other shell overlays still use raster surfaces where their bounds are already small and bounded.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `.\run_qemu.ps1 -Mode uefi -Profile fast -Headless -NoBuild`
- 2026-04-07 | panic-stop-broadcast-and-page-backed-large-gui-surfaces
  - Scope: `src/{main.rs,cpu/smp.rs,interrupts/mod.rs,gui/{surface.rs,surface_memory.rs},gfx/velvet_glove.rs,gfx/velvet_glove/app_runtime.rs}`
  - Change: Added a real panic-stop broadcast path and moved large GUI pixel ownership off the generic heap. The panic handler now marks a global SMP panic-stop flag, best-effort pokes other CPUs via IPI, and halts the faulting CPU through a dedicated self-stop routine. Timer and IRQ dispatch paths now check that flag and self-halt instead of continuing scheduler/softirq traffic after a kernel panic. On the render side, `SharedSurfaceMemory` now uses page-backed contiguous DMA pages for large surfaces, small surfaces remain heap-backed with explicit fallible reservation, and `SurfaceManager` no longer keeps a duplicate full pixel buffer alongside the shared data-plane store. In the shell app lane, `Canvas` now uses page-backed storage above the same threshold and app runtime renderers present directly from `&[u32]` slices instead of materializing a second full-window `Vec<u32>` at the end of every render.
  - Why: The earlier desktop-shortcut fix removed one 1920x1080 allocation vector, but the user correctly pointed out that opening large shell windows such as Settings could still trigger the same class of contiguous heap failure through `Canvas::new(...)` and duplicate surface storage. Separately, a panic on one CPU still left the other cores alive long enough to look like a frozen or paused QEMU session. This slice attacks both failure classes at the ownership boundary: keep large live pixel buffers out of TLSF/slab-style heap paths, and stop all CPUs from continuing normal work once the kernel has already declared panic.
  - Boundary: Large live GUI surfaces are now page-backed, but some raster-producing helper functions still return `Vec<u32>` for bounded shell overlays and legacy snapshot paths. `SurfaceSnapshot` cloning remains heap-backed at the API boundary, so this is not yet a fully page-backed retained-scene architecture. The panic-stop broadcast is best-effort and piggybacks on interrupt/timer entry for remote CPUs; it is not a formal NMI-based crash rendezvous protocol.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi -q`; `.\run_qemu.ps1 -Mode uefi -Profile fast -Headless -NoBuild`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\main.rs,src\cpu\smp.rs,src\interrupts\mod.rs,src\gui\surface_memory.rs,src\gui\surface.rs,src\gfx\velvet_glove.rs,src\gfx\velvet_glove\app_runtime.rs`
- 2026-04-07 | shared-surface-zero-copy-join
  - Scope: `src/{gui/surface_memory.rs,gui/surface.rs,services/ech_display.rs}`
  - Change: Removed the last large shared-surface heap clone from the display join path. `SharedSurfaceMemory` now exposes a closure-based pixel borrow, `SurfaceManager::snapshot(...)` stops materializing shared surfaces into owned pixel vectors, and `EchDisplay::snapshot_join(...)` carries pixel-backed windows either as owned small vectors or as `Arc<SharedSurfaceMemory>` references. Composition now draws shared pixel windows directly from the locked shared slice on demand instead of cloning the entire framebuffer payload into a temporary `Vec<u32>` every present cycle.
  - Why: Page-backed canvases fixed the producer-side 1080p allocation, but the display-side join still cloned every shared pixel window into heap-owned memory before composition. That preserved the same worst-case memory pressure and undermined the large-surface work. This slice moves the remaining high-cost ownership boundary into a borrowed/shared model where the compositor pays only the lock and draw cost.
  - Boundary: Pixel windows that are not shared-surface-backed still flow as owned `Vec<u32>` buffers, and shell overlay paint helpers that deliberately return raster vectors remain unchanged. This change closes the expensive shared-surface clone path; it does not yet make every raster path zero-copy.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi -q`; `.\run_qemu.ps1 -Mode uefi -Profile fast -Headless -NoBuild`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\gui\surface_memory.rs,src\gui\surface.rs,src\services\ech_display.rs`
- 2026-04-07 | allocator-doctrine-v3-wrappers-governance-and-gui-routing
  - Scope: `src/{allocator/{mod.rs,doctrine.rs},gui/surface_memory.rs,gfx/velvet_glove.rs}`, `.qoder/skills/echos-architect/scripts/echos_gate.ps1`
  - Change: Introduced a dedicated allocation-doctrine module with a deliberately capped seven-wrapper surface (`alloc_small_object`, `alloc_heap_buffer`, `alloc_large_pages`, `alloc_surface_pixels`, `alloc_dma_buffer`, `alloc_kernel_stack`, `alloc_snapshot_clone`) plus centralized thresholds, allocation-class telemetry, and a deny-by-default exception registry with subsystem quotas. The registry now validates required metadata and limits non-debug exceptions per subsystem so the wrapper layer cannot silently turn into an allocator marketplace. Large GUI pixel ownership was then rerouted through doctrine primitives: `SharedSurfaceMemory` now allocates and resizes through `alloc_surface_pixels`, and shell `Canvas` surfaces now route through the same doctrine buffer instead of carrying a second local page-backed policy. Snapshot clones for raster export paths are explicitly tagged and counted, while large unreviewed clones remain doctrine violations. The local gate also gained doctrine-specific checks for wrapper-count overflow and raw allocation/snapshot review sites.
  - Why: The repository already had the right low-level pieces (`PMM`, `TLSF`, `slab`, page-backed GUI buffers), but allocator choice still lived in ad hoc subsystem code. That was enough to regress back into large heap allocations or uncontrolled snapshot clones as the shell grew. This slice makes allocation intent explicit and reviewable without creating a god-wrapper API, and it moves the hot GUI allocation boundaries onto that shared policy immediately.
  - Boundary: This is a real doctrine implementation and not just documentation, but it does not yet eliminate every raw allocation helper in the tree. The doctrine is currently wired through the primary large GUI surface paths and telemetry/exception governance; broader migration across storage, capture, and other service buffers remains a follow-up battle. `SnapshotClone` remains allowed only for explicitly tagged bounded/debug paths, and the gate reports review sites rather than auto-rewriting them.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi -q`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\allocator\doctrine.rs,src\allocator\mod.rs,src\gui\surface_memory.rs,src\gfx\velvet_glove.rs,.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1,docs\agent\decision-log.md`
- 2026-04-07 | doctrine-migration-large-raster-hotpaths-and-headless-boot-closeout
  - Scope: `src/{allocator/doctrine.rs,gfx/{mod.rs,tile_renderer.rs},gop/framebuffer.rs,gui/{effects.rs,text.rs}}`
  - Change: Migrated the remaining large bare-metal raster hot paths onto Allocation Doctrine v3 without widening the wrapper API. `gfx::Surface`, `SwapChain`, and `OpenGlContext` now store pixels in `SurfacePixelBuffer` and route creation through `alloc_surface_pixels`, keeping near-screen and full-screen surfaces page-backed by policy instead of raw `Vec<u32>` allocation. GOP shadow double-buffering now uses doctrine-backed pixel storage as well, with framebuffer clone explicitly routed through the exception registry as a cold-path snapshot. GUI effects scratch planes (`KawaseBlur`, Gaussian blur temp/horizontal buffers) now use doctrine-backed scratch buffers instead of raw full-region vectors. Text rasterization allocates glyph blobs through doctrine and converts to owned pixel vectors only at the protocol boundary under a bounded `gui.text.blob_pixels` snapshot exception. Tile pixel storage was also rerouted to doctrine so repeated tile surface allocation no longer bypasses policy, while tile metadata masks remain ordinary heap vectors because they are bounded control-plane state rather than large linear raster payloads. The doctrine module gained pointer accessors for surface buffers plus new explicit exceptions for framebuffer clone and glyph blob export. Headless boot validation was re-checked against the appliance serial log and the current success path still reaches `storage-mounted`, `display-ready`, `desktop-ready`, `app-basket-ready`, and `BOOTCTRL success`.
  - Why: The previous doctrine slice secured the central shell surfaces, but large linear pixel ownership was still leaking through legacy gfx, GOP, text, and blur paths. Those were the realistic ways to regress back into the same contiguous heap failures that previously froze QEMU under large windows. This pass closes the remaining high-value raster seams instead of pretending every `Vec` in the repository must disappear; control-plane metadata remains allowed on heap by doctrine and only the large linear pixel hot paths were targeted.
  - Boundary: This closes doctrine migration for the primary bare-metal GUI raster hot paths and confirms headless boot success for the current appliance path. Gate warnings that remain are bounded or cold-path fallbacks (`new_for_test`, doctrine fallback heap construction, tile clone fallback), control-plane metadata vectors, and non-GUI host-side code such as `win32.rs`. Those are review sites, not unresolved full-screen raster heap ownership in the kernel desktop path.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi -q`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\allocator\doctrine.rs,src\gfx\mod.rs,src\gop\framebuffer.rs,src\gui\effects.rs,src\gui\text.rs,src\gfx\tile_renderer.rs,docs\agent\decision-log.md`; serial log markers confirmed in `logs/serial_20260407_185259.log`
- 2026-04-07 | doctrine-page-backed-routing-fix-and-fresh-headless-boot-green
  - Scope: `src/{allocator/doctrine.rs,gui/surface_memory.rs,gfx/tile_renderer.rs}`
  - Change: Corrected the doctrine page-backed primitive so large GUI and shared-surface allocations no longer route through `dma_alloc`. `PageBackedAllocation` now uses zeroed aligned kernel virtual allocation instead of physically contiguous DMA pages, keeping DMA ownership restricted to `PhysContigDma` callers and removing the bootstrap-time pressure that was causing `surface allocation failed` during `shell-windows`. The doctrine exception registry was also fixed to obey its own per-subsystem snapshot quota by splitting `gui.shared_surface.snapshot` and `gui.text.blob_pixels` into separate subsystem namespaces. Added serial instrumentation for shared-surface allocation/resize failures and cleaned the lingering handwave comment in tile hashing.
  - Why: Fresh headless validation after the raster migration regressed at desktop bootstrap. The actual failure path was not a generic out-of-memory collapse but a doctrine policy bug plus an allocator routing mistake: large shell surfaces were being treated as DMA-class contiguous allocations, and the invalid registry state then caused every doctrine-backed surface allocation to fail closed.
  - Boundary: This closes the headless boot regression and restores a fresh green appliance boot with the current doctrine routing. It does not claim that every remaining raw `Vec` in the repository is gone; the residual gate warnings are bounded metadata, cold-path snapshot fallbacks, or host-side code outside the bare-metal GUI hot path.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi -q`; `.\run_qemu.ps1 -Mode uefi -Profile fast -Headless -NoBuild -ForceVarsReset`
- 2026-04-07 | explicit-seed-partition-layout-and-runtime-contract
  - Scope: `scripts/build_vm_appliance.py`, `src/security/seed_store.rs`
  - Change: Promoted the appliance seed source from an implicit mounted-path convention into an explicit layout and discovery contract. The appliance builder now emits a dedicated GPT `seed` partition alongside `esp/system_a/system_b/data/recovery`, sizes it from the curated bundle set, and writes a FAT16 seed volume containing `/APPS/*.BHD`, `APPS.IMG`, and seed metadata. Runtime seed discovery was tightened in parallel: `security::seed_store` no longer treats any mount whose name happens to contain `seed` as authoritative. It now accepts only explicit seed targets (`/seed`, `/system/seed`, and their `/apps` variants) or explicit source identities such as `seed`, `/dev/appliance/seed`, and `/dev/disk/by-label/seed`, with deny-by-default tests locking the contract.
  - Why: The remaining parity nuance was not package semantics anymore; it was ownership. The old model behaved like a seed partition but discovered it heuristically from mounted path names. That was good enough for bootstrap, but not a durable storage contract. Adding a real `seed` partition to the appliance image and narrowing runtime discovery to explicit seed mounts closes that ambiguity.
  - Boundary: This slice defines and produces the explicit seed partition contract, and runtime seed-store discovery now prefers that contract. It does not add a brand-new bespoke block driver; mounted seed partitions still flow through the existing filesystem/mount stack by design.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi -q`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths scripts/build_vm_appliance.py,src/security/seed_store.rs,docs/agent/decision-log.md`
- 2026-04-07 | accessibility-core-shell-event-focus-captions-and-magnifier-lane
  - Scope: `src/{gui/{protocol.rs,client.rs},services/ech_shell.rs,gfx/{shell_invalidation.rs,velvet_glove.rs,velvet_glove/bootstrap.rs}}`
  - Change: Promoted accessibility from profile toggles plus tree publication into a shell-owned runtime lane. `gui::protocol` now exposes explicit `AccessibilityFocusState` plus dedicated shell invalidation targets for captions and magnifier surfaces. `EchShell` gained bounded accessibility event and caption queues, normalized focus-state tracking, queue coalescing, and new shell commands for listing/clearing accessibility events, captions, and current focus. Window state transitions, accessibility tree publications, and notifications now emit normalized `FocusChanged`, `WindowOpened/Closed`, `DialogOpened`, `SelectionChanged`, `ValueChanged`, `NotificationPosted`, and `LiveRegionChanged` events where the existing shell truth allows it. On the GUI side, `DesktopClient` can fetch these lanes, and `velvet_glove` now owns two real accessibility surfaces: a docked magnifier panel that follows shell focus and a live-captions lane that renders recent normalized caption events. Both surfaces participate in bootstrap, invalidation, restore, login visibility discipline, and accessibility tree publication instead of living as ad hoc debug overlays.
  - Why: The GUI parity review showed accessibility was still the clearest structural gap versus the stated Windows-class core target. The project already had `AccessibilityProfile`, AT-SPI publication, and input semantics for sticky/slow keys, but there was no canonical shell focus/event lane and no shell-managed captions or magnifier surfaces. This slice closes that gap at the shell ownership boundary instead of scattering one-off behavior across apps.
  - Boundary: The new magnifier surface is a docked focus magnifier, not a full optical screen-zoom implementation. It tracks focus semantics, role, bounds, and recent accessibility events rather than magnifying framebuffer pixels. Screen reader speech synthesis still lives outside the shell; the shell guarantees normalized event ordering, focus truth, and a visible caption/event lane.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`
- 2026-04-07 | accessibility-framebuffer-magnifier-modes-and-speech-lane-orchestration
  - Scope: `src/{gui/{protocol.rs,client.rs},services/{ech_shell.rs,ech_display.rs},gop/framebuffer.rs,gfx/{velvet_glove.rs,velvet_glove/app_runtime.rs}}`
  - Change: Closed the literal accessibility parity gap by turning the magnifier from a semantic dock into a real compositor-backed capture surface and by upgrading screen-reader delivery from raw events into an explicit speech lane. `AccessibilityProfile` now carries `MagnifierMode::{Docked,Lens,Fullscreen}`. `EchDisplay` gained bounded composed-region capture into an offscreen framebuffer so the shell can sample real framebuffer content without recursively magnifying its own window. `velvet_glove` now computes mode-specific capture and viewport rectangles, scales the sampled pixels into docked/lens/fullscreen magnifier surfaces, and keeps the shell overlay geometry synchronized with the active mode. In parallel, `EchShell` now owns a bounded speech queue with coalescing, drop accounting, timed advancement, and client commands for reading, advancing, and clearing speech state. The live captions surface renders both caption history and the active/pending speech lane state so the shell has one coherent speech-plus-captions accessibility surface instead of two unrelated toggles.
  - Why: The previous accessibility slice closed the control-plane side but still left two literal parity holes: magnifier was not reading real framebuffer pixels, and screen-reader delivery had no shell-owned utterance orchestration. That meant the system behaved like a strong semantic accessibility shell, not like a desktop with true magnifier modes and bounded speech sequencing. This slice moves those last two boundaries into explicit shell/runtime truth.
  - Boundary: The speech lane is an orchestration queue, not a hardware or TTS synthesis engine; it sequences utterances and mirrors them into captions, but it does not claim external audio synthesis where no backend exists. Magnifier is compositor-backed and mode-complete for docked/lens/fullscreen, but any future per-pixel zoom acceleration would still be an optimization pass rather than a missing correctness path.

That is exactly the kind of latent commercialization blocker that only gets discovered late unless the packaging path fails closed.

- 2026-04-08 | phase6-loopback-vfs-inotify-core-truthfulness-refresh
  - Scope: `src/{drivers/loopback.rs,fs/{ext4.rs,ntfs.rs,vfs_unified.rs,inotify.rs}}`, `docs/agent/{decision-log.md,fs-capability-matrix.md}`, `TODO.md`
  - Change: Closed the next honest Phase 6 core seams without over-claiming filesystem exactness. ext4 and ntfs mounts now carry a typed storage backend instead of assuming resident whole-image ownership, and the loopback mount lane now wires ext4/ntfs through block-backed storage rather than cloning the full image at mount time. Unified VFS mount routing now normalizes separators and respects mount boundaries, so `/mnt` no longer steals `/mnt-data` or mixed-slash paths. In parallel, `inotify` watch identity moved from bare inode indexing to a namespace-aware target tuple `(fs_type, mount_point, source, inode)`, with path-based event dispatch and regression tests proving that equal inode numbers in different mounted backends no longer fan out to the wrong watchers.
  - Why: Phase 6 was still carrying two dishonest core edges. First, ext4/ntfs loop mounts looked generic from the shell but still depended on resident image snapshots internally. Second, inotify was indexing watches by inode number alone even though the VFS already exposes multiple backend namespaces where the same inode value can legitimately exist more than once. Those two seams are now explicit, bounded, and mechanically covered.
  - Boundary: This is still not full Phase 6 exactness. `src/fs/btrfs.rs` still mounts only to superblock/subvolume metadata and does not provide real tree/inode/data-path semantics to Unified VFS, so Btrfs remains the primary hard blocker. The new path-aware inotify target contract is real, but broad filesystem-side event call sites still need wider corpus coverage before the whole event surface can be called exact. NTFS root directory listing truth also remains narrower than ext4/FAT because root metadata classification is not yet equally rich.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib fs::vfs_unified::tests::mount_resolution_normalizes_separators_and_respects_boundaries -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib drivers::loopback::tests::loopback_mounts_ntfs_image_into_vfs -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib security::seed_store::tests::explicit_seed_mount_detection_is_deny_by_default -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib fs::inotify::tests::namespace_aware_targets_do_not_collapse_same_inode_across_mounts -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib fs::inotify::tests::dispatch_targets_only_matching_namespace_watchers -- --exact`
- 2026-04-08 | phase6-btrfs-readonly-core-wiring-and-seed-lifecycle-corpus
  - Scope: `src/{fs/{btrfs.rs,vfs_unified.rs},security/seed_store.rs}`, `docs/agent/{decision-log.md,fs-capability-matrix.md}`, `TODO.md`
  - Change: Advanced the remaining honest Phase 6 storage blockers by giving Btrfs a real mounted read path instead of a superblock-only facade. `fs::btrfs` now persists mounted image ownership, boots its logical-to-physical map from the superblock system chunk array plus chunk tree, resolves the default subvolume from the root tree, indexes inode/dir/extents out of the FS tree, and serves read-only file and directory operations through that indexed tree. `vfs_unified` now routes `open/read/list/df` into that Btrfs backend so mounted images no longer fail with an unwired error when the on-disk shape is within the supported boundary. In parallel, the seed-store corpus now pins retryable-versus-quarantined lifecycle transitions so the state machine is not just visible in the shell but mechanically checked.
  - Why: After the loopback/VFS/inotify refresh, the next biggest dishonesty was that Btrfs appeared in the filesystem matrix but could not actually traverse tree/inode/data paths through the unified VFS. That meant the repo still had a major backend enum slot whose user-visible story was “mounted” while the real data path was absent. Closing the read-only core removes that blind spot and leaves the remaining Btrfs limitations explicit instead of implicit.
  - Boundary: This is a bounded read-only Btrfs core, not full Linux-grade parity. The supported shape is single-device resident images with chunk-tree/root-tree/fs-tree metadata and uncompressed extents. Compressed extents, multi-device/RAID layouts, and write-path semantics still fail closed and keep full `F-BTRFS-01` exactness open. Phase 6 also remains open because `F-F2FS-01` still carries simplified/approximate behavior in compression/recovery/allocation paths.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib fs::vfs_unified::tests::btrfs_mounted_backend_supports_open_read_list_and_df -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib fs::vfs_unified::tests::mount_resolution_normalizes_separators_and_respects_boundaries -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib security::seed_store::tests::pending_seed_entry_tracks_retry_and_quarantine_lifecycle -- --exact`
- 2026-04-08 | phase6-f2fs-metadata-checkpoint-sit-exactness-core
  - Scope: `src/fs/f2fs.rs`, `docs/agent/{decision-log.md,fs-capability-matrix.md}`, `TODO.md`
  - Change: Removed the remaining approximate F2FS metadata lanes that were still visible behind a writable-primary-filesystem story. The inode path now uses the real on-disk offsets for `i_size`, `i_nlink`, timestamps, `i_flags`, `i_extra_isize`, dynamic address windows, and inline-data capacity instead of hard-coded legacy assumptions. Compression/encryption flag updates now write through those disk-layout-aware helpers, `ensure_inode_extra_attr()` preserves the address/NID partition while opening extra-attr space for compression metadata, SIT parsing/writes now respect the packed `vblocks` allocation-type bits plus `mtime`, and stale GC/allocation/checkpoint/recovery code paths were rewired onto the existing NAT/SIT/checkpoint-pack machinery rather than bespoke fake copies.
  - Why: Phase 6 still had a dishonest center of gravity in F2FS: the shell and desktop both treat it as the primary writable backend, but several edge paths were still assuming approximate inode offsets, fake checkpoint restoration, or a parallel GC allocator that bypassed the real metadata contract. Closing those seams makes the repo's default writable filesystem truthful at the metadata/control-plane level instead of only on the happy path.
  - Boundary: This advances `F-F2FS-01` to `Verified core`, not full exact parity. The on-disk metadata, checkpoint, recovery, and allocation-control paths are now wired to the real layout, but the actual compression codec implementations remain simplified and wider mounted-field/regression corpus coverage is still missing. Phase 6 therefore remains open together with the bounded Btrfs read-only limits.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib fs::f2fs::tests::inode_extra_attr_shift_preserves_direct_addrs_and_nids -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib fs::f2fs::tests::parse_sit_entry_masks_alloc_bits_and_keeps_mtime -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib fs::f2fs::tests::parse_checkpoint_pack_accepts_crc32_and_split_bitmaps -- --exact`

  - Change: Closed the remaining dishonest parity edges in the Phase 6 storage lane without pretending unsupported shapes had suddenly become writable. `fs::f2fs` now carries a real raw LZ4 block codec and rejects LZO/Zstd paths with explicit `NotSupported` instead of fake success. `fs::btrfs` now rejects multi-device, RAID/multi-stripe, compressed-extent, and snapshot/write shapes fail-closed while preserving the mounted single-device read path already wired through Unified VFS. It lived in the unsupported edges: fake compression codecs, implicit first-stripe assumptions, and storage/package flows that looked generic from the shell but were not yet pinned end-to-end across mounted seed roots and loop images. This slice turns those edges into explicit fail-closed contracts or mechanically checked paths.

The new seed-store VFS overrides are test-only harness hooks used to pin lifecycle semantics; production discovery still goes through the real mount table and Unified VFS.

- 2026-04-09 | phase6-vfs-client-contract-and-inotify-store-sequencing-closeout
  - Scope: `src/{fs/{vfs_unified.rs,inotify.rs},services/ech_store.rs,gui/client.rs}`, `docs/agent/{decision-log.md,fs-capability-matrix.md}`, `TODO.md`

Those two seams are exactly where a repo can look "exact" on the happy path while still lying under error or mutation pressure.
