- 2026-04-09 | phase6-f2fs-audit-corrections-and-truthfulness-refresh
  - Scope: `src/{fs/{f2fs.rs,vfs_unified.rs,inotify.rs},services/ech_store.rs}`, `docs/agent/{decision-log.md,fs-capability-matrix.md}`, `TODO.md`
  - Change: Corrected the hidden F2FS parity regressions that remained after the first Phase 6 closeout claim and rewrote the docs so the repository says only what the code can now prove. `fs::f2fs` now carries real inode ids through `F2fsEntry`, raw-copy fallback chunks are tagged honestly instead of being mislabeled as LZ4, and the public encryption helper lane is explicit `NotSupported` instead of toy crypto pretending to be AES/XTS or GCM. `fs::vfs_unified` now derives F2FS `open` metadata from those real inode values and sizes its read buffers from full file length instead of a silent 4 KiB ceiling. `services::ech_store` now truncates shorter overwrite writes so F2FS updates no longer leave stale tail bytes behind. `fs::inotify` gained a same-namespace/different-inode regression so the namespace-aware target model is pinned not just across mounts but inside one F2FS namespace as well. `TODO.md` and the capability matrix now record those fixes explicitly, including the fail-closed encryption boundary.
  - Why: The audit showed that the previous "Phase 6 exact" wording was too strong. The repo had already eliminated the obvious unsupported-shape dishonesty, but it still hid three F2FS correctness bugs behind a truthful-looking shell story: every F2FS file surfaced as inode `1`, unified reads silently truncated files above 4 KiB, and overwrite-with-shorter-payload preserved stale tails. A separate truthfulness bug also remained in the compression/encryption helper lane, where raw fallback and toy crypto could be mistaken for exact behavior. This slice closes those gaps and aligns the docs with what the tests now mechanically prove.
  - Boundary: Repository-visible exactness remains valid because unsupported feature lanes are now explicit instead of heuristic. F2FS file encryption is not implemented as exact crypto in-repo and therefore stays a declared `NotSupported` boundary until a real backend exists. Broader appliance field smoke also remains a future validation lane rather than a blocker for the current matrix.
  - Validation: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib fs::vfs_unified::tests::f2fs_vfs_info_preserves_real_inode_identity -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib fs::vfs_unified::tests::f2fs_exact_read_len_tracks_full_file_size -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib services::ech_store::tests::truncate_after_overwrite_only_shrinks_when_needed -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib fs::f2fs::tests::incompressible_lz4_payload_falls_back_to_raw_copy_contract -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib fs::f2fs::tests::encryption_helpers_fail_closed_without_exact_backend -- --exact`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib fs::inotify::tests::same_namespace_different_inodes_do_not_collapse -- --exact`
## 2026-04-10 | security-memory | aslr-entropy-floor-and-secret-telemetry-fail-closed
- Decision: Raised user-space ASLR search ranges to `1 TiB` for mmap, `256 GiB` for stack, and `1 GiB` for heap, and made heap placement consume a per-address-space random offset instead of always starting at the aligned floor. Boot telemetry no longer prints stack canary values or ASLR offsets to serial/debug sinks; it emits only state transitions.
- Rationale: The previous contract combined low entropy windows with direct serial disclosure of the remaining secrets, which undermined exploit resistance before deeper speculative-execution mitigations are even considered.
- Tradeoffs: Wider randomization increases layout sparsity and makes repro traces less deterministic. That cost is acceptable inside the existing canonical user-space window; secret-bearing serial output is not.
- Evidence: Code review of `src/security/mod.rs` and `src/memory/mod.rs`; targeted `echos_gate` run on the touched paths.
- Supersedes: none
- Next validation: Run host-side layout tests plus QEMU boot smoke to confirm widened ranges and muted security telemetry do not regress process bring-up.
## 2026-04-10 | security-runtime | spectre-iommu-tls-x509-fail-closed-runtime-lane
- Decision: Added a dedicated `security::spectre` runtime lane that programs `IA32_SPEC_CTRL` for `IBRS`/`STIBP`, issues `IBPB` on context switch, and fences syscall entry/context-switch return paths for `BHB` cleanup. Wired IOMMU manager discovery/enable/self-test into early boot before DMA-capable device init. Hardened the live TLS 1.3 client state machine so `ServerHello` enforces TLS 1.3 selected-version and downgrade-sentinel checks, derives X25519 handshake secrets from a real ephemeral key pair, parses the leaf certificate for `CertificateVerify`, verifies `CertificateVerify` and `Finished`, and fails closed on malformed handshake structure. Tightened X.509 parsing to reject oversized/non-minimal DER lengths, empty issuer/subject-without-SAN inputs, malformed SANs, oversized chain lengths, and CRLs without `nextUpdate`.
- Rationale: These four lanes were the highest-value remotely reachable or boot-time privileged trust boundaries left open in the repo: speculative execution at the kernel edge, DMA isolation timing, handshake authenticity after certificate-chain acceptance, and untrusted DER parsing in the network ingress path.
- Tradeoffs: Spectre coverage is now strong on the runtime path but compiler-wide retpoline remains a follow-up item. TLS resumption/0-RTT still needs a narrower PSK binder/selected-identity follow-up; the hardened live handshake path intentionally rejects unsupported or malformed inputs rather than trying to be permissive.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -q`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/security/spectre.rs,src/security/mod.rs,src/task/scheduler.rs,src/syscall.rs,src/drivers/iommu.rs,src/main.rs,src/net/tls.rs,src/net/x509.rs`
- Supersedes: none
- Next validation: Headless QEMU smoke with boot markers plus targeted TLS/X.509 malformed corpus tests for the new fail-closed edges.
## 2026-04-10 | security-followup | slh-call-ret-hardening-plus-failclosed-psk-net-virtio
- Decision: Closed the remaining repo-visible Spectre call surface by enabling LLVM's supported x86 Speculative Load Hardening flags (`--x86-slh-indirect`, `--x86-slh-lfence`, `--x86-slh-fence-call-and-ret`) for both `x86_64-unknown-none` and `x86_64-unknown-uefi` targets in `.cargo/config.toml`. Chose this route because the current nightly LLVM backend in the toolchain does not expose the retpoline thunk flags that had been planned. In parallel, tightened the TLS 0-RTT / PSK lane so `TlsHandshakeExt` only offers early data when a cached ticket is structurally valid, verifies `selected_identity == 0`, rejects early data unless `EncryptedExtensions` explicitly echoes `early_data`, and refuses to cache unverifiable `NewSessionTicket` entries. Hardened `src/net/{ip,tcp,udp,dns,dhcp}.rs` against malformed length, compression-loop, and option-overflow inputs, and tightened VirtIO packet / descriptor bounds in `src/drivers/virtio_net.rs` plus the C virtio-blk backend in `src/c_drivers/virtio.c`.
- Rationale: The user asked to close the remaining high-value network and speculative-execution edges in one pass. The truthful way to do that on this toolchain was to use the compiler hardening knobs LLVM actually ships, and to keep TLS resumption fail-closed wherever the repository does not yet hold enough authenticated state to derive a trustworthy PSK cache entry.
- Tradeoffs: Literal retpoline thunks are not emitted by this compiler configuration; the chosen mitigation is compiler-supported SLH hardening, which is materially better than leaving indirect call/return surfaces soft but is still a different mechanism than classic retpoline. `NewSessionTicket` compatibility is intentionally narrower because unverifiable tickets are dropped instead of cached. VirtIO-blk descriptor hardening now exists in the C backend path, but the active Rust `virtio_ffi` transport still reports `BackendUnavailable` until a real queue/DMA backend is wired.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -q`; `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --lib -q`
- Supersedes: The open "compiler-geneli retpoline" follow-up from the previous 2026-04-10 security runtime pass, replacing it with the supported SLH configuration on the current toolchain.
- Next validation: Run targeted host tests or corpus cases for malformed DNS/DHCP packets, and run headless QEMU smoke after the next release-blocker slice so the new SLH flags are exercised in a boot path as well as compile time.
## 2026-04-10 | security-followup | live-resumption-cache-and-virtioffi-backend-closure
- Decision: Closed the two remaining repo-visible follow-up boundaries from the earlier security pass. `src/drivers/virtio_ffi.rs` now resolves the discovered VirtIO PCI BDF into a `virtio_drivers::transport::pci::PciTransport`, initializes the repository-local `virtio_blk` backend, and routes sector reads and writes through `virtio_blk::{read_sector,write_sector}` instead of stopping at the legacy probe-only transport surface. In parallel, `src/net/tls.rs` now derives a resumption PSK from the established TLS 1.3 master secret plus `NewSessionTicket` nonce, caches tickets through the live `TlsClient` path, and reuses those tickets in `build_client_hello()`. `src/net/http.rs`, `src/net/dot.rs`, and `src/net/doh.rs` were tightened so their live client handshake flights walk `ServerHello` through `Finished` plus post-finished `NewSessionTicket` messages instead of assuming a truncated two-step server response.
- Rationale: The previous pass intentionally left two honest boundaries open rather than claiming parity too early: live block I/O still stopped at `BackendUnavailable` in the Rust FFI path, and the repository only had a fail-closed ticket story because live clients did not yet own a trustworthy resumption-secret derivation plus handshake-flight consumption path. The user then asked to close those remaining boundaries in one pass.
- Tradeoffs: The TLS cache path is now real for the repository's active HTTP/DoT/DoH clients, but the broader TLS stack remains intentionally strict and may reject peers that rely on repository-unimplemented compatibility lanes. The DoT/DoH handshake walkers were closed by reusing the same fail-closed message sequencing contract rather than adding permissive partial-record recovery logic.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -q`; `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --lib -q`
- Supersedes: The open "ticket cache intentionally closed" and "active Rust virtio_ffi still BackendUnavailable" boundaries from `2026-04-10 | security-followup | slh-call-ret-hardening-plus-failclosed-psk-net-virtio`.
- Next validation: Run headless QEMU smoke plus storage/network smokes that actually exercise VirtIO block I/O and a resumed TLS handshake, because this turn proves compile-time and wiring truth but not full runtime device/server interoperability.
## 2026-04-10 | security-followup | spectre-secureboot-x509-iommu-parity-tightening
- Decision: Tightened the remaining repo-visible parity edges in the four fatal lanes the user singled out. `src/security/spectre.rs` now folds `SSBD` into `IA32_SPEC_CTRL`, reads `IA32_ARCH_CAPABILITIES` to detect `eIBRS`, `BHI_NO`, `MDS_NO`, `TAA_NO`, and `FB_CLEAR`, and issues a `VERW`-based buffer clear on entry/context-switch when the CPU advertises the architectural clear contract. `src/posix/windows_image.rs` now rejects PK/KEK/db/dbx reads whose UEFI attributes do not include the authenticated-variable contract and ships a repo-local `scripts/sign_uefi_secure_boot.ps1` wrapper for `sbsign`/`sbverify`. `src/net/x509.rs` now fails closed on oversized TLS certificate chains, oversized DER payloads, and extension-count blowups. `src/drivers/iommu.rs` plus `src/drivers/pci_hotplug.rs` now synchronize primary-segment PCI hotplug insert/remove/surprise-removal with IOMMU domain attachment state instead of leaving late DMA-capable devices outside the remapping contract.
- Rationale: After the previous security passes, the plan file still mixed real open runtime gaps with stale findings. The remaining honest code gaps were narrower: speculative-buffer cleanup on CPUs that advertise architectural clear support, authenticated-variable enforcement in the Secure Boot DB read path, bounded X.509/TLS chain ingestion, and hotplug-time DMA-domain drift.
- Tradeoffs: This closes the repository-visible runtime lane, not the full appliance lifecycle. Secure Boot still needs real enrolled 2023 CA or OEM db material in deployment, and GPU-specific DMA queue/BAR metadata audit remains separate from the generic GOP/framebuffer path. The new hotplug-IOMMU sync is primary-segment scoped because the current PCI hotplug model does not yet carry ACPI/PCI segment identity.
- Evidence: `rustfmt src/security/spectre.rs src/posix/windows_image.rs src/net/x509.rs src/drivers/iommu.rs src/drivers/pci_hotplug.rs`; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -q`; `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --lib -q`
- Supersedes: The stale fatal rows in `RELEASE_PLAN.md` that still described X.509 and early-boot IOMMU as open after the earlier 2026-04-10 runtime/security follow-up passes.
- Next validation: Run headless QEMU boot smoke, then targeted Secure Boot artifact signing on a host with `sbsign`/`sbverify`, and finally exercise a primary-segment PCI hotplug path under IOMMU-enabled emulation or appliance hardware.
## 2026-04-10 | security-followup | secure-boot-bundle-and-enroll-flow
- Decision: Added a repository-local Secure Boot artifact flow that covers the missing operational half of the earlier runtime hardening. `scripts/generate_secure_boot_bundle.ps1` now generates a self-consistent PK/KEK/db/dbx test bundle using `openssl`, `cert-to-efi-sig-list`, and `sign-efi-sig-list`, emits a manifest plus README, and can optionally sign an EFI payload immediately. `scripts/build_signed_uefi.ps1` wraps `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --bin ech_os` and signs the resulting EFI artifact with the generated `db` key. `docs/architecture/secure_boot_local_flow.md` records the exact local flow for bundle generation, signing, OVMF/firmware enrollment order, and runtime validation expectations.
- Rationale: The codebase already had Secure Boot runtime verification and a low-level signing wrapper, but the user specifically asked whether the certificate/enroll side could also be handled. The honest repository answer is yes for artifact preparation and repeatable local enrollment flow, but not for physically pushing keys into arbitrary firmware without platform access.
- Tradeoffs: The generated `dbx` is intentionally a bootstrap revoked certificate so the variable exists and the runtime parser remains fail-closed; production deployments must replace it with real revocation material. The scripts automate the repository half of the chain but still leave physical firmware enrollment and trust-policy choice (OEM/self-signed vs 2023 CA) as operational decisions.
- Evidence: PowerShell parser validation for the new scripts plus targeted repository review; no Rust compile surface changed beyond docs/scripts in this pass.
- Supersedes: The narrower "repo-local EFI signing pipeline only" boundary from the earlier 2026-04-10 parity-tightening pass.
- Next validation: Run the bundle generator on a host with efitools present, sign a release EFI, enroll the resulting AUTH files into an OVMF vars image or firmware UI, and confirm signed boot with Secure Boot enabled.
## 2026-04-10 | security-followup | secure-boot-qemu-prepare-verify-harness
- Decision: Promoted the Secure Boot local flow from "artifact prep" to a repeatable QEMU harness. `run_qemu.ps1` now accepts signed-EFI overrides, secure/custom OVMF code+vars paths, and extra ESP payload files. `scripts/build_vm_appliance.py` now supports `--esp-extra-file` so the appliance ESP can carry one-shot enrollment payloads alongside `BOOTX64.EFI`. `scripts/run_secure_boot_qemu_smoke.ps1` orchestrates a two-phase flow: `prepare` launches secure OVMF with the signed echOS EFI plus FAT 8.3 aliases for the AUTH/cert payloads on the same ESP, and `verify` reuses the same vars-store headlessly and asserts Secure Boot serial markers (`Runtime services verified`, `Secure Boot databases available`, `Loaded image signature OK`).
- Rationale: The previous bundle/sign scripts still left operators stitching together secure OVMF, signed EFI, vars persistence, and enrollment media by hand. The user explicitly wanted this reduced to concrete OVMF/QEMU smoke commands. Reusing the existing appliance disk builder keeps the boot path truthful and avoids a second ad hoc removable-image contract.
- Tradeoffs: The first custom-key enrollment still needs firmware UI or an external vars-store editor because this host does not ship `virt-fw-vars`/equivalent tooling. To keep the path deterministic without VFAT long-name support, the ESP payload uses explicit FAT 8.3 aliases (`PK.AUT`, `KEK.AUT`, `DB.AUT`, `DBX.AUT`) instead of pretending `.auth` filenames survive on the minimal builder.
- Evidence: PowerShell parser validation and targeted review of the new host-side harness plus touched-path gate. Runtime verification remains bounded by whether an enrolled vars-store already exists.
- Supersedes: The earlier "local flow doc only" boundary by giving the repository a concrete prepare/verify QEMU entrypoint.
- Next validation: Run `scripts/run_secure_boot_qemu_smoke.ps1 -Phase prepare -ForceVarsReset`, enroll the aliases through OVMF/KeyTool once, then run `scripts/run_secure_boot_qemu_smoke.ps1 -Phase verify` and confirm the serial markers trip on the persisted vars-store.
## 2026-04-10 | security-followup | secure-boot-autoenroll-and-gpu-bar-dma-audit
- Decision: Closed the remaining repo-visible Secure Boot parity gap by moving first-enroll into the guest boot path itself. `src/main.rs` now detects an ESP-side `SBENROLL.ON` trigger during the UEFI boot-services phase, loads `PK/KEK/db/dbx` authenticated payloads from `PK.AUT/KEK.AUT/DB.AUT/DBX.AUT`, applies them through authenticated `SetVariable`, records a non-volatile enroll state marker, and forces a warm reset so the next boot proves the same vars-store under Secure Boot instead of pretending the current unsecured boot was sufficient. In parallel, `src/drivers/gpu_native.rs` now separates MMIO BAR ownership from VRAM BAR ownership, rejects undersized or overlapping apertures, registers an IOMMU DMA domain per GPU BDF, and wraps blit/page-flip submission in that domain while rejecting MMIO-overlapping DMA ranges. `src/drivers/virtio_gpu.rs` now validates the PCI capability chain length/cycles, checks each capability window against its BAR bounds, rejects zero notify multipliers or out-of-window doorbell offsets, and fixes the control-queue descriptor flag contract to `NEXT` then `WRITE` instead of the previous inverted flag pattern.
- Rationale: The honest boundary after the earlier QEMU harness work was no longer signing or vars persistence; it was that first enroll still depended on an out-of-band firmware UI step. Likewise, the remaining GPUBreach-class repository gap was no longer generic IOMMU timing but the driver-local assumption that BAR0 could double as both MMIO and VRAM plus a VirtIO-GPU capability/doorbell path that trusted device metadata too much.
- Tradeoffs: The new Secure Boot path intentionally fails closed when `SBENROLL.ON` is armed but the platform does not leave setup mode after the reset. That is stricter than a permissive boot but prevents silent downgrade. On the GPU side, native scanout now refuses devices without a validated VRAM aperture instead of guessing. Vendor-specific proprietary queue formats remain outside this generic audit because the repository does not carry those datasheets or drivers.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -q`; `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --lib -q`
- Supersedes: The "first custom-key enrollment still needs firmware UI" boundary from `2026-04-10 | security-followup | secure-boot-qemu-prepare-verify-harness`, and the open generic GPU BAR/DMA metadata audit note in `RELEASE_PLAN.md`.
- Next validation: Run `scripts/run_secure_boot_qemu_smoke.ps1 -Phase prepare -ForceVarsReset` and confirm the serial markers show auto-enroll plus warm reset on the first boot, then run `-Phase verify`. After that, exercise native scanout and VirtIO-GPU under QEMU or appliance hardware so the new BAR/domain gates are observed on live MMIO/DMA traffic rather than compile time alone.
## 2026-04-10 | qemu-lane | whpx-host-cpu-nvme-e1000e-hardwareish-smoke
- Decision: Tightened `run_qemu.ps1` toward a more hardware-faithful appliance lane. The script now detects `HypervisorPlatform` through `Win32_OptionalFeature`, promotes debug-profile runs to `-accel whpx` plus `-cpu host` only when WHPX is actually enabled, keeps `tcg` as the honest fallback, and logs the selected acceleration/CPU/storage/NIC contract up front. The UEFI appliance path now presents the system disk through QEMU's NVMe controller instead of `virtio-blk`, and the user-network device is now `e1000e` instead of `virtio-net-pci`. The ISO path also now receives the same CPU/vCPU model selection so debug-vs-fast semantics stay aligned across both boot lanes.
- Rationale: The user asked for a donanima daha yakin smoke path. Leaving `Haswell` plus virtio block/net in place would under-test exactly the host-instruction, NVMe controller, and Intel NIC surfaces they wanted to exercise.
- Tradeoffs: `host` CPU is only selected when WHPX is enabled because `tcg` cannot truthfully promise the host instruction model. Moving the appliance disk and NIC away from virtio may surface driver gaps that were previously hidden by paravirtual devices; that is the desired pressure, not a regression in the harness.
- Evidence: Targeted review and script-level patching of `run_qemu.ps1`; follow-up validation is the debug-profile QEMU run plus touched-path gate.
- Supersedes: The earlier appliance harness assumption that debug mode always meant WHPX-plus-Haswell and that virtio block/net remained the default "trust-building" device contract.
- Next validation: Enable `HypervisorPlatform`, reboot the host, run `.\run_qemu.ps1 -Profile debug -Mode uefi -Headless`, and confirm the log prints `Acceleration: whpx`, `CPU model: host`, NVMe boot progression, and the same boot-control markers already seen on the previous generic UEFI smoke.
## 2026-04-10 | security-followup | dns-entropy-lane-and-kernel-stack-guard-containment
- Decision: Closed the remaining repo-visible P0 DNS poisoning lane by removing the sequential ephemeral-port allocator from `src/net/udp.rs` and replacing it with entropy-backed full-range selection across 49152-65535, with a one-time serial warning plus full-range collision scan when RDSEED/RDRAND are unavailable. `src/net/dns.rs` now sources query IDs from the same secure/fallback lane and rejects replies whose ID does not match the live query. In parallel, `src/allocator/stack.rs`, `src/memory/mod.rs`, `src/task/task.rs`, `src/task/scheduler.rs`, and `src/interrupts/mod.rs` now turn the kernel stack guard page from a debug-fill fiction into a real unmapped page-table boundary, classify guard-page faults in the page-fault handler, exit the offending task instead of continuing into adjacent memory, and keep a per-task low-watermark so the deepest observed kernel stack usage can be reported at the fault site.
- Rationale: The plan still had two open P0 rows whose worst-case outcome was disproportionate to their implementation size: predictable DNS source ports let a remote attacker collapse resolver entropy back toward Kaminsky-class poisoning, and a missing kernel-stack guard page converts a single overflow into silent adjacent-memory corruption at ring 0. Both were worth closing before lower-priority hardening passes because they directly affect remote trust and kernel containment.
- Tradeoffs: The DNS lane is now materially harder to predict, but DNSSEC validation corpus work is still separate from transport/query entropy and remains an explicit follow-up. The stack lane now contains overflow into a defined guard-page fault path, but the kernel stack capacity itself is still `16 KiB`; deep recursion or unusually tall call chains can still exhaust that budget and will now fail closed rather than scribbling past the stack bottom.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -q`; `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --lib -q`
- Supersedes: The open `RELEASE_PLAN.md` P0 findings that still described DNS source-port allocation as sequential and kernel stacks as lacking any real guard-page or stack-usage telemetry.
- Next validation: Run `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/random.rs,src/net/udp.rs,src/net/dns.rs,src/memory/mod.rs,src/allocator/stack.rs,src/task/task.rs,src/task/scheduler.rs,src/interrupts/mod.rs,RELEASE_PLAN.md,docs/agent/decision-log.md`, then boot a headless QEMU smoke so the new stack-fault containment and randomized networking lane are exercised in a live kernel path.
## 2026-04-11 | boot-memory | dedicated-kernel-stack-va-and-login-smoke
- Decision: Replaced the kernel-stack guard activation path that attempted to unmap HHDM-backed huge pages with a dedicated 4 KiB-mapped kernel-stack virtual window. `KernelStack::new()` still owns contiguous PMM frames, but maps them through `map_kernel_stack_pages()` under `KERNEL_STACK_VIRT_BASE..KERNEL_STACK_VIRT_LIMIT`; guard-page unmap now targets ordinary PTEs instead of forcing a live HHDM huge-page split. Task context RSP, BSP/AP startup stack tops, and SYSCALL `GS:8` stack tops are now sourced from that dedicated VA alias, and `set_kernel_stack_for_current_cpu()` refuses non-dedicated stack tops so a future HHDM caller cannot silently bypass the guard page. `run_qemu.ps1` also retains the WHPX pre-UEFI detector and TCG fallback so this host can still validate the guest when WHPX MMIO/MSI emulation fails before echOS entry.
- Rationale: The live QEMU fallback exposed a deterministic pre-login page fault after `kernel-core-ready`: `unmap_page failed ... ParentEntryHugePage`, then a write fault in the same HHDM 2 MiB region. Guard containment must not transiently tear down direct-map coverage used by the boot CPU, allocator, or interrupt path.
- Tradeoffs: The stack VA allocator is a monotonic atomic bump allocator, so dropped stacks return physical frames but do not recycle virtual gaps yet. That is acceptable for boot/login containment because the reserved window is large, but long-running task churn should eventually gain a free-list or epoch-delayed VA reuse lane.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --lib -q`; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -q`; `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --bin ech_os -q`; `.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\memory\mod.rs,src\allocator\stack.rs,src\cpu\smp.rs,src\task\task.rs,src\syscall.rs,RELEASE_PLAN.md,docs\agent\decision-log.md`; direct TCG QEMU smoke on `logs\serial_direct_20260411_124041.log` showed BSP/AP `Stack allocated at dedicated_va=0xfffffe...`, AP startup `stack_top = 0xfffffe...`, `[DESKTOP] session bootstrap step=login-visible`, `[BOOTCTRL] stage=desktop-ready`, `[BOOTCTRL] stage=app-basket-ready`, and `[BOOTCTRL] success` without `PAGE FAULT`, `PANIC`, `ParentEntryHugePage`, `outside dedicated VA`, or `refusing kernel_stack_top`.
- Supersedes: The previous stack-guard implementation detail from `2026-04-10 | security-followup | dns-entropy-lane-and-kernel-stack-guard-containment` that still depended on runtime unmap over the HHDM address.
- Next validation: Run the full scripted smoke matrix after appliance packaging time is reduced or allowed to complete: `run_qemu.ps1 -Profile fast -Headless`, `-PackagedPeSmoke`, `-SuspendResumeSmoke`, and `-MixedUpdateSmoke`. WHPX remains a host/QEMU accelerator fault on this machine and should be treated separately from echOS guest correctness.
## 2026-04-11 | scheduler | sparse-load-balancer-and-spawn-placement
- Decision: Fixed the SMP sparse-load lane by making `balance_load()` use a ceiling average across CPUs with published load, release the SMP state lock before active migration, and republish post-migration load from the actual worker queue lengths. New `spawn()` and `spawn_boxed()` placements select the lightest online affinity-compatible worker instead of always pushing boot-created tasks to the current CPU.
- Rationale: `logs\serial_20260411_124803.log` showed `20/20 CPUs online` while CPU0 carried all `15` runnable tasks. The root cause was `15 / 20 == 0`, so the old `min_load < avg_load` predicate became `0 < 0` and active balancing never fired under sparse runnable counts. Boot-time spawning also biased all initial work toward CPU0.
- Tradeoffs: The scheduler now distributes runnable queues up to the number of runnable tasks; it does not synthesize work for CPUs beyond that count. With 15 runnable tasks and 20 online CPUs, the correct steady state leaves 5 CPUs idle. Spawn placement scans online workers, so task creation pays `O(N_cpu)` in this boot path; the bounded cost is acceptable here because it removes the CPU0 pile-up before later work-stealing pressure exists.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --lib -q`; `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -q`; `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --bin ech_os -q`; `.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\cpu\smp.rs,src\task\scheduler.rs`; direct TCG QEMU smoke on `logs\serial_balance_20260411_130553.log` showed active migration from CPU0 to CPU1, later migration from CPU0/CPU1 into CPU12-14, and steady load of one task each on CPU0-14 with CPU15-19 idle because only 15 runnable tasks existed.
- Supersedes: The observed CPU0-only load skew in `logs\serial_20260411_124803.log`.
- Next validation: Add a deterministic host-side scheduler test once the worker deque API has a safe test harness, covering sparse cases where `runnable_tasks < online_cpus` and affinity masks exclude the current CPU.
## 2026-04-11 | qemu-lane | whpx-auto-probe-and-fast-fallback
- Decision: `run_qemu.ps1` now has an explicit `-Accel auto|whpx|tcg` selector. Auto mode enables WHPX when the Windows Hypervisor Platform feature is present, launches WHPX as `whpx,kernel-irqchip=off`, uses explicit `sockets=1,cores=N,threads=1` SMP topology, and falls back to TCG if the early UEFI marker is not reached. Interactive GUI runs now return control to the shell after the boot-success marker unless `-WaitForExit` is requested. Repeated normal runs reuse a fresh appliance image instead of rewriting the 1 GiB raw disk when EFI, builder scripts, bundles, and boot-control settings are unchanged.
- Rationale: This host has `HypervisorPlatform`, `VirtualMachinePlatform`, and `Microsoft-Hyper-V-Hypervisor` enabled, and QEMU 10.2 reports `whpx` support, but the echOS OVMF pflash lane fails before guest entry with `WHPX: Failed to emulate MMIO access with EmulatorReturnStatus: 2`. QEMU's WHPX documentation confirms WHPX is the hardware-acceleration backend, recommends `-accel whpx`, and documents x86_64 interrupt/MMIO limitations including `kernel-irqchip=off` as a workaround for interrupt-controller issues. Microsoft documents the host requirements: SLAT, VM Monitor Mode extensions, firmware virtualization support, and DEP/NX.
- Tradeoffs: Secure Boot and persistent OVMF-vars validation still require pflash and therefore remain TCG on this QEMU build when WHPX cannot execute pflash MMIO. Auto mode favors developer velocity by avoiding a long broken-WHPX wait; `-Accel whpx` remains available when deliberately testing a newer QEMU or firmware build.
- Evidence: Local host checks showed all relevant Windows virtualization features enabled, `systeminfo` reported an active hypervisor, and QEMU 10.2 listed `tcg` plus `whpx`. Minimal WHPX probe with default irqchip produced MSI injection failure; `whpx,kernel-irqchip=off` removed that probe warning. Full echOS WHPX pflash smoke still failed with `Failed to emulate MMIO access`, then auto-fallback booted through `[BOOTCTRL] success` on `logs\serial_20260411_210651_tcg_fallback.log` with 4/4 CPUs online. `run_qemu.ps1 -NoBuild -CreateOnly` reused the existing appliance image instead of rebuilding it.
- Supersedes: The previous fast profile behavior that disabled WHPX entirely and rebuilt the appliance image on every run.
- Next validation: Re-test `-Accel whpx` after upgrading to a QEMU build where WHPX can execute the OVMF pflash path; if that works, promote Secure Boot pflash smokes back into the accelerated lane.
## 2026-04-11 | simics-lane | vmp-aware-launcher-and-batch-timeout
- Decision: `run_simics.ps1` now reports the host VMP driver state up front, supports `-CheckOnly` and `-RequireVmp`, generates per-run Simics scripts with selectable core count, captures serial/stdout/stderr into timestamped log files, and enforces a real batch-mode host timeout by killing the whole Simics process tree when the timeout expires. `-SkipDiskUpdate` keeps the VHDX mount/UAC path out of repeat launches when the caller intentionally wants to reuse the existing disk image.
- Rationale: Simics VMP was installed successfully on this host (`vmxmon` running and Simics reporting `VMP kernel module: 1.16.11`), but the old launcher did not surface that fact, did not fail closed when VMP was required, and printed a timeout value that was not enforced by the host process. Batch smoke needed deterministic evidence files rather than one shared serial capture.
- Tradeoffs: Runtime generated `.simics` files keep core count and serial path flexible without mutating the canonical target scripts, at the cost of log-directory churn. `-SkipDiskUpdate` can intentionally run stale EFI content, so the normal path still updates the VHDX unless the operator opts out.
- Evidence: PowerShell parser validation passed; `.\run_simics.ps1 -CheckOnly -RequireVmp` reported `VMP installed=OK running=OK` and Simics `VMP kernel module: 1.16.11`; `.\run_simics.ps1 -NoBuild -SkipDiskUpdate -BatchMode -RequireVmp -TimeoutSec 5 -SerialTailLines 20` terminated in about 6 seconds, wrote timestamped stdout/stderr/serial files, and left no Simics/mini-python process behind; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths run_simics.ps1` reported clean.
- Supersedes: The previous Simics launcher behavior where VMP state was implicit, batch timeout was only informational, and serial capture reused a single fixed filename.
- Next validation: Run a longer `-BatchMode -RequireVmp -TimeoutSec 120` boot after the Simics project warning (`Project ... is not up-to-date`) is addressed, then promote the marker verdict to require the same boot-control success markers as QEMU once the Simics target reaches guest serial consistently.
## 2026-04-11 | simics-display | restore-true-1080p-gop-target
- Decision: Restored the Simics launcher and UEFI GOP preferred mode contract to true `1920x1080`. `run_simics.ps1` now reports `Ekran: 1920x1080`, and `src/main.rs` now ranks GOP modes against `PREFERRED_GOP_WIDTH=1920` plus `PREFERRED_GOP_HEIGHT=1080` instead of the accidental 1020-pixel height.
- Rationale: The user explicitly required 1920x1080, and the previous Simics lane had regressed to a 1920x1020 target. A launcher banner alone is insufficient; the kernel must also request the 1080p GOP mode before constructing the framebuffer.
- Tradeoffs: If a particular Simics firmware image does not expose exact 1920x1080, the existing ranking logic still falls back to the smallest mode that covers the target, then to the largest available lower mode. That preserves bootability while preferring the requested full-HD contract whenever firmware offers it.
- Evidence: PowerShell parser validation passed; `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q` passed with existing repository warnings; `rg` confirms no remaining `1020` in `run_simics.ps1` or `src/main.rs`.
- Supersedes: The accidental 1920x1020 Simics/GOP target introduced in the launcher/display tuning lane.
- Next validation: Run `.\run_simics.ps1 -Release -RequireVmp`, then confirm serial prints `[UEFI] GOP mode selected: 1920x1080` or framebuffer metadata `1920x1080`.
## 2026-04-11 | gui-compositor | translated-raster-and-1080p-login-legibility
- Decision: Fixed the software-GPU raster blit path so translated raster objects are clipped against source coordinates instead of treating destination Y as a source-height bound. `src/gui/renderer.rs` now routes GPU raster draws through the same coordinate-safe helper used by the remaining raster lanes, and the regression test covers a small raster drawn below origin. The Simics/display contract now also keeps `EchDisplay`'s default desktop height at `1080`, and the lock screen/desktop shortcut scene uses larger, higher-contrast cards sized for full-HD output.
- Rationale: The screenshot failure shape matched a compositor bug, not merely a theme issue: login panel geometry rendered while raster text disappeared. A small raster at `y > raster_height` would hit the old `dst_y >= height` break before copying any pixels. Separately, a 1920x1080 firmware mode still looked cropped if the display service initialized its desktop policy to 1920x1020, so the full-HD contract had to be coherent across launcher, GOP preference, display service, and shell scene layout.
- Tradeoffs: The shortcut cards consume more desktop space at 1080p to gain legibility and hit-target quality. The lock panel is now computed from screen bounds instead of a fixed 480x320 frame, which improves full-HD readability while preserving a bounded modal footprint on smaller modes.
- Evidence: `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib gpu_raster_draws_translated_small_blobs_below_origin`; `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; rustfmt over touched GUI/display/boot files.
- Supersedes: The screenshot-observed state where login cards could render without their text and the 1080p Simics lane could still inherit a 1020-pixel internal display height.
- Next validation: Run `.\run_simics.ps1 -Release -RequireVmp`, capture the 1920x1080 lock screen, and confirm the login username/password labels, masked input text, hint lines, and desktop shortcut labels remain visible without scaling down the requested resolution.
## 2026-04-17 | pe-compatibility | analyzer-plus-execution-nucleus-v1-roadmap
- Decision: Added a bounded PE V1 program to `TODO.md` under Faz 4 as `PE Compatibility Analyzer + Execution Nucleus`. V1 is explicitly scoped to offline PE analysis plus a narrow execution nucleus for selected x86_64 PE fixtures: loader/VM/TLS/thread/env/basic-window-message-loop semantics with fail-closed reporting. V1 explicitly excludes `.text` rewrite, call-site patching, D3D translation, graphics bridge, COM/registry deep clone, anti-cheat/DRM, and EGO optimization/cache/policy work.
- Rationale: The useful next PE milestone is not "normal Windows gibi her exe" and not optimizer work. The correct single-developer substrate is a small analyzer that tells the loader what is safe/risky/rejected, plus a runtime nucleus that proves selected binaries with explicit `HOST_FIXTURE`, `ECHOS_RUNTIME`, and `QEMU_BOOT` evidence.
- Tradeoffs: This plan deliberately kills romantic broad compatibility claims in V1. Browser-class, DXGI/D3D, WoW64, COM-lite, registry-lite, Raw Input, installer/update flows, and EGO fast paths are moved to named post-V1 items. The cost is narrower app coverage; the gain is truthful debugability and mechanically checkable closure.
- Evidence: Documentation-only TODO update; no runtime code changed in this decision.
- Supersedes: Any interpretation of Faz 4 Win32 exactness as "arbitrary downloaded `.exe` double-click parity" for V1.
- Next validation: Implement `tools/pe_analyzer/`, PE fixture corpus `PE_FIXTURE_001..008`, manifest schema, reject reason enum, then wire manifest consumption into `src/pe_loader.rs` before adding new Win32 API breadth.
## 2026-04-17 | repo-hygiene | preserve-first-worktree-triage
- Decision: Created a preserve-first worktree triage lane on `codex/worktree-triage-20260417`. The triage inventory is recorded in `docs/agent/worktree-triage-2026-04-17.md`; generated curated-app downloads, extracted release trees, built `.bhd` bundles, copied `.exe` payloads, generated book outputs, generated figure outputs, and `tmp_espeak_voices.json` are hidden from normal status through narrow ignore rules. `.gitattributes` now pins text/binary handling without running repository renormalization.
- Rationale: The working tree contained broad source work plus 1417 untracked files, mostly downloaded/extracted/built artifacts. Hiding only confirmed generated outputs keeps review candidates visible while preventing artifact volume from burying real code, docs, scripts, and package metadata.
- Tradeoffs: `.gitattributes` is intentionally isolated because line-ending policy can create large apparent diffs on Windows. This pass does not stage or commit feature groups, does not delete artifacts, and does not collapse submodule changes into the parent repo.
- Evidence: Inventory commands captured status, diffstat, untracked bucket counts, and submodule-local status before hygiene edits. Follow-up validation must run `git diff --check`, ignored-status inspection, host/UEFI cargo checks, and the echOS touched-path gate.
- Supersedes: Ad hoc artifact visibility in normal `git status` during curated-app packaging and documentation generation.
- Next validation: Confirm generated artifact classes disappear from normal status while `artifacts/curated-sources/*/echos-app.toml`, docs source, scripts, Rust source, release plans, and TTS provenance candidates remain visible.
## 2026-04-17 | security-hardening | tls-net-virtio-proof-pack
- Decision: Closed the next P0/P1 proof pack by adding direct TLS 1.3 downgrade-sentinel, KEX-group, and state-order regressions; replacing TLS AEAD and RSA/PSS signature byte equality with constant-time comparison; adding checked arithmetic and malformed packet regressions to IPv4/TCP/UDP/DHCP parser boundaries; and adding VirtIO descriptor-chain plus used-ring wrap validation to the active C backend with Rust-side audit tests.
- Rationale: RELEASE_PLAN had mixed current and stale checklist rows. The riskiest remaining release blockers were attacker-controlled network/TLS/VirtIO inputs that could bypass state order, trigger unchecked length arithmetic, or let a malformed virtqueue chain escape local validation.
- Tradeoffs: The VirtIO Rust audit helper proves the descriptor invariants in host tests, while the C backend enforces the same chain and used-ring delta checks in the legacy path. The upstream `virtio_drivers` internals still own the hidden Rust virtqueue descriptor construction, so the Rust helper is an audit boundary rather than a replacement for that crate internals.
- Evidence: Targeted host tests passed for 16 new regressions: TLS downgrade/KEX/state, IP/TCP/UDP/DHCP malformed parsing, VirtIO chain/wrap audits, and signature constant-time helper. Follow-up validation must include full lib test, UEFI check, and touched-path gate before release status is promoted.
- Supersedes: The previous ambiguous RELEASE_PLAN state where TLS downgrade/KEX/state and VirtIO circular/wrap items were marked open despite adjacent hardening work.
- Next validation: Run full `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`, `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics`, and QEMU/Simics smoke for runtime confirmation.
## 2026-04-19 | curated-apps | direct-vendor-seed-pack
- Decision: Added the first permissive-license curated source pack under `third_party/curated/`: `antirez/kilo` at `323d93b29bd89a2cb446de90c4ed4fea1764176e` (BSD-2-Clause), `zserge/jsmn` at `25647e692c7906b96ffd2b05ca54c097948e879c` (MIT), `richgel999/miniz` at `4b9fcf1df525114484be49f3216169b061c07ac6` (MIT), `nothings/stb` at `31c1ad37456438565541f4919958214b6e762fb4` (public domain or MIT), `phoboslab/qoi` at `6fff9b70dd79b12f808b0acc5cb44fde9998725e` (MIT), and `cktan/tomlc17` at `32972cec29cf90cbc8b3fb74ab7be6d379657697` (MIT). Imported files are unmodified upstream source/license/readme files and are recorded in `third_party/curated/VENDOR_MANIFEST.toml`.
- Rationale: echOS needs ready programs/tooling without opening a broad new app-writing front. These components are small, permissive, and useful as direct seeds for `ech-edit`, `ech-view`, `ech-compress`, JSON manifest tooling, and TOML config tooling. Terminal tools remain a separate Toybox-first slice because the full utility tree requires a selected command set plus libc/syscall adapter boundaries.
- Tradeoffs: Vendoring source gives control and offline reproducibility but creates audit ownership. No kernel/hot-path dependency is created by this import; adapters and build glue must stay outside the imported upstream files unless a future manifest entry records a deliberate mutation.
- Evidence: Upstream license files imported beside each component; source commits resolved with `git ls-remote`; downloaded files came from pinned raw GitHub URLs; local scan found no GPL/LGPL/AGPL marker except an explanatory mention inside the upstream `stb` README.
- Supersedes: The earlier app-layer discussion that only listed possible candidates without durable source, license, commit, or mutation-scope records.
- Next validation: Add a no-build license/provenance checker for `third_party/curated/VENDOR_MANIFEST.toml`, then port exactly one first tool (`kilo` into `ech-edit`) behind an explicit terminal/file-IO adapter before importing Toybox.
## 2026-04-20 | curated-apps | sbase-ubase-ech-tools-source-pool
- Decision: Promoted `ech-tools` from a Toybox-first selected subset to a broader permissive command source pool built from `sbase` plus `ubase`, while retaining the selected Toybox 0BSD subset as a reference/fallback. Imported `sbase` at `c1341583c96307cb0e6152c963ed23c4d56a4278` under MIT and `ubase` at `e8249b49ca3e02032dece5e0cdac3d236667a6d9` under MIT/X Consortium into `third_party/curated/`, excluding only upstream `.git` metadata.
- Rationale: The userland command target needs a "cafcafli" command set without GPL/LGPL/AGPL baggage. `sbase` gives a compact core shell/file/text/checksum/archive set, `ubase` adds system utilities, and both have far less license and build-system friction than embedding a full BusyBox-like GPL tree.
- Tradeoffs: The source pool is broad but not yet executable inside echOS. Many commands depend on POSIX process, file, directory, permission, terminal, mount, module, and device semantics; those must be exposed through an echOS-owned adapter/dispatcher rather than mutating upstream files in place.
- Evidence: `git ls-remote` pinned `sbase` and `ubase` heads; local upstream clones confirmed the same commits; `rg -n "GPL|LGPL|AGPL"` over the imported `third_party/curated/sbase` and `third_party/curated/ubase` trees returned no hits; Makefile BIN parsing reported 100 `sbase` commands, 52 `ubase` commands, and 150 unique combined command candidates after `dd` and `mknod` overlap.
- Supersedes: The Toybox-only terminal-tool direction in the first curated app matrix.
- Next validation: Implement `src/userland/ech_tools` dispatcher/adapters for the Tier 0 command set, add host smoke tests per command, then register `ech-tools <command> ...` in the shell image.
## 2026-04-20 | userland | ech-tools-catalog-and-shell-bridge
- Decision: Added `src/userland/ech_tools.rs` as the echOS-owned command catalog and dispatcher contract for the 150-command `sbase` plus `ubase` source pool, then exposed it through the shell as `ech-tools`. The first bridge routes 15 commands that already have shell-backed behavior: `cat`, `cp`, `echo`, `grep`, `head`, `ls`, `mkdir`, `mv`, `pwd`, `rm`, `sort`, `tail`, `touch`, `uniq`, and `wc`.
- Rationale: The command pool needs a single truth source for command names, source tree, bring-up tier, and runtime status before individual POSIX adapters are expanded. Routing already-backed commands gives users a working `ech-tools` entry point without mutating upstream `sbase`, `ubase`, or Toybox sources.
- Tradeoffs: Seven Tier 0 commands remain adapter-pending: `basename`, `dirname`, `false`, `printf`, `tee`, `tr`, and `true`. `true` and `false` require explicit shell exit-status plumbing so control-flow operators observe status without inferring from text output.
- Evidence: Catalog tests assert 150 unique commands, sorted unique names, 22 Tier 0 commands, and 15 shell-bridge commands; shell tests assert `ech-tools` catalog rendering, `ech-tools echo merhaba`, and adapter-pending reporting for `ech-tools tr a b`.
- Next validation: Connect the remaining Tier 0 adapters, then add command corpus tests with stdout, stderr, and exit-status expectations before promoting direct shell aliases.
## 2026-04-23 | userland | ech-tools-final-150-command-bridge
- Decision: Promoted the remaining 23 `ech-tools` catalog entries to `shell-bridge`: `blkdiscard`, `chroot`, `cron`, `eject`, `freeramdisk`, `fsfreeze`, `getty`, `halt`, `insmod`, `mkswap`, `nice`, `nohup`, `passwd`, `pivot_root`, `renice`, `rmmod`, `setsid`, `swaplabel`, `swapoff`, `swapon`, `switch_root`, `tftp`, and `unshare`. Added shell-owned module, swap, frozen-mount, ejected-media, nice-value, root, and namespace state records, plus password-hash update support in the local user database.
- Rationale: Users should not learn echOS-specific command names when the Unix command vocabulary already covers the terminal surface. The bridge keeps the Linux-style names while mapping effects into existing echOS shell, user, driver, net, loopback, and init surfaces.
- Tradeoffs: Commands whose full behavior depends on boot-time hardware still use existing driver/model calls or shell-owned state records in host tests. This gives deterministic catalog closure without pretending host smoke can eject physical media, unload kernel text, or complete a remote TFTP transfer without a peer.
- Evidence: `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib shell::tests::final_system_namespace_commands_bridge_through_ech_tools -- --nocapture` passed for the final slice, including file discard, cron, fsfreeze, module load/remove, swap header/label/on/off, nice/nohup/renice, passwd/login/getty, chroot/pivot/switch root, setsid/unshare, local TFTP, eject, freeramdisk error path, and halt host arm. `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib ech_tools -- --nocapture` passed 16/16 catalog and shell bridge tests. `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -q` passed with existing repository warnings. `git diff --check -- ...` passed with the existing `docs/agent/decision-log.md` line-ending warning. `.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths ...` exited 0 with review warnings for pre-existing atomic/allocation/snapshot patterns.
- Next validation: Run boot-image shell smoke before promoting the command suite into release notes.
## 2026-04-23 | curated-apps | sqlite-lua-source-wave
- Decision: Added two new permissive-source imports under `third_party/curated/`: SQLite official amalgamation release `3.53.0` from `https://www.sqlite.org/2026/sqlite-amalgamation-3530000.zip` and Lua official source release `5.4.8` from `https://www.lua.org/ftp/lua-5.4.8.tar.gz`. `sqlite/` now carries `sqlite3.c`, `sqlite3.h`, `sqlite3ext.h`, `shell.c`, and the official SQLite copyright page. `lua/` now carries the upstream `src/` and `doc/` trees plus `README` and `Makefile`; the upstream `doc/readme.html` retains the MIT license text.
- Rationale: The next app-layer wave needed one structured-state engine and one embeddable scripting runtime that stay inside the permissive-license boundary and can be shimmed into echOS without inheriting a foreign process model. SQLite gives a canonical local metadata/state substrate; Lua gives a small scripting surface for shell/config/plugin work.
- Tradeoffs: This slice vendors source only. No claim is made yet about echOS VFS binding, allocator hooks, disabled Lua `io/os` libraries, or a hardened SQL runtime boundary. Those adapters must remain echOS-owned and outside the imported upstream files. SQLite is imported as the amalgamation rather than the full canonical source tree, which minimizes integration surface at the cost of upstream-internal granularity.
- Evidence: Official SQLite download metadata reported release `3.53.0`, relative archive `2026/sqlite-amalgamation-3530000.zip`, and SHA3-256 `c2325c53b3b41761469f91cfb078e96882ac5d85bac10c11b0bd8f253b031e5b`; the imported `sqlite3.h` carries SQLite's public-domain blessing. Official Lua FTP listed `lua-5.4.8.tar.gz`; upstream `README` states `Lua 5.4.8, released on 21 May 2025`; upstream `doc/readme.html` carries the MIT license text. `third_party/curated/VENDOR_MANIFEST.toml`, `third_party/curated/README.md`, and `docs/apps/direct-vendor-candidates-2026-04-19.md` were updated to pin the new imports.
- Next validation: Add an echOS-owned `ech-db` seam with SQLite allocator/VFS hooks, then add an `ech-lua` bring-up lane that prunes `io`/`os`, exposes an `echos` host module, and proves script execution under host tests before shell image wiring.
## 2026-04-23 | userland | ech-db-and-ech-lua-host-bringup
- Decision: Added `src/userland/ech_db.rs`, `src/userland/ech_lua.rs`, and a host-only `build.rs` C integration lane. `ech-db` now compiles the vendored SQLite amalgamation on non-UEFI host targets, installs a real SQLite allocator hook through `sqlite3_config(SQLITE_CONFIG_MALLOC, ...)`, exposes `open_memory`, `open_path`, `execute_batch`, and `query_rows`, and keeps kernel/UEFI targets fail-closed with an explicit host-only error. `ech-lua` now compiles the vendored Lua core on non-UEFI host targets, opens only `base`, `coroutine`, `math`, `string`, `table`, and `utf8`, and registers an `echos` host module with `exec`, `exists`, `readfile`, and `writefile`.
- Rationale: The source imports were inert until echOS owned the ABI boundary. SQLite needed a concrete allocator contract and a minimal query surface; Lua needed a narrow runtime surface that could drive shell/config work without inheriting upstream `io`/`os` process semantics.
- Tradeoffs: SQLite currently rides the default host VFS rather than a custom echOS VFS registration; the implemented seam closes allocator ownership and open/query policy, not filesystem virtualization. Lua currently uses host libc-backed upstream core files and a lightweight host module; `package`, `io`, `os`, debugger, and dynamic loading remain closed on purpose. Kernel/UEFI targets intentionally return explicit runtime-unavailable errors instead of pretending those host seams exist in-image already.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib -q`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib userland::ech_db::host::tests:: -- --nocapture`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib userland::ech_lua::host::tests:: -- --nocapture`. The DB tests prove in-memory SQL round-trip and file-backed reopen persistence. The Lua tests prove `io`/`os` are absent, string/math libs work, `echos.writefile/readfile/exists` round-trip through the host file boundary, and `echos.exec('echo merhaba')` resolves through the shell bridge.
- Next validation: Replace SQLite's default host VFS with an echOS-owned file adapter, then expose `ech-db` and `ech-lua` through a shell/image launch surface once the same host semantics are mirrored into the runtime layer.
## 2026-04-23 | userland | ech-db-host-vfs-seam
- Decision: Replaced the file-backed `ech-db` open lane with an echOS-owned SQLite VFS registration in `src/userland/ech_db.rs`. `open_path()` now calls `sqlite3_open_v2(..., "echos-host-vfs")`, and the registered VFS owns `xOpen`, `xDelete`, `xAccess`, `xFullPathname`, `xRandomness`, `xSleep`, `xCurrentTime`, `xGetLastError`, plus file methods for close, read, write, truncate, sync, size, lock, unlock, reserved-lock checks, and basic file-control queries.
- Rationale: The previous seam only owned allocator policy while file IO still escaped through SQLite's default host VFS. The new lane makes the filesystem boundary explicit and measurable inside echOS-owned Rust code before any runtime-layer VFS mirroring.
- Tradeoffs: This host VFS is intentionally scoped to rollback-journal style file traffic. It maintains a per-process lock table in host Rust for `SHARED/RESERVED/PENDING/EXCLUSIVE` state but does not implement shared-memory/WAL callbacks, dynamic loading, or kernel-target file virtualization. `open_memory()` remains SQLite's builtin in-memory path; only `open_path()` is forced through the custom VFS.
- Evidence: `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib userland::ech_db::host::tests:: -- --nocapture` now passes 3/3, including a proof test that counts custom VFS `xOpen` hits on file-backed opens. The existing reopen persistence test still passes through the new adapter.
- Next validation: Add an echOS runtime file adapter beneath the same VFS contract, then decide whether WAL support is needed or whether `ech-db` should stay delete-journal only in the first shell/image exposure.
